# Backlog

Authoritative task list for the **current milestone only**. Each row is one
commit. IDs are stable and never reused. `scripts/check-commit-msg.sh` requires
a commit subject to start with an ID present in this file.

⚠️ **M-1.1 was split into M-1.1 (documentation) and M-1.2 (machinery).**
review.md rule 12: as one 129-file commit it took five review rounds and ~2.5
hours, and every extra round found defects in the previous round's *fix*. Docs
and enforcement code want different lenses, so they are different commits.

⚠️ **The first commits land M0.4 before M-1.1.** Task IDs are stable
identifiers, not an ordering mandate ([git.md](../standards/git.md) rule 1 only
requires the subject to name a real task). M-1.2 carries the parser regression
suite (M0.18), those tests are Java, and Java needs the build that M0.4 brings —
so the build goes first.

**Current milestone: M8 — resilience: the system assembled, and then broken**, specified in [milestones/M8/SPEC.md](milestones/M8/SPEC.md) and decomposed into M8.0-M8.34 below (⚠️ **THE IDs ARE NOT IN NUMERIC ORDER**: M8.20-M8.25 were added by M8.0's own spec review, which found the production transport owed to M8 since M5 and missing, one of research 08 §9's nine chaos rows silently dropped, and criterion 16's re-deferral branch checkable by nobody). ⚠️ **ITS OPENING TASKS ARE ASSEMBLY AND THE FIRST REAL BACKEND, THROUGH M8.3, BY [ADR-0052](decisions/0052-m8-owns-assembly-and-the-first-real-backend-because-its-evidence-is-unbuyable-without-them.md)**, because a chaos matrix over a system nothing assembles kills processes that run none of the mechanisms under test, and `MemoryBinStore`'s RPO is 0 for survivors and infinite for the killed pod independent of any code this project wrote. An ordering decision, not new scope.

⚠️ **M7 (retention, GC and the consumer watermark) IS COMPLETE** — [milestones/M7/VERIFIED.md](milestones/M7/VERIFIED.md), one evidence line per acceptance criterion, FOUR of which carry an explicit `NOT-RUN` or `OBSERVED-NOT` clause -- criteria 6, 10, 14 and 18. ⚠️ **`check-milestone-verified.sh` PRINTS "6 explicitly NOT run" AND THAT NUMBER IS OCCURRENCES OF THE STRING, NOT CRITERIA**, the summary paragraph's two forward references included; quoting it as a count of criteria is the defect `VERIFIED.md` records for a different gate twelve lines below. It was specified in [milestones/M7/SPEC.md](milestones/M7/SPEC.md) and decomposed into M7.0-M7.35 below (M7.21-M7.35 are the milestone review's and this commit's review's harvest and are CARRIED, not required for completion) (⚠️ **THE HIGHEST ID IS NOT THE LAST TASK**: M7.16 is the refusal a consumer meets when its position has been collected and sits beside M7.10, while M7.17 and M7.18 are gaps the work itself opened -- the reporter's production source of positions, and the frame that carries the retained floor to the consumer). ⚠️ **THIS IS THE FIRST MILESTONE THAT CAN DESTROY COMMITTED DATA** -- nothing in the tree deleted anything before it (and nothing DEPLOYED does even now, because no production `main()` assembles a GC pass -- M7.25), and a retention bug is silent in the way M6's placement bug was silent: the delete succeeds, storage drops, and the loss surfaces as a 404 on a shard coming back from a restart. So every M7 row below carries a KEEP obligation as well as a DELETE one, and a rule that deletes nothing fails exactly as many criteria as one that deletes everything. ⚠️ **AND THE WATERMARK IT RELIES ON IS OPTIMISTIC BY CONSTRUCTION** (ADR-0005, ⚠️ **AMENDED BY ADR-0051 AT M7.14**): the ingestion-state API reports the IN-MEMORY pointer, and a plugin INSIDE the node can read the committed one out of the shard's last Lucene commit -- which does not remove the staleness, only moves the error to the safe side -- so a watermark may only EXTEND retention and the time floor -- never the watermark -- is the safety net. **M6 is complete** -- [milestones/M6/VERIFIED.md](milestones/M6/VERIFIED.md), with eleven rows carried forward (M6.14-M6.24) that stay below rather than being deleted. ⚠️ **M6.19 IS THE ONE THAT BITES M7**: the routed path is wired into no deployable server, and no production `main()` exists at all (M5.91), so every M7 criterion crossing the ingester<->plugin boundary does so over a test transport (M5.6e, owned by M8). ⚠️ **A multi-pod INGESTER deployment is STILL NOT CORRECT** for the same reason.

| M6.1 | **A DROPPED DELIVERY IS A GAP THE CONSUMER REPORTS, NOT A SILENT SKIP.** `ConsumerClient.deliver` offers into a bounded queue and drops on full -- correct, because blocking would stall the ingester's commit path for every stream on the node, and its javadoc says so. ⚠️ **WHAT IS MISSING IS THE OTHER HALF: NOTHING NOTICES.** The shard then reads offset 41 after offset 12 and indexes it, so the records between are missing with no exception on any path, no metric, and a healthy-looking shard -- the same silent-loss shape M5.45g's criterion 5 names one layer down. ⚠️ ACCEPTANCE: (1) with the queue forced full, the consumer SEES the gap and names the offsets it did not receive; (2) it does NOT hand the engine the later records as if contiguous; (3) the case reds against TODAY's behaviour, which is the whole point -- a green-only test here repeats the non-proof M1.16 was withdrawn for. ⚠️ RECOVERING from the gap is NOT this row's: the ladder is M5.18's policy and executing it is M8's ⚠️ **DONE.** `DeliveryGapException` carries the stream, the missing range, and **whether THIS consumer dropped them** -- the two cases need different answers from an operator, since a local drop is a capacity to raise and a gap with nothing dropped is upstream, where raising it changes nothing. `ConsumerClient` tracks the offset the next delivery must start at, which is the COMMIT LOG's contiguity rather than an invention: offsets are assigned per run at commit, so `n` records at `f` are followed by `f + n`. ⚠️ THREE THINGS THE CASES PIN THAT THE OBVIOUS IMPLEMENTATION GETS WRONG: the FIRST delivery carries any offset (a resumed session answers at the next record, so comparing against 0 reports a gap for every resume in the fleet); the gap is reported ONCE and the stream continues (refusing every later read turns one lost window into a dead shard); and the delivery that REVEALED the gap is not discarded with it -- measured, discarding it produced an inverted second gap, "skipped offsets [7, 5)". ⚠️ AND THE CASE THAT REDS AGAINST TODAY'S BEHAVIOUR NEEDED THE CONSUMER TO KEEP READING: with everything after the first delivery dropped, nothing later ever arrives to reveal the gap and the stream is merely idle. ⚠️ **AND IT IS COUNTED AND LOGGED, NEVER THROWN** -- round-1 review found the corpus paragraph that rules a throw out: "Any exception thrown from `readNext` pauses ingestion for that shard ... and requires operator intervention to resume ... only throw for genuinely unrecoverable states" (research doc 02 §6). A gap is the OPPOSITE of unrecoverable -- the records still exist upstream -- so the first draft, which threw, turned a queue that overflowed for a second into a shard an operator must restart by hand, and `BinStoreShardConsumer.drain` would have discarded the records already polled into its list, losing MORE than the gap did. `gapsDetected()` and `lastGap()` are the surface instead, on the client and on the shard. ⚠️ AND A DROP IS ATTRIBUTED TO THE FIRST GAP AFTER IT, not compared against zero: a lifetime comparison labels every later gap 'local' once anything has ever been dropped, which is the misdiagnosis the type exists to prevent. The baseline moves only when a gap consumes it, because a drop can be counted while an EARLIER delivery is still queued. ⚠️ AND THE REPEAT CASE REPEATS A STRICTLY EARLIER, SHORTER WINDOW, which round-2 review measured as the difference between pinning the rewind and only claiming to: repeating the LAST window makes `max(end, expected)` and `end` the same number, so the mutation survived and the case asserted nothing it named. ⚠️ ELEVEN CASES, EACH RED AGAINST THE DEFECT IT NAMES -- including two at the PLUGIN layer, which round 1 named as the gap the client-side cases could not see. `./gradlew build` green at 1378 tests, `:plugin:clusterTest` green at 9 | FR-10 | done |
| M6.2 | **`IndexRegistration` IN `format`, ITS ADR, AND ITS GOLDEN FILE.** The plugin must tell the ingester `indexUUID`, `indexName`, `aliases[]`, `numShards`, `routingNumShards`, `routingFactor` and `routingPartitionSize` -- everything placement needs and nothing else. ⚠️ **A WIRE-FORMAT CHANGE**, so it carries the `wire-format-change` obligations IN ONE COMMIT: the format, every reader, every writer, the fakes, the golden file and the ADR. ⚠️ AND THE DECODER IS WRITTEN AND PROVED IN THIS COMMIT, which is what "the read side ships first" means for a format whose reader and writer land together: an old peer meeting a new frame must REFUSE it rather than misread it, so the refusal cases are part of the same commit as the encoder rather than a follow-up. ⚠️ **DONE.** `IndexRegistration` in `format` with magic `BIRG`, version 1, and **ADR-0047**. ⚠️ TWO GOLDEN FILES, not one: an unsplit index has `routingNumShards == numShards` and `routingFactor == 1`, so a single fixture cannot see those two fields swapped in the encoder. ⚠️ AND THE FACTOR IS CROSS-CHECKED AGAINST THE SHARD COUNTS, because OpenSearch DERIVES it -- a frame carrying a different one describes an index that cannot exist and would misplace every routed record for it, silently. `routingNumShards` must also be a multiple of `numShards`, which is what makes a split a renumbering rather than a rehash. ⚠️ A SEMANTICALLY INVALID FRAME IS AN `IOException`, NOT AN `IllegalArgumentException`: it arrived over a wire, and an unchecked throw out of a decode is the shape that kills a poll loop. ⚠️ THE UUID TRAVELS AS A STRING, because an OpenSearch index UUID is BASE64URL and `UUID.fromString` throws on every real one -- which `BinStoreConsumerFactory.indexUuidOf` records finding on a booting node, since every in-process test built a `java.util.UUID` directly. ⚠️ ROUND-1 REVIEW FOUND THE NUMERIC AND STRING BOUNDS UNPINNED: deleting `toInt`'s range check leaves the suite green while a `numShards` uvarint of `0x1_0000_0004` NARROWS TO 4 -- a structurally valid, semantically wrong registration accepted without a word, which is the silent misplacement this format exists to refuse, arriving through arithmetic rather than through a torn frame. It also found trailing bytes ACCEPTED, `routingPartitionSize` unbounded against the shard count, the alias list not proved copied, and a comment asserting a power-of-two invariant OpenSearch does not enforce (it enforces multiple-of; power-of-two is how the DEFAULT is computed, and the stronger claim would refuse a legitimate hand-configured index). ⚠️ AND TWO BOUNDS CASES HAD TO BE PINNED BY THEIR MESSAGE, because removing either bound still throws -- from `Cursor` four fields later -- so a case asserting only `IOException` measures the cursor rather than this decoder. ⚠️ TWENTY-ONE CASES, EACH RED AGAINST THE DEFECT IT NAMES. `./gradlew build` green at 1399 tests | FR-16 | todo |
| M6.3 | **`IndexCatalog` IN `ingest`: WHAT IS REGISTERED, AND WHAT AN ALIAS MEANS.** Holds the registrations, resolves an alias to the CURRENT concrete index, and answers "what is this index's shape". ⚠️ **STREAM IDENTITY IS `(indexUUID, partition)` -- PER CONCRETE INDEX, NEVER PER ALIAS** (ADR-0015 §4), so the alias is resolved at write time and never stored in a stream id. ⚠️ **AND `routingPartitionSize > 1` REFUSES THE ROUTING MODE, NOT THE INDEX**: with built-in tenant partitioning the shard depends on both the routing value AND the document `_id` (`OperationRouting:587`), which the ingester does not have, so the placement cannot be computed and the failure would otherwise be a silent miss per record. The refusal names the setting. ⚠️ **AND THE REFUSAL IS SCOPED TO ROUTING-BASED PLACEMENT**, as SPI §4b scopes it: an index with `routingPartitionSize > 1` may still be written with an EXPLICIT partition, which needs no hash at all -- refusing its registration outright would take a working mode away. What is refused is placing a record in it BY ROUTING VALUE, and the refusal names the setting. ⚠️ **DONE.** `IndexCatalog` in `ingest`: `register` replaces, `resolve` answers by concrete name or alias, `resolveForRouting` refuses the MODE. ⚠️ **AN ALIAS THE NEW REGISTRATION DROPPED IS DROPPED -- CONDITIONALLY.** An index that stops claiming `logs` must stop answering for it, or a rollover leaves BOTH indices claiming it and the winner is whichever push arrived last; but the removal is conditional on the alias still pointing THERE, because a rollover registers the new index first and the old index's own later push would otherwise take the alias back, leaving it unresolvable and every write to it pending until it timed out. ⚠️ A CONCRETE NAME WINS OVER AN ALIAS OF THE SAME SPELLING: OpenSearch forbids an alias named after an existing index, so the two collide only across a delete-and-recreate, and a producer writing to a name that IS an index must never be sent elsewhere. ⚠️ AND UNKNOWN IS NOT UNROUTABLE: the first waits for a registration, the second is a 400, and collapsing them turns every plugin reconnect into a refusal storm. ⚠️ ROUND-1 REVIEW FOUND A READER SEEING A HALF-APPLIED REGISTRATION ON EVERY ORDINARY RE-PUSH: `register` removed every alias the previous registration listed and put back the ones the new one claims, so a concurrent `resolve` answered EMPTY for an alias NOTHING had changed about -- and by this row's own words that sends the producer's write to the pending pool until it times out. An alias the new registration still claims is now left alone, and the case reads from another thread across 20,000 re-pushes, because a single-threaded case cannot see a window that opens and closes inside one call. ⚠️ AND THE REFUSAL BOUNDARY IS `> 1`, NOT `> 2`: every refusal case used 4 and every accepting case used 1, so moving the boundary left all nine green while `routing_partition_size = 2` -- legal from three shards up -- was routed by murmur3 alone. ⚠️ A DELETED INDEX PRODUCES NO PUSH AT ALL and its entry answers forever; that is M6.7's, named in the class rather than left for a reader to assume covered. ⚠️ ELEVEN CASES, EACH RED AGAINST THE DEFECT IT NAMES. `./gradlew build` green at 1410 tests | FR-16 | todo |
| M6.4 | **`RoutingPartitioner`, PINNED AGAINST `OperationRouting`.** `floorMod(murmur3_32(routing), routingNumShards) / routingFactor`. ⚠️ **PINNED AGAINST OPENSEARCH'S OWN CLASS, NOT AGAINST A RE-DERIVATION** -- ADR-0015 says so in as many words, and a test that recomputed the expected value from our own formula would pass while wrong. The fixture lives in `plugin`'s test tier, the one module whose tests see both `binjava.ingest` and `org.opensearch.cluster.routing`. ⚠️ ACCEPTANCE: >=1,000 routing values across shard counts {1, 3, 5, 16} AND a SPLIT index where `routingNumShards` != `numShards`, every one equal to `calculateScaledShardId`'s answer. ⚠️ THE MUTATIONS IT MUST DIE UNDER: `floorMod` -> `%` (a negative hash lands on shard 0 -- roughly half of all routing values), dropping `/ routingFactor` (every split index misplaced), and a murmur3 seeded 1 rather than 0 ⚠️ **DONE, AND THE PARITY CASE EARNED ITSELF ON ITS FIRST RUN.** The formula was right, the constants were right, the arithmetic around them was right -- and the hash was over the **UTF-8 bytes**, where `Murmur3HashFunction.hash(String)` hashes the **UTF-16 chars as two bytes each, low byte first**. Every routing value, including pure ASCII, hashed to a different number; every record would have landed on a shard no routed query looks at, with no exception anywhere. NOTHING ELSE IN THE TREE WOULD HAVE FOUND IT, which is exactly why ADR-0015 requires the expectation to come from `OperationRouting` rather than from a re-derivation. ⚠️ THE SPLIT FIXTURE ALSO LIED AT FIRST: `INDEX_NUMBER_OF_ROUTING_SHARDS_SETTING` in the settings map left `getRoutingNumShards()` at `numShards`, so the case named split-8-from-32 was testing an UNSPLIT index -- it is on the builder now. ⚠️ AND TWO CASES WERE DELETED RATHER THAN FAKED: a non-ASCII case in `ingest` could assert only range and determinism, which the byte-swap mutation survives, and a negative-hash premise case could not be red by any mutation of our code since it measures OpenSearch's hash -- it is folded into the parity case as a premise instead. ⚠️ ROUND-1 REVIEW READ `generateShardId` RATHER THAN THE COMMENT ABOVE IT and found TWO index shapes the simple form does not place -- virtual shards and an in-progress split -- which this class cannot detect because the registration carries no field for them; **M6.14** owns it and the javadoc says so where an implementer reads it. ⚠️ AND THE PARITY FIXTURE COULD NOT REACH A 1- OR 3-BYTE TAIL: a String makes `length * 2` bytes, always even, so `data.length & 3` was only ever 0 or 2 and replacing the three-byte tail branch with a constant left every case green. There is now a case over ARBITRARY bytes at every length 0..37, against Lucene's own `StringHelper`. ⚠️ TEN CASES. `./gradlew build` green at 1420 tests | FR-19 | todo |
| M6.14 | **ROUTED PLACEMENT IS ONLY CORRECT FOR THE INDICES ADR-0006 NAMES, AND TWO SHAPES CAN STILL REACH IT** (found by M6.4's round-1 review, which read `OperationRouting.generateShardId` rather than the comment above it). For an index with `index.number_of_virtual_shards` set, OpenSearch resolves through a VIRTUAL-SHARD MAPPING; for one MID-SPLIT it goes through `getSplitShardsMetadata().getShardIdOfHash`. Neither is `floorMod(hash, routingNumShards) / routingFactor`, and `RoutingPartitioner` cannot tell: `IndexRegistration` carries no field that says so, so a routed write to such an index is placed by the simple form and every routed query for it returns NOTHING -- no exception, no metric, no gate. ⚠️ ADR-0006 ALREADY STATES THE OBLIGATION -- "refuse the mode where the simple form does not hold" -- and M6.4 discharged the `routing_partition_size` half of it only. ⚠️ THE FIX IS A FIELD AND THEREFORE A WIRE-FORMAT CHANGE (version 2 of `IndexRegistration`, with its golden file and its ADR paragraph), plus the refusal in `IndexCatalog.resolveForRouting` beside the one that is already there. ⚠️ UNTIL IT LANDS, the safe deployment is not to register such an index at all, which makes its writes PEND and then be refused rather than silently misplaced -- stated in `RoutingPartitioner`'s javadoc where an implementer reads it | FR-19 | todo |
| M6.5 | **THE PENDING POOL: BUFFER AND REROUTE, BOUNDED, WITH A TIMEOUT** (ADR-0015 §3). A record for an index whose shape the ingester does not yet know is accepted into a bounded FIFO pool and placed when registration arrives. ⚠️ **IT WORKS ONLY BECAUSE ADR-0001 ASSIGNS ORDERING AT COMMIT**: a buffered record has no offset yet, so it can still be moved between partitions -- no keying by routing value, nothing deferred to read time. ⚠️ **AND THE TIMEOUT REJECTS RATHER THAN FOLDS** (ADR-0006's "reject, never fold"): folding to partition 0 would funnel an unregistered index into one shard while returning 202. ⚠️ **IT COVERS THE UNKNOWN-SHAPE CASE ONLY, AND NOT THE ROLLOVER WINDOW** -- an early draft of M6's criterion 6 had it absorbing both, which contradicts ADR-0015 §4: mid-rollover the alias still resolves to the PREVIOUS concrete index, whose shape IS known, so those records land there and §4 calls that normal for time-series rollover. ⚠️ **DONE.** `PendingPool` in `ingest`: FIFO per index, bounded in BYTES per index, expiry per RECORD rather than per index, and the clock injected. ⚠️ THE BOUND CASE HOLDS RECORDS OF SIZES THAT VARY 30x AND ASSERTS A SECOND INDEX SURVIVES THE FLOOD, because a record-COUNT bound is not unbounded, refuses the flood and names the index while still holding N times the largest record -- and a GLOBAL bound passes every single-index case while letting one unknown index refuse every other index's writes during an ordinary plugin reconnect. ⚠️ EXPIRY IS PER RECORD: the case ages one record five seconds and another one second and asserts only the first is refused, because refusing both punishes a write whose registration may still be milliseconds away. ⚠️ AND `expire` SWEEPS EVERY INDEX, not the one a caller asks about -- an index nobody writes to again would otherwise hold its records forever, which is the leak the bound exists to prevent arriving by another road. ⚠️ REFUSED, NEVER EVICTED: dropping the oldest waiting record to make room loses a write the producer was told nothing about. ⚠️ ROUND-1 REVIEW MEASURED RECORDS BEING LOST OUTRIGHT: `offer` resolved its entry with `computeIfAbsent` and then took the entry's own monitor, so a concurrent `take` could unmap it in between and the record landed in an object nothing could reach -- never taken, never expired, and the producer holding a 202. ONE offer thread of 200,000 against ONE consumer lost 3,510. Every mutation now happens INSIDE a `compute` on the map, which is what makes membership and content atomic together; there is no second lock and there must not be one. The case is a CONSERVATION LAW -- everything accepted is taken or still waiting -- because a count assertion cannot say which interleaving lost what. ⚠️ AND THE BYTE COUNT IS ASSERTED EXACTLY, not as an upper bound: deleting the routing term from the estimate, and deleting the credit in `expire`, both left the suite green, because every case either asserted `<= 4096` or read the count after the entry was already removed, where it reads 0 either way. ⚠️ TWELVE CASES, EACH RED AGAINST THE DEFECT IT NAMES. `./gradlew build` green at 1432 tests | FR-13 | todo |

registered count is refused, which reds against today's 202. ⚠️ AN UNREGISTERED INDEX WAITS on a condition the registration signals, bounded by the pool's timeout, and the refusal at the end names -- round 2 MEASURED FR-13's refusal arriving as a 500, which a producer retries forever. ⚠️ AND THE PENDING UNIT IS A BATCH -- ONE REQUEST, ONE ROUTING VALUE -- which round 1 MEASURED the absence of: | M6.7 | **THE PLUGIN PUSHES REGISTRATION ON CONNECT AND ON CLUSTER-STATE CHANGE** (ADR-0015 §2). A `ClusterStateListener` inside the node, pushing over the subscription the node already holds -- no new endpoint, no new credential, no unauthenticated surface. ⚠️ **NODE-SCOPED, NOT SHARD-SCOPED**, for the reason `NodeSubscriptions` exists: `createShardConsumer` runs once per shard, so a listener built there would push once per shard per change. ⚠️ **AND IT PUSHES ON CHANGE, NOT ON A TIMER**: a poll would put a message rate on an idle cluster, which is NFR-2's shape one layer up even though no object-store request is involved. ⚠️ **AND M6's CRITERION 12 IS ITS OWN**, added after the spec review found FR-16 served by three criteria none of which OBSERVED the push: a listener built in `createShardConsumer` fires once per SHARD per change and every other criterion here stays green. ⚠️ ACCEPTANCE: one push on connect carrying every index this node hosts; one push per RELEVANT change (shard count, alias set, index added or removed) and NONE for an irrelevant one; and the push count flat in the number of SHARDS the node hosts ⚠️ **DONE.** `IndexRegistrar` is a `ClusterStateListener` holding no clock, no socket and no thread: it derives each hosted index's shape from the state, DIFFS it against what the ingester last ACCEPTED, and pushes only what changed. ⚠️ THE K-PROPERTY IS BOUGHT BY THE KEY: the loop walks this node's SHARDS and collects by the index's UUID, so eight shards are one message -- counted at K = 1 and K = 8 in one case, because at K = 1 a per-node push and a per-shard one are indistinguishable. ⚠️ A FAILED PUSH IS RETRIED THREE TIMES IMMEDIATELY AND THEN CARRIED, and the carry is the SAME fact as the diff rather than a second copy of it: a registration is recorded as accepted only after the push succeeds, so the next cluster-state change finds it missing and re-sends it. ⚠️ AND IT IS HANDED NO NODE ID -- `clusterService.localNode()` is not answerable from `createComponents`, which runs before the node has joined anything, and every event carries the id anyway. ⚠️ THE PUSH TRAVELS ON `SubscriptionTransport.register`, whose default REFUSES rather than accepting silently: a no-op default would make a transport that cannot carry registrations look exactly like one that can, and no production transport exists yet (M5.6e, M8). ⚠️ THE PUSHES RUN ON THE NODE'S GENERIC POOL, NEVER ON THE CLUSTER APPLIER THREAD that delivers the event -- round 1 measured that forty indices due against an unreachable ingester is 120 serial attempts with allocation, mappings and the ack to the cluster manager stopped behind them. ⚠️ AND `onReconnect()` IS THE 'ON CONNECT' HALF, which the change half cannot serve: the ingester's catalog is in memory and dies with it, so after it restarts this node's diff finds nothing due and a steady cluster sends nothing for hours while every routed write on it is refused at `pendingTimeout`. Its call site is the transport's reconnect (M6.15). ⚠️ TWO LOCKS, FOR TWO REASONS: the monitor guards the MEMORY of what the ingester accepted, and a separate lock serialises the pushes -- round 2 measured a parked `register` holding the monitor and a second `clusterChanged` failing to return in two seconds, which blocks the applier on the monitor instead of on the socket and stops every cluster-state update on the node just the same. ⚠️ AND THE PUSH TASK RE-DERIVES FROM THE NEWEST STATE rather than from the event that submitted it, because `threadPool.generic()` is multi-threaded and orders nothing: two rollover events pushed out of order leave the ingester's catalog holding the shape that arrived LAST, which is the stale one. ⚠️ AND THE PUSHES ARE COALESCED: one task at a time, with the running one looking again before it finishes. Round 3 measured what the uncoalesced version costs on the fault path -- nothing is recorded as accepted until a push succeeds, so while the ingester is unreachable EVERY index is due on EVERY cluster-state publication, and each publication queued another task on `generic()`, an unbounded pool shared with peer recovery and snapshots, each repeating every attempt for every index. ⚠️ TWENTY-FOUR CASES ACROSS TWO FILES -- `RegistrationPushTest` counts messages against a deterministic executor and `RegistrationWiringTest` owns the WIRING and the two THREADS, split at the 700-line cap. `./gradlew build` green at 1483 tests | FR-16 | done |

| M6.10 | **ALIAS ROLLOVER: NEW RECORDS MOVE, OLD ONES DO NOT** (ADR-0015 §4). After a rollover, records written to the alias land in the NEW concrete index's streams; records already committed keep their offsets in the PREVIOUS index's streams, and nothing is repartitioned because nothing needs to be. ⚠️ **THE STALENESS WINDOW IS ADR-0015 §4's, AND IT IS NOT THE PENDING POOL'S**: between the rollover and the plugin's push the alias still resolves to the PREVIOUS concrete index, whose shape is KNOWN, so records land there -- §4 calls that normal for time-series rollover and requires it be documented rather than discovered. An earlier draft of this row said records WAIT, which contradicts the ADR and is not what the code does. What is asserted is the documented behaviour: before the push, the previous index; after it, the new one; and nothing repartitioned either way. ⚠️ AND WHAT IS NOT SUPPORTED IS STATED: changing the shard count of an ingesting index, which resize cannot do anyway since it needs a read-only source ⚠️ **DONE.** `AliasRolloverIT` on a real node, with the WINDOW made deterministic by HOLDING the plugin's push in the fixture's transport rather than racing it -- the only way to assert "before the push" without a sleep. ⚠️ TWO OF THE THREE HALVES ARE FALSIFIABLE BY A MEASURED MUTATION: applying the held registration immediately makes the WINDOW assertion red, and making the alias never move (`putIfAbsent` plus skipping the conditional release) makes the AFTER-PUSH assertion red. ⚠️ The third -- the old index's records staying put -- is an ORDERING property of the case rather than a red: it is asserted after the new index has been written to, and nothing in this tree repartitions, so there is no mutation to kill. Said plainly rather than counted as a third red. ⚠️ AND THE ROLLOVER HAS TO CARRY THE INGESTION SETTINGS, which this task MEASURED rather than assumed: OpenSearch builds the rolled-over index from the matching TEMPLATE, not from the source index's settings, so a rollover naming neither produces an ordinary index that ingests nothing -- green, empty, and never registered, because the node had no BINSTORE index to report. A real deployment puts them in an index template. ⚠️ ONE T4 CASE, OBSERVED RED BY HAND THREE WAYS (`check-tdd` cannot see a `public void testX()` under RandomizedRunner). `./gradlew build` green at 1496 tests; `:plugin:clusterTest` green | FR-19 | done |

And `?routing=%20` passes the `isEmpty()` guard, so every mis-templated producer in a fleet funnels to ONE shard: a hot-shard incident presenting as a capacity problem. ⚠️ **THE SILENT MISS ARRIVING THROUGH THE ADAPTER** rather than through the hash, which is what makes it worth a row of its own. ⚠️ ACCEPTANCE: a repeated `routing` (or `partition`) is 400 naming the parameter; a blank-after-trim routing value is 400; both cases red against today's behaviour | FR-13 | todo |
| M6.24 | **`PendingPool.expire()` HAS NO PRODUCTION CALLER, AND TWO MUTATIONS SURVIVE BEHIND IT.** Every routed write settles its own batch in a `finally`, so the pool does not leak -- but nothing bounds a wait whose caller died, and M6.6's review recorded two surviving mutations (`arrived.isEmpty() OR mine.isEmpty()`, and the `settled OR` disjunct in `offer`) that are unreachable for exactly that reason. ⚠️ **ONE ROW, NOT TWO**: they share a cause. ⚠️ ACCEPTANCE: a production sweeper calls `expire()` on a schedule the ingester owns, and both disjuncts become reachable and red under mutation | FR-13 | todo |
| M6.12 | M6's `VERIFIED.md` and the milestone review. One evidence line per acceptance criterion, each naming the test or command that demonstrated it, `scripts/check-milestone-verified.sh` green, and anything not observed marked `NOT-RUN` or `OBSERVED-NOT` rather than claimed. ⚠️ **AND THE HARVEST IS MECHANICAL**: every M6 commit body deferring a minor gets a row, which M5.102 records an agent missing twice in a row when it was left to memory — ⚠️ **DONE.** `VERIFIED.md` with one evidence line per criterion, `check-milestone-verified` green at 13 criteria; the milestone review run over all twelve commits; ten rows carried forward (M6.14-M6.24), SIX of them raised by the review itself (M6.19-M6.24) -- and the count is stated once per document rather than twice per commit, because round 2 found this row and `VERIFIED.md` disagreeing about it; the roadmap row marked complete; and three corpus corrections landed -- the placement formula (wrong twice: no `/ routingFactor`, and murmur3 over UTF-16 chars rather than UTF-8 bytes), `generateShardId`'s two uncovered branches, and `DefaultStreamPoller` swallowing a `createShardConsumer` refusal forever. ⚠️ THE REVIEW FOUND WHAT NO PER-COMMIT REVIEW COULD: `RoutedIngest` is constructed only by tests, so criterion 5 holds at the class and not in a deployment (M6.19) | FR-7 | done |
| M7.0 | This spec, its backlog rows, and the spec review. ⚠️ **DONE.** SPEC.md with EIGHTEEN criteria and seventeen tasks, five rounds of review, four of which found a real defect -- the orphan sweep, the GC lease and observability with no criterion at all; "superseded deltas" undefined in a way that deletes the only object locating a retained segment; a copy's backwards report unhandled; and the normative rule unparenthesised so the `maxRetention` ceiling never fires alone. Signed past the round cap in `review/overrides.md`. ⚠️ **THE SPEC REVIEW IS THE CHEAPEST GATE AVAILABLE AND THE ONLY ONE THAT CATCHES A WRONG SPEC**: correct code solving the wrong problem passes every downstream gate, and for a milestone whose subject is DELETION that means correct code deleting the wrong bytes. What it is reviewed against is the mission, the architecture, ADR-0005, ADR-0033, ADR-0036, cost.md rules R2/R8/R15 and research 06 and 09 -- does M7 serve the requirements it cites, does it contradict a recorded decision, are its EIGHTEEN criteria checkable by something other than an opinion, and does every criterion that deletes have a sibling that keeps | FR-9 | done |
| M7.1 | **`ConsumerProgress` IN `format`, ITS ADR, AND ITS GOLDEN FILE.** ⚠️ **DONE.** `ConsumerProgress` (magic `BPCG`, v1) with 24 cases across two files, two golden files (a three-entry batch across two indices and a single entry at zero), ADR-0049, and the corpus §5 sketch pointed at it. The plugin must tell the ingester, per partition it hosts, `(indexUUID, partition, shardCopy, consumedUpTo)` -- BATCHED across every partition on the node, because the node is the reporting unit and a frame per shard is 8x at eight partitions. ⚠️ **A WIRE-FORMAT CHANGE**, so it carries the `wire-format-change` obligations: the format, every reader, every writer, the fakes, the golden file and the ADR in ONE commit. ⚠️ **`shardCopy` IS THE ALLOCATION ID AND IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT DECORATION** -- research 09 §6.2: with `all_active = true` every copy consumes the partition independently, so the feed is keyed by copy and the answer is a `min()` over copies. A frame keyed by partition alone cannot express a lagging replica and silently deletes its data. ⚠️ Same channel, same shape and same reasoning as ADR-0047's `IndexRegistration`: metadata-only, same-AZ, zero object-store requests | FR-9 | done |
| M7.2 | **`WatermarkTable` IN `ingest`: `min()` ACROSS COPIES, SILENCE FROZEN, RELOCATION SAFE.** ⚠️ **THREE TRAPS, AND EACH ONE SILENTLY LOSES DATA** (research 09 §6.2-6.4). (1) `min()` is over ALL KNOWN copies, never over the reporting ones: a copy at 90 that stops reporting while its peers advance to 500 must still answer 90, and a `min()` over reporters answers 500 and passes every other case in the file. (2) A copy silent past `reportTimeout` makes the whole table UNFRESH rather than being dropped -- GC then keeps everything on the watermark clause, which is the correct failure direction. (3) A new allocation id appearing for a partition does NOT retire the old one; both legitimately exist mid-relocation, and retirement is by explicit deregistration or an expiry GREATER than `minRetention` only -- and the constructor REFUSES a `copyExpiry` that is not, because that configuration quietly turns the outage budget off. ⚠️ **DONE.** `WatermarkTable` with 24 cases, and `RunKey.ofIndexUuid` with 6: the frame carries OpenSearch's BASE64URL uuid and the stream is keyed by the 16 bytes it decodes to, so the plugin's private `indexUuidOf` now DELEGATES to it -- two implementations of that mapping do not throw when they disagree, they make two streams for one index -- and BOTH retirement paths must be asserted to FIRE, since a criterion that only asserts nothing is retired is satisfied by an expiry that never runs. ⚠️ **(4) A COPY'S REPORTED OFFSET GOES BACKWARDS ON EVERY RESTART AND THAT REPORT IS TRUE** (found by M7.0's spec review): the feed is the IN-MEMORY pointer (ADR-0005), so a shard resuming from its last Lucene commit reports a LOWER offset than before the crash. A per-copy `max()` -- which is what the word watermark suggests -- pins the value at the pre-crash position and GC deletes exactly the records that shard is about to re-read. The latest report wins per copy; monotonicity belongs to the RULE, never to the FEED | FR-9 | done |
| M7.3 | **THE PLUGIN REPORTS PROGRESS: ONE FRAME PER NODE PER INTERVAL, AT ZERO STORE COST.** A reporter inside the node, pushing over the subscription it already holds -- no new endpoint, no new credential, no unauthenticated surface, the shape M6.7's `IndexRegistrar` established. ⚠️ **NODE-SCOPED AND INTERVAL-DRIVEN THROUGH A SEAM, NOT A TIMER**: a timer is untestable and a frame per shard multiplies the channel by the shard count. ⚠️ **AND WHAT IT REPORTS IS THE IN-MEMORY POINTER**, because OpenSearch exposes no other (ADR-0005); the optimism is paid for by `safetyMargin` in M7.5, not hidden here. ⚠️ **AND WHAT THE FRAME CARRIES IS ASSERTED, NOT ONLY HOW MANY AND WHAT THEY COST** (found by M7.0's spec review): a reporter sending the stream's TAIL offset, or a constant, produces one frame of eight entries at zero requests -- green on every count-and-cost assertion -- and drives `min()` to the live edge forever, so GC deletes records no shard has indexed. Eight shards get eight DIFFERENT positions and at least one is behind the tail. ⚠️ **DONE.** `ProgressReporter` with 10 cases and `SubscriptionTransport.report`, whose default REFUSES for the reason `register`'s does. ⚠️ **AND IT IS NOT WIRED INTO A NODE, WHICH IS M7.17**: what the plugin can observe is OpenSearch's in-memory pointer, and `readNext(pointer, ...)` carries the engine's pointer only on a forced or reset pointer (research 20/02 §2), so the steady-state source has to be the node's own ingestion state and `createComponents` is handed no `IndicesService` | FR-9 | done |
| M7.17 | **THE REPORTER HAS NO PRODUCTION SOURCE OF POSITIONS, AND THE OBVIOUS ONE IS WRONG** (opened by M7.3). `ProgressReporter.Positions` is a seam with no implementation on a real node, so nothing reports and every watermark stays UNKNOWN -- which keeps data rather than deleting it, and is therefore latent rather than dangerous, exactly as M6.15's unwired `onReconnect()` is. ⚠️ **THE TEMPTING SOURCE IS THE DEFECT M7.3's OWN CRITERION NAMES**: our consumer knows what it DELIVERED, and delivered-to-the-node is ahead of committed-by-the-shard by exactly the window retention exists to protect. ⚠️ **AND THE SECOND-MOST TEMPTING ONE IS STALE**: `readNext(pointer, includeStart, ...)` carries the engine's own pointer, but `DefaultStreamPoller` calls that overload only on a forced or reset pointer (research 20/02 §2), so a reporter built on it would freeze at the last reset and pin retention to `maxRetention`. What is left is the node's ingestion state, which `createComponents` is handed no handle for -- so this row owns finding the handle as much as using it. ⚠️ **AND THE SOURCE IS NOW SPECIFIED RATHER THAN OPEN** (M7.14, ADR-0051): the shard's LAST LUCENE COMMIT carries `batch_start`, readable through `IndexShard.acquireLastIndexCommit` from inside the node, and it is the COMMITTED pointer -- strictly better than the in-memory one for the same cost, and it shrinks `safetyMargin` from covering a translog flush window to covering the report interval ⚠️ **OWNED BY M8.43 SINCE M8.6 WAS SPLIT** | FR-9 | todo |
| M7.4 | **`Checkpoint` v2: `consumerWatermark` PER STREAM, ITS ADR AND ITS GOLDENS.** Research 09 §8 puts the watermark in the object that already carries `nextOffset` and `oldestRetainedOffset` -- no new object, no new CAS, no new request (R6 untouched). ⚠️ **A SECOND WIRE-FORMAT CHANGE IN THIS MILESTONE**, and `golden/checkpoint-v0.bin` and `checkpoint-v1.bin` must still decode to what they decode to today: a v0 object already in a bucket keeps decoding for the whole retention window. ⚠️ **THE FIXTURE MUST GIVE `consumerWatermark` AND `oldestRetainedOffset` DIFFERENT VALUES**, or writing one where the other belongs is green. ⚠️ **DONE.** `Checkpoint` v2 as an `OptionalLong` behind a PER-STREAM flag -- absent is NOT zero, because nobody-has-reported and nobody-has-read are different facts -- with 15 cases, `golden/checkpoint-v2.bin`, ADR-0050, and the v0 and v1 goldens asserted to come back with NO watermark and their pod pointers intact. ⚠️ **AND NOTHING SUPPLIES A WATERMARK YET**: the table is in `ingest` and `CheckpointWriter` is in `sequencer`, and the seam joining them does not exist -- carried for the reason `oldestRetainedOffset` was carried from M4.8a | FR-9 | done |
| M7.5 | **`RetentionRule`: THE PREDICATE OF RESEARCH 09 §7, BOTH DIRECTIONS AND THE BOUNDARY.** `delete iff age > minRetention AND every known copy reported within `reportTimeout` AND min(watermark) > endOffset + safetyMargin, OR age > maxRetention`. ⚠️ **THE WATERMARK IS A BRAKE, NEVER AN ACCELERATOR**: a fully-consumed twenty-minute-old segment is KEPT, because the outage budget IS `minRetention` (NFR-13) and a consumer restarting into a freshly-emptied bucket has lost data it never committed. A rule that deletes on the watermark alone is faster, cheaper and wrong, and only the keep-case reds it. ⚠️ **AND THE CEILING ALARMS RATHER THAN DELETING QUIETLY** -- crossing `maxRetention` means a consumer WILL lose data (a paused shard pins retention forever, §6.5), so the assertion is that the alarm FIRED, not that the object went. ⚠️ **DONE.** `RetentionRule` with 19 cases, half of them KEEPS. ⚠️ **AND `safetyMargin` IS IN OFFSETS WHILE THE CORPUS SIZES IT IN TIME**: research 09 §6.1 says to size it above the observed Lucene commit interval, the rule adds it to an offset, and the conversion is the records a stream commits within that interval -- so M7.14 yields the interval and the margin follows from it and the rate. Until then the value is a configured guess and every report says so | FR-9, NFR-13 | done |
| M7.6 | **GC FROM THE COMMIT LOG: EXPIRED SEGMENTS DELETED IN BATCHES, ZERO LIST.** Everything GC needs is already in the chain -- `CommitDelta -> SegmentCommit(segmentKey, runs) -> RunCommit(key, recordCount, firstOffset)`, so a segment's last offset for a stream is `firstOffset + recordCount - 1` and its age is the timestamp already in `SegmentKey`. ⚠️ **NO LIST, NO HEAD, NO PER-SEGMENT GET** (R2): exact information is already held, and a LIST costs a PUT each. DELETEs are free and `DeleteObjects` batches 1,000 keys, so the budget is **<=1 DELETE call per 1,000 keys** and it is asserted by `CountingBinStore` over >=2,500 segments rather than reasoned about. ⚠️ **DONE.** `SegmentGc` with 12 cases -- 2,500 expired segments at 0 LIST, 0 GET, 0 PUT and 3 DELETE calls, the 1,000-key batch boundary, and an EMPTY pass at zero requests of any kind. ⚠️ **AND THE RUNS MERGE ACROSS DELTAS BEFORE ANYTHING IS JUDGED**: one object can be named by two deltas, and judging each mention separately deletes on the first that says yes, with the other stream's unread records inside it. ⚠️ **`SegmentKey.timestampOf` IS NEW AND READS THE TAIL**, because a `stat` per segment would cost a request per object collected -- the absence of which is what makes commit-log-driven GC free | FR-9 | done |
| M7.7 | **THE POINTED DELTA IS PINNED; SUPERSEDED DELTAS AND CHECKPOINTS ARE COLLECTED.** ⚠️ **THIS IS THE DEBT ADR-0036 NAMED AND M5.55 MADE UNBOUNDED**: a checkpoint pod-slot points at the delta that last applied for that pod so a detected replay can be ANSWERED rather than guessed, and since M5.55 inherited pointers ride in every checkpoint and are re-propagated by each successor -- so a pinned delta can be of ANY age, not one term old. GC that pins by AGE passes on a young chain and deletes the pointer's target on a real one, which turns ADR-0036 criterion 6 ("a detected replay is ALWAYS answered") false. The fixture therefore uses a pointer OLDER than `maxRetention`. ⚠️ **DONE.** `ChainGc` in `sequencer` -- not `ingest`, because the keys are `LogKeys` and that record is package-private there -- with 17 cases: the three-part collectability test, a pointer matched on (epoch, sequence) and not the sequence alone, the newest checkpoint never deleted, and a TAIL of older ones kept because a recovery that has already read `LATEST` would 404 on one deleted between the two requests. ⚠️ **AND "SUPERSEDED" IS DEFINED IN THE SPEC BECAUSE ITS OBVIOUS READING LOSES RETAINED DATA** (found by M7.0's spec review): ADR-0033 keeps the offset-to-segment index in the DELTAS and deliberately out of the checkpoint, so a delta is the only object that can say which segment holds an offset. Deleting every delta below the newest checkpoint leaves RETAINED segments unlocatable -- the consumer gets nothing for an offset inside the retention window -- and no deletion criterion can see it. Collectable means all three: below the newest checkpoint, EVERY segment it indexes already deleted (research 06 §4's second conjunct, which the one-line version drops), and no live pod-slot pointer | FR-9 | done |
| M7.8 | **THE ORPHAN SWEEP: ONE HOUR-PREFIX PER PASS, A GRACE PERIOD, AND A LIST BUDGET.** A pod that died after the PUT and before the commit leaves a segment the log BY DEFINITION cannot know about, so this is the one job LIST keeps (research 06 §4). ⚠️ **THE GRACE PERIOD MUST EXCEED THE MAXIMUM POSSIBLE COMMIT DELAY, INCLUDING THE DEGRADED PATH -- GETTING IT WRONG DELETES LIVE DATA**, and the case that matters is the within-grace uncommitted segment being KEPT, not the past-grace one being deleted. ⚠️ Bounded to one LIST per 1,000 keys over ONE hour-prefix at a time (`SegmentKey.hourPrefix` already exists), 44 LIST for a 43,200-object hour (`ceil(43200/1000)` -- the corpus says 43, which rounds DOWN, and an assertion written to 43 reds a correct sweep), and under R15's ~1/s sustained ceiling -- asserted by a counting store, because no governor exists to refuse it (NFR-16 is M10's). ⚠️ **AND THE 44 WAS NOT ASSERTED IN THE END, WHICH M7's MILESTONE REVIEW MEASURED** (M7.22): the case that landed puts 2,500 keys and asserts 3 LIST, and 44 appears only inside an `as()` description, so the R15 claim rests on proportionality pinned at two points rather than on the hour. ⚠️ **THE DEFAULTS ARE NAMED RATHER THAN LEFT TO THE IMPLEMENTATION**: `orphanGrace = 1 h` and a sweep period of 1 h (research 06 §4), and the grace is generous on purpose -- the degraded `ctl/inbox/` commit path is slower than the direct one and does not exist yet to be measured (M8's). R15 is a RATE and a counting store counts, so the budget is LIST calls per sweep over an injected clock, which bounds the rate only because the period is named. ⚠️ **DONE.** `OrphanSweep` with 19 cases: the within-grace KEEP and the boundary itself, a committed key never a candidate whatever its age, ONE hour-prefix per pass, 2,500 keys at THREE LIST and THREE DELETE, an empty prefix at exactly one LIST, and zero GET and zero stat -- the age is in the key, and a store's last-modified time is not the write time once an object has been copied by a lifecycle rule | FR-9 | done |
| M7.9 | **GC IS A LEASED ROLE, AND A FENCED GC DELETES NOTHING.** Same lease mechanism as the sequencer (research 06 §4) -- never two pods deleting at once. ⚠️ **THE ASSERTION IS THE ABSENCE OF A DELETE AFTER FENCING, NOT THE PRESENCE OF AN EXCEPTION**: a loser that throws on its next store call has already deleted, and the delete is what is irreversible. ⚠️ **DONE.** `GcLease` and `LeasedGc` with 7 cases: the fence is re-asked BEFORE EVERY BATCH rather than once per pass, so a pass that loses the lease mid-way issues ONE delete call and not three; only `delete` is fenced, because refusing reads would turn an ordinary lease loss into a cascade; the lease is released even when the pass throws; and a pod holding no lease issues ZERO requests of any kind | FR-9 | done |
| M7.10 | **`oldestRetainedOffset` BECOMES TRUE: THE CHECKPOINT RECORDS WHAT GC ACTUALLY DELETED.** `CheckpointWriter` writes a literal 0 today and its own comment (`CheckpointWriter.java:162`) says that is truthful only until M7 gives retention a meaning. ⚠️ **THIS IS THE MUTATION THAT SURVIVES EVERY OTHER M7 TEST** (found by M7.0's spec review): leave the literal 0 in place and every other row of M7's test plan stays green, while M7.16's refusal becomes unreachable by construction -- there is no offset below which to refuse. The second mutation is advancing to the deleted segment's FIRST offset rather than past its LAST, which under-reports by one segment and refuses a consumer that is fine. ⚠️ **DONE.** `RetainedOffsets.after` (8 cases) computes the LOWEST RETAINED FIRST OFFSET rather than the highest deleted end -- the two differ whenever a kept segment sits between two deleted ones, which happens whenever one stream in a shared segment is behind, and the highest-deleted reading refuses a consumer for records still in the bucket -- and `CheckpointWriter.observeRetained` (6 cases) carries it, merged with `max` so retention only moves forward -- two passes can report OUT OF ORDER once a retry is involved -- and CLAMPED to `nextOffset` because a throw there is on the COMMIT THREAD. ⚠️ **AND `RetentionPass` CONNECTS THE TWO**, which round 1 found missing: `SegmentGc` knew what it deleted, the writer had a method to be told, and nothing in the tree joined them, so a deployed ingester would have kept writing 0 after every pass and M7.16's refusal would have had no boundary to be below. `SegmentGc.Result` now carries the deleted KEYS and not only a count | FR-9 | done |
| M7.16 | **A POSITION BELOW `oldestRetainedOffset` IS REFUSED DISTINGUISHABLY** (FR-10's `oldestRetainedOffset` half; split from M7.10 by M7.0's spec review, which measured it as two commits and a possible third). ⚠️ **SILENCE HERE REPRODUCES ADR-0020's FAILURE MODE ONE LEVEL UP** -- a shard that starts, reports healthy and indexes nothing. And it is distinguishable from BOTH neighbours: an empty delivery means "nothing new", M6.1's GAP means "records were dropped", and this means "your position is gone and data was lost", which is an operator incident rather than a retry. ⚠️ **WHETHER THE REFUSAL IS A WIRE-FORMAT CHANGE IS DECIDED IN THIS COMMIT, NOT ASSUMED**: if it needs a new frame or a new field on the subscription, it carries the `wire-format-change` obligations and an ADR; if an existing refusal carries it, the commit says which and why that is not an overload. ⚠️ **DONE, AND THE ANSWER IS NO -- WITH A SPLIT.** `PositionCollectedException` in `client` carries the stream, the requested offset, the floor and the SIZE of the loss, and `ConsumerClient.refuseIfCollected` throws it, because research doc 02 §6's rule that an exception out of the poll path PAUSES that shard is exactly right here and exactly wrong for a gap: the records below the floor will never arrive however long the shard waits. 14 cases. ⚠️ **BUT CARRYING THE FLOOR FROM THE INGESTER TO THE CONSUMER IS A WIRE-FORMAT CHANGE AND IS NOT IN THIS COMMIT** -- `retainedFrom` is the seam, nothing production calls it, and **M7.18** owns the frame | FR-9, FR-10 | done |
| M7.18 | **THE RETAINED FLOOR NEVER REACHES THE CONSUMER, SO M7.16's REFUSAL CANNOT FIRE IN PRODUCTION** (opened by M7.16). `ConsumerClient.retainedFrom` is a seam with no production caller: the boundary lives in the checkpoint (`oldestRetainedOffset`, true since M7.10) and the consumer holds no checkpoint reader -- by design, since a consumer that read the chain would pay a request per interval for a number that changes hourly. ⚠️ **SO IT HAS TO TRAVEL ON THE SUBSCRIPTION, WHICH MAKES IT A WIRE-FORMAT CHANGE**: a field on `SubscriptionEvent` or a frame of its own, with the `wire-format-change` obligations and an ADR. ⚠️ **AND WHERE IT IS RAISED DECIDES WHETHER AN OPERATOR EVER SEES IT**: M6.8 MEASURED `DefaultStreamPoller` catching whatever `createShardConsumer` throws, logging at WARN and retrying forever -- index GREEN, shard started, nothing indexed. So the refusal belongs on the `readNext` path, where research 02 §6 says an exception PAUSES that shard and the state is readable through `_ingestion/_state`; raised from consumer construction it is swallowed and M7.16 reproduces the failure it exists to replace, with the row already marked done. ⚠️ **AND THE FAILURE DIRECTION TODAY IS SILENT RATHER THAN LOUD**: an unknown floor refuses nothing, so a consumer whose position has been collected meets a 404 on the segment it was about to fetch -- ADR-0020's failure mode with no name on it, which is what M7.16 exists to replace ⚠️ **CLOSED BY M8.6** (ADR-0056): the floor travels on the subscription as its own frame, asked for by the consumer and read by the serving pod on demand, and the refusal is wired onto `BinStoreShardConsumer`'s `readNext`, where it pauses the shard -- it had NO production caller before, M7.16 having built the method and not the call. The end-to-end join with a real GC pass is M8.30's | FR-9, FR-10 | done |
| M7.11 | **NFR-13: A CONSUMER OFFLINE FOR THE RETENTION WINDOW LOSES NOTHING; PAST THE CEILING IT LOSES LOUDLY.** A consumer stops at offset k, the ingester keeps committing across a simulated `minRetention` with GC running throughout, and the resume reads EVERY record -- asserted by count AND by content, because a resume that reads none and throws nothing passes an absence-only case. ⚠️ **T2 AND NOT T4 DELIBERATELY**: the property is our rule over a simulated six hours, a real node adds no evidence and costs six hours. The second half is the same consumer past `maxRetention`, which must meet **M7.16's** refusal and M7.5's alarm. ⚠️ **DONE.** `ConsumerOutageToleranceTest`, 4 cases: SEVEN hours of flushes at 15 minutes with a GC pass after every one and the consumer frozen at 0 -- ⚠️ **the extra hour past the floor is what makes it a test**, since stopping AT six hours leaves every segment inside the floor and a rule deleting on AGE ALONE would keep everything too; the other direction, where a consumer that KEEPS UP lets its read records go and what is left is a SUFFIX rather than a hole; the ceiling deleting and ALARMING; and the boundary reaching the reporting path. ⚠️ **THE REFUSAL ITSELF IS ASSERTED IN `RetainedOffsetRefusalTest` (M7.16)**, because `ingest` does not depend on `client` and must not -- ⚠️ M7.10's until round 3 of this spec's review caught it, a stale task ID left by the M7.10/M7.16 split and the same defect as the stale criterion number round 2 caught one level down | NFR-13 | done |
| M7.19 | **`RoutedIngestTest#aROUTEDWriteToAnUNREGISTEREDIndexWAITSAndIsRELEASED` FLAKES UNDER LOAD, AND THE TWO DEADLINES ARE EQUAL** (found while running M7.11's build, and MEASURED by that task's reviewer: it failed once under a parallel build and passed on rerun). The case builds `RoutedIngest` with a TEN-SECOND pending wait and then spins with its OWN ten-second deadline before calling `register()`, so on a loaded machine the production wait expires first and `write.get(10, SECONDS)` throws. ⚠️ **THE FIX IS TO WIDEN THE PRODUCTION TIMEOUT ABOVE THE SPIN DEADLINE, NOT TO RAISE BOTH**: equal budgets make the outcome a race between two timers rather than an assertion about the pool, and raising both keeps the race and makes the suite slower. ⚠️ **AND IT IS NOT M7's DEFECT** -- the case predates this milestone and nothing in M7 touches that path; it is recorded here because this is where it was seen | FR-13 | todo |
| M7.12 | **NFR-2 RE-ASSERTED WITH PROGRESS AND GC LIVE, AND THE TWO REQUEST BUDGETS ASSERTED.** >=1,000 idle consumers over >=3,000 intervals reporting progress every interval, with a GC loop running and nothing expired: ZERO object-store requests and ZERO grants. ⚠️ **RE-ASSERTED RATHER THAN INHERITED** -- M5's and M6's SPECs both record why "it held before" is not evidence, and M7 adds two new things that could buy a request: a frame on the subscription and a loop that exists to call the store. ⚠️ The GC half is the sharper one: a GC pass that LISTS when nothing has expired is the natural implementation and it is what this row refuses. ⚠️ **DONE.** `IdleConsumerCostTest` extended with `theWATERMARKPathAndTheGCLoopCostZEROOfBothNumbersWhenIDLE`: 1,000 idle consumers, 3,000 intervals, a progress frame observed into the watermark table EVERY interval and a `RetentionPass` run EVERY interval over a non-empty chain -- zero requests, zero grants -- and its red record is exactly the listing-GC mutation. ⚠️ **THE BUDGETS THEMSELVES ARE ASSERTED WHERE THE WORK IS**: 2,500 expired segments at 3 DELETE and 0 LIST in `CommitLogGcTest`, and, in `OrphanSweepTest`, 2,500 keys at 3 LIST and 3 DELETE at the 1,000 page size plus a separate 100-key case pinning a CONFIGURED page size of 40 at 3 LIST, rather than restated here | NFR-2 | done |
| M7.13 | **OBSERVABILITY: THE WATERMARK, THE OLDEST RETAINED AGE, THE OUTAGE BUDGET, AND THE THREE ALARMS.** Research 09 §9: per partition the tail offset, the min watermark, the lag, the oldest retained age; alarms for any copy silent beyond `reportTimeout`, any segment approaching `maxRetention`, any paused shard older than `minRetention`. ⚠️ **THE ONE TILE WORTH A DASHBOARD IS `minRetention - age(oldest unread data)`** -- how much outage budget is left. ⚠️ **AND IT IS READ AT TWO AGES AND MUST COUNT DOWN, BECAUSE A CONSTANT GAUGE PASSES EVERYTHING ELSE AND A SIGN FLIP PASSES A DIFFERENCE-ONLY ASSERTION** (found by round 3 of this spec's review): `return minRetention` is exported, carries no label and satisfies every alarm case, while the tile reads a healthy six hours with the budget minutes from zero. ⚠️ **AND NO PER-STREAM, PER-INDEX OR PER-COPY LABEL** (observability.md rule 1, `check-metric-cardinality.sh`): the detail goes in a bounded top-K event, the shape M4.14 already established. ⚠️ **DONE.** `RetentionObservable` with 29 cases: the budget COUNTS DOWN (a constant gauge and a sign flip are both named and both red), it is the WORST stream's rather than an average, it goes NEGATIVE rather than clamping -- an hour over and a day over are different incidents -- and it is NULL when nothing is known, because zero would page an operator about a cluster that has not started. Each of the three alarms fires INDEPENDENTLY, the ceiling one BEFORE the deletion rather than at it, the same condition does not alarm twice in a row, and a condition that CLEARS can alarm again: de-duplication must not become suppression. ⚠️ **AND THE SILENCE IS PER COPY, NOT PER STREAM** (found by review, MEASURED): keyed by stream, two healthy copies keep the entry fresh while a third is wedged and nothing alarms -- while `WatermarkTable` takes `min()` across ALL known copies and freshness from the OLDEST report, so that stream's watermark IS frozen. ⚠️ **AND NOTHING WAS EVER FORGOTTEN**: an entry for a deleted index or a relocated copy alarmed for ever and, under the de-duplication rule, could then never re-raise for a real incident -- `deregisterCopy` and `observeNothingUnread` are the clearing paths | FR-9 | done |
| M7.20 | **`check-metric-cardinality.sh` DOES NOT SEE A LABEL MAP**, so criterion 17's "the gate is green with no per-stream label" is true by ACCIDENT (found by M7.13's review, MEASURED). Its regex matches `tag|label|attribute|withTag|addTag|put` followed by a forbidden name, and `Map.of("stream", stream.toString())` inside a `labels()` method matches none of them -- the reviewer added exactly that and the gate stayed green, with only a test catching it. ⚠️ **THIS IS A RUNG-3 GATE FAILING QUIETLY, WHICH IS WORSE THAN NO GATE**: `gate-design`'s ladder says a rule statable as a predicate over files must not be left to an agent, and this one IS so statable -- it is the pattern that is too narrow, not the rule. ⚠️ **AND WIDENING IT NEEDS CARE**: `Map.of(` and `entry(` appear all over the tree for things that are not labels, so the fix is to recognise the SHAPE that reaches a metric rather than to add two more verbs | FR-9 | todo |
| M7.14 | **MEASUREMENT M5 AND M6: THE COMMITTED-POINTER INTERVAL ON A REAL NODE, AND `safetyMargin` SIZED ABOVE IT.** 50-open-questions §3 assigns both constants to M7 and the standing rule is that a task blocked on a measurement constant builds the thing that measures it. ⚠️ **IT REPORTS A NUMBER AND ASSERTS ONLY THAT THE NUMBER EXISTS AND THE DEFAULT EXCEEDS IT** -- a test asserting a specific interval would pin OpenSearch's translog policy, which is not ours to pin. ⚠️ **AND IF IT CANNOT BE MEASURED, `safetyMargin` IS A GUESS AND `VERIFIED.md` SAYS `NOT-MEASURED`** rather than shipping a plausible default quietly. M6's half -- can the plugin observe the COMMITTED pointer rather than the in-memory one -- is an ADR if the answer is yes, because it shrinks the margin and therefore the storage bill. ⚠️ **DONE, AND THE ANSWER IS YES**: `CommitIntervalProbeIT` reads `batch_start` out of the shard's last Lucene commit user data -- `commitUserDataKeys=[max_unsafe_auto_id_timestamp, batch_start, max_seq_no, min_retained_seq_no, local_checkpoint, history_uuid, translog_uuid]`, `batchStart` moving from `0000000000000000000` at shard creation to `0000000000000000059` after sixty records -- so ADR-0005's "OpenSearch does not expose the committed one" is true of the ingestion-state API and false of a plugin running INSIDE the node. **ADR-0051**, which AMENDS ADR-0005's consequence in this commit. ⚠️ **AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THE PROBE ASSERTED ONLY THAT THE KEY EXISTED**, which review measured passing with the flush removed and sixty records unaccounted for -- an empty commit at shard creation already carries `batch_start=0`. ⚠️ **AND M5 IS A FLOOR RATHER THAN A FIGURE**: the pointer did not advance on its own in three consecutive TEN-SECOND observations (10027/10026/10025 ms) because a commit follows the translog flush policy (512 MB or 30 minutes), not ingestion -- so the margin cannot be sized at production rates from one shard and 60 records, and `DEFAULT_SAFETY_MARGIN = 10_000` is an upper bound on a guess, which the ADR says in as many words. ⚠️ **AND ADR-0051's FIRST DRAFT CLAIMED THE MARGIN NOW COVERS ONLY THE REPORT INTERVAL, WHICH REVIEW REFUTED FROM THE PROBE'S OWN OUTPUT**: sixty records were live and searchable while the committed pointer still read 0 for thirty seconds. What reporting the committed pointer buys is the error's DIRECTION -- behind-durable rather than ahead-of-durable, so being wrong costs storage rather than records -- and the cost it carries is that a LOW-RATE stream's data is kept until `maxRetention`, where the ceiling alarm fires as the normal end of the window rather than as an incident. | FR-9 | done |

A partial selection is the bounded-work shape. ⚠️ **NOT URGENT AND RECORDED ANYWAY**: it runs over an in-memory map and nothing calls it yet, so it is a performance note with no measured cost -- but a per-interval full sort over every stream on the node is exactly the thing that is invisible until the stream count grows | FR-9 | todo |

| M8.0 | **M8's SPEC, ADR-0052, AND THE DECOMPOSITION.** ⚠️ **THE MILESTONE CANNOT DEMONSTRATE ITS OWN CONDITION AGAINST A SYSTEM THAT DOES NOT EXIST**: there is no production `main()` in the tree (M5.91), no real object-store backend (the roadmap's deferred table assigns it to no milestone), and a list of mechanisms enumerated in ADR-0052 is constructed only by their own tests -- and the list is GIVEN rather than counted, because every count this project has written for a set like this has been short -- of which one is worse than unwired, because no `src/main` method can produce the commit chain every GC cost claim is measured against (M7.25). `MemoryBinStore` is a `ConcurrentHashMap` in the killed process's own heap, so its RPO is 0 for survivors and infinite for the killed pod INDEPENDENT OF ANY CODE THIS PROJECT WROTE -- a measurement of the fixture. **ADR-0052** decides it: M8 owns assembly and the first real backend as its FIRST tasks, before any chaos task, with the completion condition unchanged. ⚠️ **AN ORDERING DECISION, NOT NEW SCOPE** -- every requirement M8 already owned it still owns, and what is refused is demonstrating them against nothing ⚠️ **DELIVERED IN `ad816c3` AND LEFT MARKED `todo`** -- a bookkeeping slip found and corrected by M8.6, whose commit says so | — (planning) | done |
| M8.1 | **THE `server` MODULE AND THE COMPOSITION ROOT: CONFIG IN, OBJECT GRAPH OUT.** A new module that depends on everything and that NOTHING depends on, so `check-module.sh`'s dependency surface gains a leaf rather than a cycle. ⚠️ **NOT A CLASS IN `http`**: that module is the thin adapter (architecture.md rule 4, ADR-0019) and the in-process API is the primary one, so a `main()` there would make the HTTP surface the assembly point and every in-process embedder would inherit a web server. ⚠️ **AND IT LANDS EARLY AND INCOMPLETE ON PURPOSE** -- a root that arrives last discovers every composition failure at once, and the mechanisms enumerated in M8's SPEC § *The unwired set* have never been in one object graph. Nothing is wired to a socket in this row. ⚠️ **ADDING A MODULE UPDATES `settings.gradle.kts` AND architecture.md IN THE SAME COMMIT**, which the settings file says in as many words. ⚠️ **DONE.** `server`, last in `settings.gradle.kts` and in architecture.md's module table -- it depends on `ingest`, `sequencer` and `binstore-backends`, and NOTHING depends on it. `StoreConfig` + `StoreFactory` are the only `src/main` site that turns a configured NAME into a backend, and every misconfiguration is REFUSED rather than defaulted: an unknown kind, a `local-fs` with no root, and ⚠️ **a `memory` kind GIVEN a root**, because an ignored setting is a lie an operator cannot see -- someone who configured a root meant durability. `Assembly` asserts CONNECTEDNESS rather than behaviour, which is what eight milestones of green component suites could not: the writer's store IS the sequencer's (asserted at `ctl/log/`, ⚠️ **not at `ctl/`, because the writer's own index registry lives at `ctl/registry/indices.json` -- MEASURED, and it made the first draft of that case pass against a sequencer wired to a different store entirely**), the hub the root exposes IS the one the writer publishes to, and the stream a write lands on is the one `RunKey.ofIndexUuid` names rather than one made up per index NAME. ⚠️ **`close()` RELEASES THE LEASE, ASSERTED ON THE LEASE OBJECT's OWN EXPIRY** -- a successor merely LEADING is also what happens when each pod has a store of its own, so it cannot carry that assertion. ⚠️ **THE TRANSPORT IS A PARAMETER**, because no production one exists until M8.20, and ⚠️ **AN INJECTED STORE IS NOT CLOSED BY `close()`**: the chaos harness runs several pods over one store in one JVM, and closing it would read as a store outage nobody injected | NFR-3, NFR-2 (the seam the budgets are asserted at) | done |
| M8.2 | **THE FIRST REAL OBJECT-STORE BACKEND, AGAINST MinIO: PUT, GET, CONDITIONAL WRITE, LIST, DELETE** (the conformance suite that judges it is M8.22, split out by this spec's review). S3-compatible, so a partition, a slow endpoint and a signed URL become things that can HAPPEN rather than things that are modelled. Closes the roadmap's unassigned "first backend that can presign" row (ADR-0041). ⚠️ **THE CONFORMANCE SUITE ALREADY PASSES AGAINST TWO BACKENDS AND THAT BUYS THE SPI's SHAPE, NOT A DEPLOYMENT's BEHAVIOUR** -- both answer `presignedUrls=false` and neither has a network. ⚠️ **AND MinIO IS NOT S3**: where the protocol is ambiguous the two differ, ADR-0011's CAS turns on `If-None-Match`, and both support it with DIFFERENT ERROR SHAPES -- so the suite asserts the shape we depend on, and `VERIFIED.md` says which criteria rest on MinIO alone. ⚠️ A container in the test path is a build.md cost (memory caps, Docker off by default). ⚠️ **DONE.** `S3BinStore` and `S3MultipartWriter` over the AWS SDK v2 SYNCHRONOUS client (ADR-0054), with 13 T3 cases against a real MinIO -- put/get, an inclusive range, stat, both conditional writes, a lexicographic page that resumes STRICTLY after its cursor, a batch delete, a multipart upload that occupies the key only on complete, and an aborted one that leaves nothing staged. ⚠️ **THE TWO CONDITIONAL-WRITE FAILURES ARE NOT THE SAME FAILURE**: 412 is "someone else got there", which is an ordinary empty, and 404 under `If-Match` is "there is nothing to match", which THROWS -- a lease renewal that read its own lease's disappearance as "another pod holds it" would wait out a TTL for a term nobody holds. ⚠️ **AND THE JDK's OWN HTTP CLIENT**: excluding Netty and Apache HttpClient 5 took the pinned-jar count from 45 to 30, from one list that drives both the module's exclusion and the root's licence gate. ⚠️ `presignedUrls` STAYS FALSE until M8.18, because M5.42's obligation is a behaviour rather than a signature | NFR-8 | done |
| M8.3 | **THE COMMIT CHAIN's IN-MEMORY SOURCE, SO A GC PASS COSTS NO GET** (M7.25, and the criterion M7 could not write). `List<CommitDelta>` is consumed by `RetentionPass`, `SegmentGc` and `RetainedOffsets` and produced by NOTHING in `src/main` -- not even expensively: `CommitLog.recover()` returns `void` and `ChainReplay.Result` discards the deltas it reads. ⚠️ **SO M7's "0 LIST, 0 GET" IS TRUE OF `SegmentGc` AND UNPROVEN OF A PASS**, and a wiring that reached for the recovery walk anyway would pay one LIST per 1,000 deltas plus one GET per delta, PER PASS, for ever. ⚠️ **THIS ROW COMES BEFORE ANY GC LOOP IS WIRED** (ADR-0052 § Decision 3), because the cheap wrong answer is available the moment the loop exists. ⚠️ **DONE.** `ChainMemory`, fed from `CommitLog.apply` and NOT from `commit` -- the same argument the compaction histogram beside it records: `apply` runs on a live commit AND on every entry a replay reads, so a leader that has just taken over holds the chain the LOG describes rather than the one its own process watched. `ChainReplay.Result` now carries the deltas it had been decoding and discarding, which is the whole of M7.25. ⚠️ **THE CRITERION M7 COULD NOT WRITE IS NOW ASSERTED**: a full `RetentionPass` over this source costs 0 LIST, 0 GET and 0 STAT, with the premises asserted too -- three deltas really in the chain, segments really seven hours old, three really deleted in ONE batched request -- and the same over a RECOVERED log, where the walk is paid once at startup as R2 permits. ⚠️ **BOUNDED, AND WHAT IT DROPS IS VISIBLE**: 100,000 deltas by default, about seven hours of a term at M7's 250 ms commit interval with the GC loop stopped, past which the OLDEST goes and the snapshot says `complete=false` -- a pass over an incomplete chain UNDER-deletes, which is the safe direction, but one that believed itself complete would move a retained-offset boundary over segments it never saw. The ordinary way it stays small is `forgetThrough`, which a pass calls with how far it got | NFR-3 | done |

| M8.5 | **THE RETENTION LOOP, THE GC LEASE AND THE ORPHAN SWEEP WIRED, WITH THEIR COST BUDGETS ASSERTED IN-PROCESS.** Closes **M7.21n, M7.24 and M7.26** -- ⚠️ **NOT M7.18, WHICH IS M8.6's**, and not M7.19, which is a flaky test deadline naming no mechanism at all; an earlier draft of this row claimed both and dropped M7.26. ⚠️ **COMPOSING `LeasedGc` WITH `RetentionPass` THE OBVIOUS WAY COMPILES, PASSES, AND DELETES UNFENCED** -- MEASURED at M7.10 and recorded in its commit body, which makes this the one wiring row with a known silent data-loss path waiting in it. ⚠️ **AND THE IDLE ASSERTION IS WALL CLOCK, NOT AN INJECTED ONE**: M5's and M7's zero-request proofs drive a `Clock` seam, and what this row adds is that no REAL timer in the assembled process fires a request ⚠️ **DONE, AND SPLIT.** `RetentionLoop` drives `RetentionPass`, `LeasedGc`, `OrphanSweep` and `RetentionObservable` on the assembled graph's own scheduler, under a production `StoreGcLease` over `LeaseManager` -- `GcLease` had only a test implementation, so the fence that stops a pod that lost the role from deleting could not be built by anything that shipped. ⚠️ **THE KNOWN DATA-LOSS PATH IS MADE UNREPRESENTABLE RATHER THAN TESTED AROUND**: the loop holds NO store, and the `SegmentGc` and `OrphanSweep` are constructed inside `runIfLeader` from the fenced store it hands them, so there is no unfenced one to reach for. ⚠️ **A TICK WITH NOTHING PAST THE FLOOR COSTS NOTHING, NOT EVEN THE LEASE**: whether anything could be due is decided in memory before the lease is asked for, since taking it is a conditional write and a read. ⚠️ **AND THE GATE IS COARSE, WHICH COSTS SOMETHING**: a segment past the floor that the rule KEEPS -- unread, or with no fresh watermark -- makes every tick take and release the lease, about three requests per interval on the GC leader for up to the retention ceiling. Per node and nothing else, so inside cost.md, but not zero; the exact gate would run the rule first, and `verdictFor` raises the ceiling alarm as a side effect. `AssembledGcCostIT` measures M8's criterion 4 on the REAL timer, counted by the loop's own tick counter: ten passes, zero LIST, GET and DELETE. ⚠️ **TWO HAZARDS FOUND IN THE DESIGN, EACH CLOSED IN THE CODE**: (1) forgetting a pass's whole snapshot would forget KEPT deltas, which both leaks their segments and drops them from the orphan sweep's keep list -- which FAILS OPEN, so a committed segment would be deleted as an orphan; `ChainMemory.forgetCollected` forgets only a prefix whose every segment was deleted. (2) After a takeover the chain lacks everything below the newest checkpoint, so the sweep is confined to hours starting at least `SKEW_MARGIN` after the term was first seen, over a COMPLETE chain only. (3) FOUND BY REVIEW, AND IT WAS BLOCKING: `heldTerm()` keeps returning a term after a takeover has FENCED it -- the term retires only when a commit through it throws, so a node that receives no writes keeps a dead one -- and its frozen chain as the sweep's keep list would delete the SUCCESSOR's committed segments, with the GC lease genuinely held so the fence passed every batch. `LocalSequencer.serving()` is now asked when the term is obtained, before the pass, and before every hour's sweep -- and ⚠️ **ROUND 2 FOUND THE FIRST VERSION OF IT CHECKING ONLY THE FLAGS**: `fenced` is set only by an EMPTY renew, so a renew that keeps throwing and a renewer that has died both leave a term HELD, unfenced, and past its TTL. `serving()` now also requires the term's own lease unexpired by its own clock (`LeaseManager.heldUnexpired()`, no request), which bounds all three paths at the TTL. ⚠️ **NOT DONE HERE, EACH WITH A ROW**: `ChainGc` (M8.39, because every checkpoint source is a read per pass), criterion 3's 1-versus-1,000-subscriber idle equality (M8.40), and what the loop cannot reach after a takeover (M8.42). ⚠️ **THE FLOOR's DEFAULT IS NOW NFR-13's 6 HOURS**; M8.4 left a 1-hour placeholder in `Assembly` that nothing had read yet. `RetentionObservable` is constructed and swept every tick; its report inputs arrive with M8.6's position source | NFR-3, NFR-2, FR-9 | done |
| M8.6 | **`ProgressReporter`'s PRODUCTION POSITION SOURCE (M7.17) AND THE RETAINED-FLOOR FRAME (M7.18).** ADR-0051 named the source and M7 did not wire it: the shard's last Lucene commit carries `batch_start`, readable through `IndexShard.acquireLastIndexCommit` from inside the node. ⚠️ **THAT RETURNS A `GatedCloseable` THAT PINS SEGMENT FILES**, so a reporter that leaks it grows node disk silently -- the probe closes correctly and the production path must too. ⚠️ **AND THE TEMPTING SOURCES ARE BOTH WRONG**, in the two directions M7.17's row records: what our consumer DELIVERED is ahead of what the shard COMMITTED by exactly the window retention exists to protect, and `readNext`'s carried pointer freezes at the last reset ⚠️ **SPLIT, AND THIS ROW IS NOW THE FLOOR ALONE** (M7.18): the position source (M7.17) is M8.43, because it is read from inside an OpenSearch node and can only be tested against a real cluster, while the floor is a wire change testable without one. ⚠️ **DONE, AS A WIRE-FORMAT CHANGE WITH AN ADR** ([ADR-0056](decisions/0056-the-retained-floor-travels-on-the-subscription-read-by-the-serving-pod.md)): a new format type `RetainedFloor` (magic `BPRF`, v1) with golden bytes WRITTEN BY HAND from the ADR's layout rather than produced by the encoder; sent as the FIRST frame of an answer, and only to a poll that asks (`floor=1`), because every decoder in `format` refuses an unknown magic and an old plugin handed one would refuse the whole answer; the consumer asks only when a shard RESUMES, for a bounded number of polls, and only a floor reported after the resume may clear it. ⚠️ **TWO EARLIER SHAPES WERE WRONG AND REVIEW FOUND BOTH**: sending only on the session-creating answer lost the floor to one dropped response, and asking until one arrived then never again asked FOR EVER where no floor existed -- a store read per interval on an idle pod -- while never refreshing one that did, so a reset hours later was checked against a floor GC had passed. ⚠️ **READ BY THE SERVING POD, ON DEMAND, NOT BY THE CONSUMER AND NOT ON A TIMER**: `RetainedFloors` reads the newest checkpoint of the term this pod last committed under -- a stat and a get -- at most once per refresh interval and only when a poll that asks arrives, so a burst of resumes costs one read per pod and a node whose shards are all tailing costs none. The cache only rises, since a takeover's first checkpoint can report less than the term before it. ⚠️ **THE TWO LAMBDAS THAT WOULD HAVE DROPPED IT** -- `ConsumerClient`'s `client::deliver` and the plugin's node router -- now implement the floor AND the ask; a method reference implements only the abstract method, and both new ones default to doing nothing. `SubscriptionFloorTest` runs a real `ConsumerClient` over the real transport against the real service and watches it refuse. ⚠️ **AND THE REFUSAL HAD NO PRODUCTION CALLER, WHICH REVIEW FOUND** -- `ConsumerClient.refuseIfCollected` was called only by tests, so the floor arrived and nothing read it, and an earlier draft of this row claimed the opposite. `BinStoreShardConsumer` now records a resume's position, requests a fresh floor, and refuses it on BOTH `readNext` overloads whenever the held floor is above it, throwing out of `readNext` so the SHARD PAUSES visibly (research 02 §6); it keeps checking until a floor reported AFTER the resume has passed it. ⚠️ **THE END-TO-END JOIN** (a real GC pass, then the refusal) is criterion 22's and M8.30's | FR-9, NFR-13 | done |
| M8.7 | **GRACEFUL SHUTDOWN IN RESEARCH 08 §7's ORDER, WITH THE GRACE BUDGET MEASURED.** That section says the ordering "gets more of the value than any failover code", and step 5 alone turns a 10 s visibility stall into sub-second ON EVERY DEPLOY -- most real-world AZ-resilience incidents are rollouts. ⚠️ **ASSERTED AS THE OBSERVED ORDER OF EVENTS, NOT AS A CLEAN EXIT CODE**: releasing the lease BEFORE telling subscribers exits cleanly and produces exactly the stall the ordering exists to prevent. ⚠️ **AND THE BUDGET IS REPORTED AS A NUMBER**, because `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` must exceed the worst case of steps 2-5 and an operator has to set it | NFR-9 | done — `ShutdownSequence` runs §7's five steps in order over a `DrainGate`: readiness fails (`/ready` 503, `/live` 200), waiting polls are woken with a 503 and new ones refused, bulk admission closes and the requests inside are flushed and answered, then the final flush, then the lease. MEASURED: Helidon's own JVM shutdown hook stopped the listener mid-drain and is now off, and the JDK's logging reset lost the step lines, so `Main` prints the report. `ShutdownDrainIT` SIGTERMs a real process with a subscriber, a request inside and a held term: 7 ms, in order, the request answered 202, both writes in the store, the lease released |
