| M5.62 | **THE HUB CAN MERGE A CONSUMER’S STREAMS AND THE PLUGIN NEVER ASKS IT TO.** M5.40a made `SubscriptionHub` group by `Subscriber` IDENTITY, so a consumer that registers ONE `Subscriber` for K runs is handed the segment once. ⚠️ PRODUCTION REGISTERS K OF THEM. `NodeSubscriptions.clientFor(RunKey)` builds one `ConsumerClient` per run and each constructs its own `transport.subscribe(key, lambda)`, so a node holding 178 runs of a segment is still 178 consumers to the hub and still takes 178 copies. ⚠️ SO M5.40a BOUGHT THE CONTRACT AND NOT THE BYTES, and that is stated rather than implied: the property is asserted at the hub and holds for anything that opts in, and nothing in the plugin opts in yet. ⚠️ WHAT IT NEEDS: a node-scoped subscriber that `NodeSubscriptions` owns, routing each push to the per-key queue its `ConsumerClient` already has, plus a `SubscriptionTransport` that can register one subscriber against MANY keys -- today `subscribe` takes exactly one `RunKey`. ⚠️ AND THE FAILURE SEMANTICS FOLLOW THE NODE: one stream means one broken stream fails every run that node holds, which is M5.40a’s all-or-none rule arriving where an operator can see it. ⚠️ NOT THE SAME ROW AS M5.40a: that one is `ingest` alone, this one crosses `client` and `plugin` and changes a transport interface ⚠️ **DONE.** `SubscriptionTransport.subscribe(List<RunKey>, Listener)` registers ONE listener against many streams, `ConsumerClient` gains a FED constructor holding no subscription of its own, and `NodeSubscriptions` owns a single node-scoped listener that routes each delivery to the client for its `Delivery.key()`. ⚠️ THE DEFAULT DOES NOT MERGE AND SAYS SO: it subscribes each key separately, which is exactly the K-consumer shape this row removes -- correct and paying for every copy -- so a transport that cannot merge is not forced to lie about it. ⚠️ THE SUBSCRIPTION IS REBUILT ONLY WHEN THE KEY SET MOVES, not on every `clientFor`: a hundred shards of ONE stream share one client and must share one registration, which `oneHundredShardsOfOneStreamOpenASingleSubscription` already measured and which a naive resubscribe breaks. ⚠️ AND THE REGISTRATION IS A TYPE, NOT A RE-SUBSCRIBE, which is what round 1 changed: `SubscriptionTransport.MultiSubscription` has `add` and `remove`, each touching ONE key. The first design rebuilt the whole registration whenever the key set moved, and review found a major in the window between the two calls -- opening the new one first registers every shared key TWICE, and nothing on the consumer path dedups a repeated `Delivery`, so `decodeInto` re-emits the same records at the same offsets; closing the old one first drops whatever arrives in the gap. It also removes the O(K^2) registration cost that design had. `aStreamOpenedLaterDoesNotREREGISTERTheOnesAlreadyHeld` counts registrations PER KEY, because a total would be satisfied by a rebuild that happened to register the same number of times. ⚠️ AND BOTH MOVE INSIDE THE MAP'S `compute`, which round 2 caught: the registration used to happen after `compute` returned, so a `release` dropping the last holder could be descheduled before its `remove` while a `clientFor` created a fresh entry and `add`ed a key that was still registered -- a no-op -- and the release then unsubscribed a stream a LIVE client held, with nothing re-entering that branch again. That is the index-reopen shape the `Entry` javadoc records from M1.17b, returned as a race. The subscribe was inside `compute` before this row moved it out, via `ConsumerClient`'s constructor. ⚠️ A FAILED `add` TAKES THE ENTRY WITH IT, which round 1 also caught: leaving it behind left a client nobody was subscribed for, and since the caller never received it and never releases it, the next `clientFor` for that key would find the entry, skip the registration branch, and that stream would receive nothing for the life of the node. ⚠️ MEASURED: `NodeSubscriptionMergeTest.aNodeHoldingTHREERunsOfOneSegmentIsOpenedONCE` drives a real `SubscriptionHub` through a merging transport and asserts the subscriber is OPENED ONCE while all three runs still get their own delivery at their own offsets. RED against per-key subscription (`scripts/tdd-red.sh`). `./gradlew build` green at 1305 tests. ⚠️ THE DEFAULT ARM IS PINNED BY `MultiSubscriptionDefaultTest`, which round 5 asked for and which is the memory note about review fixes landing unpinned: the arm is unreachable from production -- `NodeSubscriptions` starts from an empty key list and every merging transport overrides the method -- so two fixes made inside it during review were both revertible with the whole suite green. Four T0 cases now pin a duplicated key subscribing once, a partly-failed subscribe closing what it already opened, a throwing close KEEPING its handle so `close()` can try again, and `add`/`remove` touching one key. Each was observed red against the defect it names. ⚠️ AND THE THROWING-`remove` RULE IS IN THE CONTRACT, not only in the default: an implementation that unsubscribed and then threw would leave a live consumer with no registration, because the caller's recovery re-enters `remove` and never `add`. ⚠️ THE DEFAULT `MultiSubscription` IS SYNCHRONIZED ON EVERY METHOD, also round 2: its only production caller manages shards from different threads, and a lost update to its handle map leaves a stream registered that `close` never unsubscribes. ⚠️ THE ASSERTION IS THE OPEN COUNT, NOT THE DELIVERY COUNT: K deliveries are correct and required, and what must not scale with K is how many times the segment is handed over. ⚠️ FAILURE SEMANTICS FOLLOW THE NODE, as the row asked: one byte stream means one broken stream fails every run the node holds, and the interface's javadoc says so where a caller chooses | NFR-5 | done |
| M5.63 | **TWO OVERLAPPING PUBLISHES OF ONE SEGMENT ARE TWO GETS**, because `SegmentCache` is filled at the END of a read and consulted at the START. M5.40b closed the SEQUENTIAL repeat -- a late subscriber arriving after the first publish finished now hits -- and left the concurrent one, which was the other half of that row’s "coalesces nothing in flight". ⚠️ WHEN IT BITES: doc 10 §4’s "AZ recovery: many nodes replaying the same backlog", where the overlap is the normal case rather than a race. ⚠️ WHAT IT NEEDS: a per-key in-flight entry a second caller can join -- the winner reads once and the losers wait for the same bytes -- which changes the memory story, because a joiner holds the whole segment rather than streaming it. ⚠️ AND IT OWES A DECISION M5.40b DID NOT HAVE TO MAKE: what a joiner does when the winner’s read THROWS. Failing every joiner with it turns one store blip into K failed deliveries; retrying independently is the duplicate GET the row exists to remove | NFR-4 | todo |
| M5.64 | **A SHORT READ THAT DOES NOT THROW IS NOW STICKY.** `SegmentProxy` admits a segment to `SegmentCache` when its read loop ends, and an `InputStream` that ends EARLY without an exception ends that loop the same way a complete one does. ⚠️ THE CACHE MADE THIS WORSE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS THIS ROW AND NOT A PRE-EXISTING ONE: before M5.40b a truncated read cost ONE truncated delivery and the next publish read again; now the truncation is admitted and served to every later subscriber for as long as the entry lives. ⚠️ A READ THAT THROWS IS ALREADY SAFE and pinned by `aREADThatDIEDPartWayCachesNOTHING` -- the exception leaves the method before admission. This is the silent arm only. ⚠️ WHAT IT NEEDS IS THE EXPECTED LENGTH, and the obvious source is the wrong one: `stat` before every read is one request per segment, which is the cost M5.40b exists to remove. ⚠️ **AND THE OBVIOUS CHEAP CHECK DOES NOT WORK, which review caught this row claiming it did.** `headerLen` is in the segment key, but `SegmentKey`'s own javadoc says it exists so a reader can range-GET the PREAMBLE and DIRECTORY -- it says nothing about DATA. A segment is preamble + directory + DATA + footer, and DATA is almost all of it, so validating bytes-read against `32 + headerLen` catches a truncation inside the tiny header and is BLIND to one anywhere in DATA, which is where a real truncation lands. A taker who implements that check closes the row and ships the defect. ⚠️ WHAT WOULD ACTUALLY CLOSE IT: the DIRECTORY gives each run's `byteLen`, so the expected total is derivable from a header this reader already has -- or `BinStore.get` exposes the object size the response already carries, an SPI change. Either is a row's worth, which is why this is a row and not a fix | NFR-4 | todo |

Term-holding is `Leadership` (M5.6d); the refusals it does not yet make are M5.6c | FR-11 | **done** |
| M5.7 | Extend the commit-protocol simulation with pods that forward rather than lead | FR-11 | **done** -- a SECOND 1,000-seed sweep rather than a changed one, so M4's numbers stay reproducible: forwarding is a parameter the four-argument `run` leaves off, VERIFIED bit-identical to HEAD by a per-seed fingerprint over 1,000 seeds (`a8dda98e1b145071`, epochs 85872, readers 85451, acks 160480 ⚠️ (M5.26 has since re-based this deliberately -- the current aggregates are in M4's VERIFIED.md, though the per-seed fingerprint is NOT recomputed there; this line records what M5.7 measured and is left standing) on both). MEASURED with forwarding on: 4.52 forwarded commits, 39.54 attempts and 22.82 REFUSALS per seed, 14.82 commits per seed against 11.11 leader-only, 3 zero-commit seeds against 12, ZERO invariant violations. ⚠️ THE REFUSALS REACH THE RE-READ AND NOT THE RESEND -- 22,824 caught, 22,136 re-read, ZERO resends to a moved holder, which a single-threaded driver with atomic rounds cannot produce; `RemoteSequencerTest` moves the lease by hand and covers that arm. ⚠️ Followers do not purely ADD either: leader-originated commits fall 11.110 to 10.304 per seed, because a consumed round is one the leader did not get |
| M5.6a | Tell a FENCED writer apart by TYPE, not by the word "lease" in its message ⚠️ `LocalSequencer` says "this sequencer RELEASED its lease" after close and "this sequencer LOST its lease" when fenced; both contain the word, so a caller matching on the text reads a deliberate shutdown as a fencing. `CommitLog` has a second, independent fence -- discovering a successor's seal on the write. Only a fenced commit is safe to re-send elsewhere, so the distinction has to be a type | FR-11 | **done** |
| M5.6b | The COMMIT path proved across two pods: two `DefaultIngest` instances over one store, one leading and one forwarding, a producer routed to the follower landing in the leader's single total order at the leader's next offset, for the cost of the follower's own segment plus the leaseholder's one delta and zero LIST ⚠️ Split out of M5.6 because the external reviewer's packet cap refused the combined 43,646-byte diff. ⚠️ It does NOT close M4's hole and an earlier draft of this row said it did: the forwarding hop runs over `InProcessTransport`, a test fixture that is the only `SequencerTransport` in the tree (M5.6e), and there is no production `main()` to assemble any of it -- the transport is owned at M5.6e, the `main()` by nothing. | M5.6c | `FleetSequencer` and `RemoteSequencer` stop doing three things that are wrong: committing AFTER `close` (`Leadership` already refuses to elect after close, so the commit fell through to FORWARDING -- a pod that had released its lease and reported a clean shutdown went on committing through a peer); FORWARDING TO ITS OWN ENDPOINT, which ADR-0027's self-fence reaches -- an ambiguous renew stands a healthy leader down for the remainder of the TTL while the lease goes on naming it, and the transport routes the request straight back to the fenced sequencer that refused it; and `close` REPLACING the lease-release failure with the transport's, when the release is the one that says the lease was not handed back and every other pod must wait out the TTL. ⚠️ SPLIT FROM A ROW THAT HELD NINE ITEMS -- `Leadership`'s lifecycle is M5.6f and the per-commit election cost is M5.6g, because one commit of nine unrelated refusals is what review.md rule 12's cap exists to stop. ⚠️ The old row's "`leading()` narrowed to the term actually held" is re-homed NOWHERE because it is already CLOSED: M5.6d made `retire` drop the reference and `close` do `getAndSet(null)`, so `isHeld()` is the term actually held, and `FleetSequencerTest.aLeaderThatIsFENCEDFallsBackToForwardingRatherThanFailing` pins the NARROWING (`leading()` false after a fence), while `anAMBIGUOUSLocalFailureIsPROPAGATED_NeverForwarded` pins the other direction (still true after an ambiguous failure) -- an earlier draft of this cell cited only the second, which asserts the opposite of narrowing. Said here because a split that silently drops an item is indistinguishable from one that forgets it | FR-11 | **done** |
| M5.6f | `sequencer()` no longer hands back an ALREADY-CLOSED sequencer. The compare-and-set that reconciles a term won during a close returned `won` when it FAILED -- and it fails exactly when `close` got there first, so that sequencer was already closed. Null on both outcomes: winning means close did not take it and this thread gives it back; losing means close is doing so. ⚠️ THE TIDIER FIX WAS WRITTEN, MEASURED AND WITHDRAWN, and that is **ADR-0038** rather than a comment: making `close` take the election lock removes the race instead of reconciling it, and it stops the lease being released on the one path it exists for -- `takeOrFail` throws when the interrupt flag is ALREADY set and `DefaultIngest.close` arrives in exactly that state, a trap `LeaseManager.release` already dodges with `if (!tryLock()) takeOrFail(...)`. Its bound also composes to ~30 s against the 5 s `DefaultIngest` cut a wait to because thirty exceeded Kubernetes' grace period. ⚠️ The losing arm is reachable only when `close` lands BETWEEN the publish and the re-read, so it is driven through an `afterPublish` seam rather than by racing two threads for two adjacent statements -- a race there would assert a schedule. The seam is a no-op in every shipping path and carries the licence `LocalSequencer.sleepFor` already has | FR-11 | **done** |

A test both worlds agree on guards neither. ⚠️ IT ALSO INHERITS `RemoteSequencerTest.anAMBIGUOUSFailureIsNOTResent` from M5.25, which constrains "not resent" with a transport that records nothing -- a bounded retry to the SAME endpoint survives it (MEASURED), and the fixture one test above already shows the shape: `transport.sentTo()).hasSize(1)`. ⚠️ WHAT REMAINS: the existing promotion tests and `CommitProtocolSimulation` still model death as a refusal, so the fleet-level paths are still not exercised against a silent peer -- and those use the shipping 3 s wall-clock renewer beside injected clocks, which `DeadPeerTest` had to park to stop a JVM stall reddening it with no bug present | FR-11 | todo |

| M5.8 | The membership seam, static implementation, and the AZ-scoped ring. ⚠️ ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA, written before any code because the row had none: (1) the seam does NO I/O -- non-negotiable 7 -- and the static implementation is built from configuration, which is what SPEC.md means by "until the `EndpointSlice` watch lands, membership is configuration"; (2) an AZ-scoped call cannot NAME a peer in another AZ, which is criterion 10's "not addressable, not merely avoided" at the membership layer (M5.9 owns it at the fetch path); (3) the ring picks exactly ONE owner per (segment, AZ) and the choice is deterministic -- same segment and same member list, same owner, whoever asks; (4) the owner is STABLE under unrelated change: adding or removing a peer in another AZ never moves it, and removing a non-owner in the same AZ never moves it, which is what makes the hash consistent rather than merely a hash; (5) ownership is BALANCED across members, to a measured factor rather than an asserted one. ⚠️ WHY (3) MATTERS AT ALL: FR-12 mandates >=2 ingester nodes per AZ, so without a rule saying WHICH pod fetches, each fetches for its own consumers -- 2 pods x 2 non-writing AZs = 4 GETs against criterion 9's 2, and ADR-0004's arithmetic fails | NFR-4 | **done** -- `Membership` (seam, no I/O), `StaticMembership` (configuration), `Peer` (podId, endpoint, az) and `PeerRing.ownerOf`. ⚠️ RENDEZVOUS, NOT A VIRTUAL-NODE RING: both give "removing a member moves only what it owned", but rendezvous gives it with no replica count to tune, and it is called once per segment per AZ. MEASURED: moved == ownedByGone EXACTLY at 2, 3, 5 and 8 members over 30,000 segments. Skew (max over ideal) 1.000 / 1.018 / 1.019 / 1.037 ⚠️ WITH 0-BASED pod ids AND `seg/2026/09/10/` keys -- review reproduced all four series and showed the KEY PREFIX moves the figure as much as the id basis -- at 8 members, 1-based ids give 1.033 with the `seg/2026/09/10/` prefix and 1.019 with a bare `seg/` -- so both variables are named or the number means nothing. The test file's own fixtures are 1-based, giving 1.010 / 1.010 / 1.013 / 1.033. ⚠️ CRC32C ALONE WAS NOT ENOUGH -- it is linear, and pod ids differing only in their last character gave [760, 740, 1500] over 3,000 segments, one pod owning half the AZ's fetches; the 64-bit avalanche finalizer fixes it and the balance test reds without it, MEASURED. ⚠️ CRITERION (2) IS ONLY HALF CLOSED HERE, and the ADR says so where it cannot vanish at M5 close: `AzPeers` makes a MIXED view unrepresentable, but nothing at this layer knows the local pod's AZ, so asking for the wrong one is still possible -- review MEASURED 20 of 20 nameable that way. M5.9 owns the rest. ⚠️ A DUPLICATE podId IS SETTLED, NOT REFUSED: the tie-break is a total order on `(podId, endpoint)`. Refusing was the first fix and review MEASURED it worse -- it threw from `inAz` on EVERY call, 100 of 100, taking out the whole AZ including undupliated peers, and a duplicate is what every rolling restart produces (stable `podShortId` under a StatefulSet). On podId alone two pods with differently ordered config disagreed on 102 of 200 segments; on `(podId, endpoint)`, 0. ⚠️ AND ONE TEST WAS DELETED THEN RESTORED: against a bare list it asserted `x.equals(x)` and its red would not record; once `AzPeers` existed review MEASURED an implementation reading a STATIC fleet passing every other case, so the property is falsifiable after all -- just not through the parameter |
| M5.9 | Cross-AZ peer fetch not addressable by construction ⚠️ ADR-0012 measures a cross-AZ peer fetch at 419x the object-store GET it would replace and requires the rule "enforced in code, not just documented". ⚠️ ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA, written before any code because the row had none: (1) the seam offers NO WAY TO NAME AN AZ -- not a discouraged way, none -- so `Membership` declares no method taking an AZ, which a reflection test pins because absence is otherwise unfalsifiable; (2) `localAz()` answers the peers of THIS pod's AZ AS THE FLEET LISTS THEM, and NEED NOT contain `self` -- ownership is a podId comparison, never a record one. ⚠️ THIS CRITERION ORIGINALLY SAID "contains `self`" and the work falsified it: substituting self in made two pods disagree on 96 of 200 segments where the right answer is 0, so the criterion was wrong rather than the code. Corrected here rather than left for M5.20 to walk; (3) the same fleet handed to a pod in another AZ answers that AZ's peers, so the view follows identity rather than an argument; (4) M5.8's `AzPeers` guarantees stay -- a mixed view is still unrepresentable. ⚠️ WHY REMOVING THE QUERY IS THE WHOLE FIX: M5.8 left `inAz(String)` public and review MEASURED that an az-a caller could name az-b's peers 20 of 20 times, so the type prevented a MIXED view and nothing prevented the wrong one. SPEC.md's prefetch paragraph says the ring owner in EACH AZ fetches for that AZ, so no pod ever needs another AZ's membership and the query has no legitimate caller to keep it for | NFR-5 | **done** -- `inAz(String)` deleted; the seam is `self()` and `localAz()`. ⚠️ REVIEW CHECKED THE REMOVAL AGAINST M5.10-M5.19 AND THE SEQUENCER PATH and found no row needing another AZ's membership: the fetch ladder and prefetch both evaluate the local AZ, forwarding resolves its target from the lease body, and NFR-9's early challenge needs a fleet-wide EVENT that no version of this seam ever had. ⚠️ ONE THING THE SEAM STILL DOES NOT ANSWER, flagged for M5.16 rather than discovered there: "did MY AZ write this segment", which the writing-AZ-fetches-nothing rule needs -- nothing in `format/` carries a writer AZ, and `inAz` could not have supplied it either since it is the inverse lookup |
| M5.10 | ADR + `BinStore.presign` and `Capabilities.presignedUrls`, both backends, conformance. ⚠️ ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA, written before any code because the row had none: (1) a backend that cannot presign says so in `Capabilities` and is refused at STARTUP, not at first use -- the shape `requireConditionalWrites` establishes; (2) `presign` is scoped to ONE key with a caller-supplied TTL, per security.md rule 3; (3) a signed URL cannot reach a log, a trace or an error message BY CONSTRUCTION, per security.md rule 4 -- a returned `String` makes that a rule someone must remember, which non-negotiable 9 puts at the wrong rung; (4) the conformance suite every backend runs covers both answers, so a backend claiming the capability and throwing is caught. ⚠️ I FIRST CLAIMED THE WIRE-FORMAT OBLIGATION DID NOT APPLY AND THAT WAS WRONG. The skill's contracts table lists the **Store SPI** (`BinStore`, `Version`, `Capabilities`) as contract 5, with `07-pluggable-store-abstraction.md` as its document and "every backend" as its audience -- so the trigger fires on a Java interface BY DESIGN, and M5's SPEC assigns it to this very row. "Nothing crosses a process boundary" does not reach it. The obligations are applied: every reader updated in one commit (`CountingBinStore` was the one that mattered), fakes and `MemoryBinStore` updated, the conformance suite green on every backend, and research doc 07 -- which literally lists the old `BinStore` and `Capabilities` -- carries a banner, following the ADR-0022 precedent twelve lines above it. ⚠️ TWO ITEMS ARE N/A, AND FOR ONE REASON: an interface has no serialized shape, so there is no version to bump, no old bytes to keep parsing, and no round-trip property to test -- and the golden files in `format/src/test/resources/golden` are byte formats, which this is not. ⚠️ AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS ROW WAIVED ONLY THE GOLDEN FILES, which by its own word left the version bump applying -- in a row marked done, with no bump anywhere in the diff. M5's SPEC carries the same amendment (sdd.md rule 8). A signed URL first crosses a process boundary at M5.14 | FR-6 | ⚠️ **done** -- see the outcome notes in the Task cell |
| M5.11 | `FetchMode` and the ingester-side selection policy, threshold configurable. ⚠️ ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA, written before any code because the row had none: (1) `FetchMode` names exactly the three modes the contract document already defines -- `04-discovery-and-tailing.md`'s "Do the bytes cost a fetch?" table gives `inline`, `proxy` and `direct` -- so this row IMPLEMENTS contract 4 rather than changing it, and no banner or version bump is owed; the subscription's own shape changes at M5.14. (2) A CONSUMER CANNOT NAME A MODE, by construction and not by a documented rule: the policy's input type carries no consumer-supplied mode and no preference field, so there is nothing to ask with. FR-6 says the ingester chooses, and M5's SPEC names the falsifier -- "a policy that lets the consumer choose" (T0) -- and the failure mode, "`direct` becomes the default by accident". A consumer that could demand `direct` at fan-out 300 reproduces the $3,732/month design ADR-0004 rejected. Same rung-1 discipline as M5.9 deleting `inAz(String)`. (3) THE POLICY TOUCHES NO STORE, SOCKET OR CLOCK (non-negotiable 7): a pure function of its inputs, testable with no fixture. (4) `inline` IS THE DEFAULT AND STAYS IT, because it is the only mode under which a consumer issues no request at all -- a small same-AZ segment is `inline` at every fan-out. (5) THE CROSS-AZ INLINE CROSSOVER IS DERIVED, NOT HARD-CODED: cost model R12 defines it as (one GET) / ($ per GB) ≈ 19.5 KiB, so changing the backend's `CostTable.getPerThousand` MOVES the threshold, pinned by a test. ⚠️ M5's SPEC said it "reads it from `CostTable`" and that is only half available -- `CostTable` prices requests and knows nothing about moving a byte between AZs. The SPEC is amended (sdd.md rule 8) rather than worked around: the GET half comes from `CostTable`, the per-GB half is configuration -- DECIMAL GB, since that is what R12 divides by and what the bill uses; GiB would give ~21.0 KiB. ⚠️ `CostTable` is deliberately NOT extended -- it is part of `Capabilities`, which is `wire-format-change` contract 5 read by every backend, and cross-AZ egress is a property of the DEPLOYMENT rather than of the store, so every backend would be made to declare a number it does not know. (6) THE `direct` FAN-OUT THRESHOLD IS CONFIGURATION, NOT A CONSTANT -- measurement M3 is deferred to M9, the same discipline M4 applied to the lease TTL. (7) `direct` IS NEVER CHOSEN WHEN THE BACKEND CANNOT PRESIGN (`Capabilities.presignedUrls` is false). | M5.15b | SESSION RESUME (SPEC criterion 11's first half). ⚠️ Reconnecting with `(sessionId, sessionEpoch)` plus `add`/`remove` deltas yields the NEXT record -- **no duplicate and no skip**, which is the property, not "roughly where it was". ⚠️ IT NEEDS M5.15a FIRST: the handshake carries the SESSION epoch, and resuming on the sequencer's term would make every failover look like a lost session. ⚠️ AND IT NEEDS A SESSION REGISTRY the ingester side owns -- `SubscriptionHub` mints nothing today, which the M5.14 review noted needs no new seam, only a decision about where the identifier lives ⚠️ **DONE.** `SessionRegistry` is the ingester-side registry and `SubscriptionHub.Session` the handle: `openSession` registers ONE subscriber for every key -- so a session over K streams is one consumer to the serving path and takes the segment once (M5.40a, M5.62) -- and `resume(sessionEpoch, add, remove)` answers, per stream the session keeps, ONE PAST the last offset delivered. ⚠️ THE IDENTIFIER IS MINTED BY THE REGISTRY, which is the decision the row left open: a consumer-supplied id lets two consumers claim one session by copying a config, and the second resumes from the first's offsets -- a skip with no symptom. ⚠️ DELIVERED, NOT PUSHED, and that is where the skip would have lived: the recording happens after `complete`, which `SegmentServingPath` calls only for sinks that took every byte, so a consumer handed a truncated prefix does not advance. MEASURED -- recording at push time instead answers 13 where the case wants nothing, and the window is lost silently. ⚠️ A STREAM WITH NOTHING DELIVERED IS ABSENT FROM THE ANSWER, not zero: zero is a real offset and would point a consumer at the beginning of a run that may be long gone. ⚠️ THE EPOCH IS KIP-227's AND IS IDEMPOTENT: a resume at an epoch already served returns the SAME answer rather than recomputing it against newer state, and one behind it is refused -- the retry case publishes BETWEEN the answer and the retry, because re-applying key deltas is idempotent by itself and a fixture that only resent them passed with the epoch check deleted. ⚠️ FOUR CASES, EACH RED AGAINST THE DEFECT IT NAMES (`scripts/tdd-red.sh`). `./gradlew build` green at 1313 tests. ⚠️ ROUND 1's TEST PASS FOUND THE REGISTRATION REBUILD PINNED BY NOTHING: with either loop in `Session.resume` deleted -- the one that subscribes an added stream, the one that unsubscribes a removed one -- every case stayed green, because they all read the REGISTRY's bookkeeping and nothing published after a resume. `aResumesDELTASChangeWhatIsDELIVERED` publishes on both the added and the removed stream and asserts which arrives, which is the assertion bookkeeping cannot make. ⚠️ AND FOUR PRODUCTION MINORS WERE FIXED IN THE SAME ROUND, all correctness-shaped: the idempotence cache was keyed on the EPOCH alone, so a genuinely new request reusing one had its deltas silently dropped and the consumer waited forever -- it now compares the REQUEST and refuses a different one loudly; `open` overwrote the identity mapping instead of refusing a second session on one subscriber, which left the first recording nothing and resuming behind the truth; an added stream was registered AFTER the registry was told, so a publish in that window was dropped -- registration now happens first and is rolled back if the registry refuses; and `Session.resume` took the registry's monitor three times while mutating a plain list, so two concurrent resumes could double-register a key. ⚠️ THE COST CLAIM IS NOW ASSERTED rather than prose: every case built a `CountingBinStore` and read nothing off it, so `aSessionAndItsRESUMECostNOObjectStoreRequest` asserts the whole exchange -- open, deliver from held bytes, two resumes, close -- costs ZERO requests. ⚠️ ROUND 2 FOUND ALL FIVE OF ROUND 1's FIXES UNPINNED -- reverting each one left every ingest test green, which is the shape the harness has paid for repeatedly: a fix exists because review called the behaviour it prevents a defect, and a one-line revert brings that defect back with nothing to say so. Six cases now pin them: a DIFFERENT request at an already-served epoch is refused, a SECOND session on one subscriber is refused, an out-of-order delivery does not move a resume point backwards, a stream removed and re-added resumes as NEW rather than where it left off, a refused resume closes the registrations it opened early, and a key listed twice is registered once. ⚠️ TWO MORE PRODUCTION MINORS WENT WITH THEM: `Resumed` was a package-private type returned from a public method, so the transport M8 builds could not have named it -- it is now `SubscriptionHub.Resumed`; and neither `openSession` nor `resume` deduped its key list while the registry did, so `[C, C]` registered twice against one registry entry and `SegmentServingPath` put the run twice in one consumer's push list, which is the duplicate delivery this task exists to make impossible. ⚠️ WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO is carry the resume over a wire: there is no production `SubscriptionTransport` (M8), so what is built is the ingester half the transport will call | FR-6 | done |

Independent of M5.15b: a reset is what the SERVER says when it cannot honour a resume, so it can be built and tested against a stub resume ⚠️ **DONE.** `SessionResetException` is the signal and it is checked: a resume against a session the ingester no longer holds answers RE-SEND FULL STATE rather than refusing, because there is nothing left to apply a delta to and a refusal leaves the consumer retrying something nothing can honour. ⚠️ IT IS NOT A PROTOCOL ERROR, and both directions are pinned: an epoch behind what the session served, or different deltas at an epoch already served, leave the session INTACT and are refused with `IllegalStateException` -- answering a client defect with state loss would hide it as ordinary reconnection. ⚠️ THE NEGATIVE ASSERTION CRITERION 12 ASKS FOR IS HERE IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, as far as the hub can carry it: the exception names a SESSION and its message mentions no epoch, lease, leaseholder or term -- asserted, because an operator whose failover metric matched that text would count a failover every time a consumer reconnected after a restart -- and a sequencer failover, which reaches this hub as nothing but the next delta from a different pod, leaves the session's epoch untouched and its resume carrying offsets from BOTH sides of the change. ⚠️ A RESET IS A SIGNAL, NOT A DISCONNECT: the registrations are deliberately left in place, so a consumer keeps receiving while it re-establishes -- tearing them down would make a reset a silent gap as well -- and resetting one session leaves its neighbours alone, or one consumer's restart becomes a fleet-wide re-send. ⚠️ ROUND 1 FOUND THAT OBEYING THE SIGNAL PRODUCED A DUPLICATE, and it is the finding that shaped the answer: the only way a consumer could re-send full state was to open a NEW session, which leaves the old registrations in place -- `subscribe` does not dedupe -- so every run arrived TWICE, MEASURED; and closing the old session first opens a window registered for nothing, which is the gap the reset exists to avoid. `Session.reestablish(keys)` reconciles onto the handle the consumer already holds, added before removed, so a stream carried across is never touched. ⚠️ AND THE NEW CHECKED EXCEPTION BYPASSED M5.15b's ROLLBACK: `Session.resume` caught `RuntimeException` alone, so a reset interleaving with a resume left early registrations neither closed nor recorded -- unreachable even by `close()`, and therefore permanent duplicate delivery. ⚠️ THE REASON IS A CLOSED SET NOW, not free text: round 1 was right that "says nothing about the sequencer" was asserted rather than enforced, since any caller could have concatenated an epoch into a session signal. The orders are not symmetric, which is why one is right: subscribing first risks a push delivered-but-unrecorded, a possible duplicate, which is the half a consumer can act on. ⚠️ AND `reestablish` NOW REFUSES A SESSION THE REGISTRY STILL HOLDS: overwriting one discards its epoch and every resume point silently, and a consumer doing it has misread a refusal as a reset. ⚠️ ONE CLAIM IS WITHDRAWN RATHER THAN PINNED, which is the honest half of this row: the widened `catch (RuntimeException | SessionResetException)` in `Session.resume` is correct and is reachable only under a CONCURRENT reset -- `resume` calls `keysOf` first, so a session already gone throws before anything is registered early. A case written for it passed with the catch narrowed back, i.e. it tested nothing, and it was deleted rather than kept. Pinning it needs a registry that can throw from `resume` and not from `keysOf`, which means making the registry injectable -- a change to the seam, not to this row. `./gradlew build` green at 1330 tests. ⚠️ WHAT STAYS OPEN is the wire: no production `SubscriptionTransport` exists (M8), so the signal is thrown rather than framed, and M5.15d is what puts the sequencer epoch on the push site | FR-6 | done |
| M5.15d | WIRE THE SEQUENCER EPOCH TO THE PUSH SITE, which M5.14 scoped out with its reason. ⚠️ `CommitDelta` carries `(sequence, segments)` and no epoch; the epoch lives on the chain KEY (`LogKeys(prefix, epoch)`, package-private in `sequencer`), so `SubscriptionHub.publish` needs it from `DefaultIngest`, which needs a NEW ACCESSOR on the `Sequencer` seam. ⚠️ **AND THE TWO EPOCHS ARE ADJACENT SAME-TYPED `long`s IN THE CONSTRUCTOR** (`String session, long sequencerEpoch, long sessionEpoch, RunKey key, ...`), which round-3 review of M5.15a flagged as this row's hazard specifically: a transposition at any of the ten sites COMPILES, ENCODES, ROUND-TRIPS and passes every test in `format`, because every one of those tests constructs with its own literals in the declared order and none constrains a CALLER's argument order. ⚠️ The wire order differs from the declaration order anyway -- `sessionEpoch` is appended after `inline` -- so the adjacency buys nothing and costs this. Whoever wires the sites decides: a builder, two wrapper types, or a test that constructs from distinct values and asserts which field landed where. ⚠️ IT ALSO REWIRES TEN `Push`/`Delivery` CONSTRUCTION SITES across `ingest`, `client`, `plugin` and the cluster tests -- the "every reader, writer and fake in one commit" half that M5.14's criterion 4 satisfied only vacuously, as its round-3 review noted. ⚠️ UNTIL THIS LANDS the push channel carries neither identifier, so the roadmap's "push channel with session/epoch" is met by M5.14 + M5.15a + this together, not by any one of them ⚠️ **DONE.** `Sequencer.epoch()` is the new accessor: `LocalSequencer` already had one and now overrides it, `RemoteSequencer` reads the lease it forwards to, `FleetSequencer` asks the term it already holds and forwards otherwise, and both decorators pass through. `DefaultIngest` reads it AT FLUSH TIME and carries it on the pending push -- reading it in the pusher, which runs off the ingest lock and can lag, would label a push with whatever the chain had moved to by delivery rather than with the epoch its commit landed under. ⚠️ THE DEFAULT IS `EPOCH_UNKNOWN` (-1) AND NOT ZERO, because M4.4b reserves 0 for "no lease": a fake that models no chain must not be indistinguishable from a commit under the reserved chain. ⚠️ `FleetSequencer` DOES NOT ELECT TO ANSWER -- `Leadership.sequencer()` elects as a side effect, so reading a field would have acquired a lease, sealed an ancestor and replayed a chain; a new `heldWithoutElecting()` is what it reads. ⚠️ THE TRANSPOSITION HAZARD IS ANSWERED BY LAYOUT RATHER THAN BY A TEST where it could be: on `Push` and `Delivery` the epoch is not adjacent to any other `long`, so swapping it with `firstOffset` does not compile. `SubscriptionEvent`'s two epochs ARE still adjacent and are left so -- reordering its canonical constructor would rewrite thirty-eight call sites, a mechanical diff dwarfing the wiring this row is about -- and the row's sanctioned alternative is taken instead: `theOFFSETAndTheEPOCHAreDistinctFieldsWithDistinctValues` constructs from distinct values and asserts which field landed where. ⚠️ ROUND 1 FOUND `RemoteSequencer.epoch()` DOUBLING THIS FOLLOWER'S LEASE GET RATE -- it read the lease again, the same object `commitAll` had just decoded for the same flush, which cost.md R4 calls a read to coalesce rather than to repeat. It now REMEMBERS the epoch every commit already observed and issues no request at all, answering `EPOCH_UNKNOWN` before the first commit, which is honest: what a consumer is told is the epoch this pod's commits are actually routed by, and before the first there is no such thing. ⚠️ AND ROUND 1 FOUND THE CONSUMER HALF UNWIRED: a convenience constructor defaulted `Delivery.sequencerEpoch`, so all eight `Push`-to-`Delivery` bridges across `plugin`, the cluster tests and `client` silently dropped it -- a field with no producer, invisible to javac forever. Every bridge now carries `push.sequencerEpoch()`. ⚠️ AND THE WHOLE SEQUENCER HALF WAS UNTESTED, which the new file's own `AtEpoch` fake hid: `SequencerEpochTest` pins `LocalSequencer` answering its chain, `RemoteSequencer` answering what it forwarded to AND buying no request to do it, `FleetSequencer` answering its own term when it leads, that reading the epoch does NOT take a term -- `Leadership.sequencer()` elects as a side effect, so the reader uses a new `heldWithoutElecting()` -- and a fake answering `EPOCH_UNKNOWN` rather than the reserved 0. ⚠️ ROUND 2 FOUND THE PROXY AND DIRECT ARMS STILL UNCONSTRAINED, and an earlier draft of this row claimed otherwise: every case published an inline-sized payload, so both arms could pass the sentinel with the whole suite green -- and a segment large enough to go PROXY, the mode that path exists FOR, would have delivered every push labelled "no chain". `everyMODECarriesTheEpoch` is parameterized over both, publishing COLD so the bytes are not in hand, and asserts the arm it actually took before asserting the epoch. ⚠️ THE ORPHANED FOUR-ARGUMENT `deliver` IS GONE, which round 2 also found: it hard-coded the sentinel and had no caller left, so it was the next author's ready-made way to drop the epoch. ⚠️ TEN CASES, EACH RED AGAINST THE DEFECT IT NAMES. `./gradlew build` green at 1340 tests. ⚠️ AND IT PUT `DefaultIngest` BACK ON THE CAP: M5.69 split it to 652 lines and this wiring took it to 702, which `check-file-size` refused. The six-line comment explaining WHY the epoch is read at flush time rather than at push time is now two lines pointing here -- the trade M5.69's own row calls the answer that does not scale, taken knowingly rather than splitting the file a second time inside a commit about wiring. The next change to that file pays for a split | FR-6 | done |
| M5.16 | Prefetch on **durability**, per segment per AZ through the ring owner ⚠️ On DURABILITY, not on commit: prefetching is cache warming with no visibility implication, so it starts 20-350 ms earlier. The PUSH still waits for the commit -- that is I4 ⚠️ **DONE.** `SegmentPrefetcher.onDurable(segmentKey, writerAz)` is the decision, taken locally by every pod that is told: the writing AZ fetches nothing, and elsewhere only `PeerRing`'s owner for that segment fetches, once, into its `SegmentProxy`'s cache. ⚠️ THE NUMBER IS THE PROPERTY AND IT IS COUNTED: three AZs cost TWO GETs at 1, 3 and 30 pods per AZ -- the same case run at each -- because a rate that moved with the node count IS the fetch-on-demand design NFR-4 rules out. ⚠️ OWNERSHIP IS BY `podId`, NEVER BY `Peer` EQUALITY, and that needed its own case: every other fixture lists each pod's current address, so the two forms agree and the equality version stayed green. `aRESTARTEDPodStillOwnsItsShare` gives the fleet the pre-restart address -- the normal transient `Membership.localAz()` documents -- and dies under the equality form. ⚠️ FIVE REASONS NOT TO FETCH are each a normal state rather than an error, and each has a case: this AZ wrote it, this AZ has no ring (ADR-0012's ladder then ends at the object store, one extra GET at read time), this pod is not the owner, or the proxy holds no cache -- warming one that cannot hold anything is a GET bought for nothing. A repeated signal costs no further GET, which is what makes a retried broadcast harmless: five rounds over six pods still cost ONE -- and round-1 review measured that claim false for a segment the cache CANNOT ADMIT (capacity 4096, segment 8192: five signals, five GETs), because the dedupe was the cache hit rather than a decision. The trigger is M8's durability broadcast (M5.6e). Stated rather than implied: the mechanism and its cost property are here, the trigger is not. ⚠️ NINE CASES, EACH RED AGAINST THE DEFECT IT NAMES -- the ninth pins the BOUND on the remembered keys, which round-2 review measured as held by the tree and by no test: `removeEldestEntry` returning `false` left all eight green, because each used one key. `./gradlew build` green at 1349 tests | NFR-4 | done |

| M5.72 | **THE CHECKPOINT'S PODS MAP NOW GROWS AND NOTHING EVICTS, AND ONE COLLISION REMAINS UNDECIDABLE (opened by M5.55).** ⚠️ **THE COLLISION FIRST, because it can duplicate records.** Two incarnations of one pod can share a pointer -- `podb/i1` submits a flush, the pod dies, restarts as `i2`, and `i2` submits before the batcher drains; nothing partitions a batch by pod and only SEGMENT keys must be distinct, so both land in ONE delta and `applyOffsets` stamps both with the same `(epoch, sequence)`. M5.55 made the collapse DETERMINISTIC, which was the whole of what it could fix: review measured the pre-M5.55 tree duplicating on 4 of 4 runs for that input and the coin-toss draft on 5 of 8. ⚠️ **BUT NO FIELD RULES THE LIVE INCARNATION IN**: the pointer is equal by construction, ADR-0036 makes the id unordered on purpose -- in production a random UUID -- and the watermark separates the two only over the window recorded below, so the tie-break is stable but arbitrary. The live incarnation is the one that can still retry, and that is not knowable at inherit time. ⚠️ SO FOR THAT INPUT CLASS THE DUPLICATION IS NOW REPRODUCIBLE RATHER THAN RANDOM, which is a smaller claim than fixed and is why this row exists. ⚠️ **AND ONE OPTION WAS NOT TAKEN RATHER THAN BEING UNAVAILABLE**, which M5.55 first wrote as "undecidable" and review corrected: on a TIE the two slots are one pod's two incarnations in ONE delta, and the replacement's watermark begins at 0, so a `<`-on-watermark tie-break picks the live one over a WINDOW. ⚠️ THE WINDOW IS BOTH-SIDED, which a second review round had to correct after the first: it decides NOTHING in the minimal tie, where the dead incarnation's in-flight flush is its own first and both watermarks are 0, and it INVERTS once the replacement climbs past the dead watermark. So it trades a stable arbitrary answer for a heuristic that is right in the middle of that range and silent or wrong at both ends. Decide it here rather than rediscovering it. ⚠️ ALSO OPEN: `theFirstCheckpointAfterATakeoverCarriesNoPodsAtAll` still carries a METHOD NAME stating a system property M5.55 removed. A `@DisplayName` now carries the correction, because renaming makes `check-tdd` demand a red record for a test that is not new; a rename with `SKIP=check-tdd` and a stated reason is the M1.18 route if it becomes actively misleading. Before that row a successor's `CheckpointWriter` started EMPTY, and that truncation was the de-facto eviction -- wrong, because it dropped pods that were still live and duplicated their records on any rolling deploy, which is what M5.55 fixed. ⚠️ THE COST IT ACCEPTED IS STATED RATHER THAN HIDDEN: the map is now the union of every DISTINCT podId that has ever committed to this chain. Scale to 200 pods and back to 10 and all 200 slots ride in every checkpoint thereafter. ⚠️ IT IS FLEET-SHAPED ONLY WHERE POD NAMES ARE STABLE **AND REUSED**, and nothing in the code requires that: ADR-0031 assumes uniqueness among LIVE pods and its Consequences push naming reliability to another layer. `Checkpoint.PodState`'s own javadoc names the invariant at stake -- "the checkpoint would grow with history against ADR-0033". ⚠️ AND A SECOND OBLIGATION ARRIVED WITH IT, recorded in `Sequencer`'s **REFUSED** enumeration -- the pointer-names-a-gone-delta bullet, not the DUPLICATED list an earlier draft of this row pointed at: inherited `(epoch, sequence)` pointers now ride in every checkpoint and are re-propagated by each successor, so ADR-0036's requirement that a pointed delta still EXIST grows from one-term-old pointers to unbounded-age ones. Nothing deletes deltas today, so both are future work -- but they are M7's retention rule's inputs, not footnotes. ⚠️ THE FIX IS A DECISION, NOT A PATCH: evicting by age loses the protection M5.55 bought, and evicting by liveness needs a source of truth for which pods exist that the sequencer does not have. | — | ⚠️ **open** |

| M5.66 | A READER THAT CAN DECODE A RUN FROM A BOUNDED READ, OR THE COORDINATES COME OFF THE WIRE. ⚠️ ADR-0044 KEPT `SubscriptionEvent`'s `byteStart`/`byteLen` WITH NO READER CONSUMING THEM, priced at 5.4% of the subscription stream they travel on -- ADR-0044 derives that figure once -- and created this row so the question has an owner instead of being reopened a fourth time. ⚠️ **THE REASON THEY ARE KEPT IS ONE ROUND TRIP, NOT POSSIBILITY, AND AN EARLIER VERSION OF THIS CELL OPENED WITH THE REFUTED REASON**: `RunEntry` carries the same extents in the DIRECTORY, which a bounded reader must fetch first anyway for `codecFlags`, so the copies do NOT make the read possible. What they buy is that the slice GET can be issued WITHOUT waiting for the header, because `SegmentWriter` writes `byteStart` as an ABSOLUTE object offset. That is a latency argument in a project that trades latency for cost, which is exactly why this row exists -- and a cell opening with the refuted reason makes its own removal arm self-defeating, since `next-task` sends this row's owner here before the ADR. ADR-0042 REMOVED these fields, ADR-0043 RESTORED them, ADR-0043 then REOPENED itself, and ADR-0044 closed it by deferring to this row. ⚠️ **WHAT IS MISSING IS ONE READER ENTRY POINT, NOT A FORMAT CHANGE, AND AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS CELL OVERSTATED IT AS \"TWO THINGS IN THE SEGMENT LAYOUT FORBID IT\".** The bounded read is TWO EXPRESSIBLE REQUESTS: (1) the header range `[0, PREAMBLE + headerLen)`, which is exactly what `SegmentKey`'s `h<headerLen>` exists for and which `SegmentPublisher.headerRangeEndInclusive` computes in the tree TODAY, then (2) the run's slice `[byteStart, byteStart + byteLen)`, which the event already carries. ⚠️ **AND THE CODEC OBJECTION DISSOLVES WITH IT**: `u32 codecFlags` lives per run in the DIRECTORY and DATA carries no codec id, so a SLICE-ONLY GET cannot decompress what it fetched -- but a reader that fetched the header HAS the directory and therefore has the flags. That objection rules out the one-request form only. ⚠️ **WHAT GENUINELY BLOCKS IT** is that `SegmentReader.open` checks the FOOTER magic before the directory, so no prefix it will accept exists, and `[0, objectLen)` is not nameable because nothing the consumer holds carries the object length. So the work is a second `SegmentReader` entry point taking a directory and a slice SEPARATELY -- a new method, NOT a wire change -- which makes this row cheaper than its first framing and the coordinates more clearly worth their 5.4%. ⚠️ **AND THE REQUEST COUNT MOVES THE WRONG WAY**: directory-then-slice is TWO requests where a whole-object GET is one, so this row trades a request for bandwidth and must say at which node share that trade wins. ADR-0042 sizes a node at ~400 events of a ~1,600-run 8 MiB segment -- a QUARTER of it -- where one GET is plainly right; the case that wants a bounded read is the catch-up node holding ONE run of 1,600, fetching 8 MiB for 5.2 KiB, an amplification of ~1,600x. ⚠️ ACCEPTANCE: (1) the crossover is MEASURED AND THE NUMBER IS RECORDED IN ADR-0044 ITSELF, not asserted and not left in a commit body -- at what node share does two-requests-and-less-bandwidth beat one-request-and-more? ⚠️ **AND THE PRICE MODEL IS NAMED FIRST, BECAUSE THE ANSWER DEPENDS ENTIRELY ON WHICH BYTES ARE BEING PRICED.** `cost.md` and `CostTable` price REQUESTS only -- ⚠️ but the tree DOES hold a per-byte term, and an earlier version of this clause said it holds none: `FetchPolicyConfig.DEFAULT_CROSS_AZ_MICRO_DOLLARS_PER_GB = 20_000L` is R12's $0.02/GB and `derivedFrom` takes it as a parameter, in the very file this criterion names as the shape to copy. Read literally, the false version invited a SECOND constant for the same price in a second module. ⚠️ **THAT TERM PRICES CROSS-AZ TRAFFIC AND NOT THIS PATH**: the bounded read's extra request is an object-store GET, whose bytes are in-region free in any AZ, so `02-cost-model.md` says outright that only requests cost money here. So on dollars alone, in region, the bounded path NEVER wins -- it is +1 GET for $0 saved -- and a crossover of 0 selects the removal arm on a price nobody argued. ⚠️ **AND THE CROSS-AZ ARM IS WITHDRAWN, NOT OFFERED**: ADR-0044 withdrew it and an earlier version of this cell kept it a clause later, while pricing every figure below on that same withdrawn rate -- 19,968 bytes per GET IS R12's cross-AZ exchange rate. R12 prices EC2 cross-AZ transfer on the peer mesh, which M5.9 makes unaddressable by construction; the bounded read's extra request is an object-store GET whose bytes are free in any AZ. **The only quantity left to trade is TIME**, which is not money and is what this project trades AWAY, so the row owes a LATENCY derivation and inherits the figures below as byte-and-request counts rather than as a dollar crossover. ⚠️ **AND REVIEW ALREADY DID THE ARITHMETIC, WHICH THIS ROW INHERITS RATHER THAN REDOES**: `DIRECTORY_ENTRY_BYTES = 49`, so a 1,600-run segment's header range is 78,400 bytes; with K runs spread through a `(indexId, partitionId)`-sorted segment the union spans about `objLen*(K-1)/(K+1)`, so the bounded path saves `2*objLen/(K+1) - 78,400` against one extra GET (19,968 bytes at R12). Positive to **K ~ 169**, and on bandwidth alone to **K ~ 213** -- both well below ADR-0042's ~400 share, which is why criteria 1 and 2 no longer red each other. ⚠️ **AND THE ~400 SHARE IS NOT A CROSSOVER ARTIFACT**: review measured that fixing the bounded path at two requests makes its extra cost one GET at every K, which on R12's byte-to-GET conversion with a TIGHT union wins up to K ~ 1,556 -- ⚠️ an earlier version attributed that figure to a request-only price, under which the bounded path loses at every K and the crossover is 0 and would RED criterion 2's whole-object mandate. The reason whole-object is right at ~400 is physical, not economic -- `SegmentWriter` sorts runs by `(indexId, partitionId)`, so a node's share is INTERLEAVED across the segment and the union of ~400 of 1,600 runs spans most of the 8 MiB. Two requests for nearly the whole object loses to one. ⚠️ **SO THE FIXTURE PLACES THE K RUNS AS PRODUCTION DOES, INTERLEAVED**: consecutive runs make the union tight and the case assert nothing. ⚠️ **AND THE CODE'S THRESHOLD IS DERIVED FROM THE RECORDED NUMBER, NOT MERELY COMPARED TO IT** -- review named the gap: record a crossover of K = 40, branch at K <= 1, and both tested shares stay green while the measurement binds nothing. ⚠️ **AND THE BOUNDARY IS ASSERTED, NOT ONLY THE TWO ENDS**: two shares pin a threshold anywhere in [1, 400), so the cases are at K = crossover and K = crossover + 1, which is the only pair that moves when the recorded number moves. The shape to copy is `FetchPolicyConfig`'s `derivedFrom`, which exists so a price change MOVES the threshold rather than stranding it; ⚠️ **THE PRECEDENT IS R12 OF THE COST MODEL** (`docs/research/00-problem/02-cost-model.md`), which derives the ~19.5 KiB cross-AZ crossover as (one GET) / ($ per GB) -- ⚠️ **NOT `cost.md` R12, WHICH IS THE COST GOVERNOR**: an earlier draft of this row cited it that way, and M5's own SPEC records the identical mis-citation being caught before, because it sends a reader to the wrong rule entirely. ⚠️ **AND `CostTable` HOLDS NO PER-BYTE PRICE**, which the same SPEC line records and which this criterion cannot assume: it prices REQUESTS, so the bandwidth half of the crossover comes from somewhere this row must name; (2) EITHER a reader that decodes a run from a bounded read, with the codec **read from the fetched directory and ACTED ON, asserted against a segment whose directory carries a NON-`CODEC_NONE` flag** -- ⚠️ **AND \"ACTED ON\" MEANS READ-THE-FLAG-AND-REFUSE, BECAUSE `format` CANNOT HOLD A DECOMPRESSOR**: `check-io-seam` bans `java.util.zip` outright and its carve-out is checksums only, and no compression dependency exists in the tree, so an owner reading \"honoured\" as owing a codec would be owed a module instead. ⚠️ The fixture IS buildable today and review confirmed how: not through `SegmentWriter`, which writes `CODEC_NONE` unconditionally with no override, but as `GoldenSegmentV1Test` already does -- patch the directory entry, recompute the header CRC, reopen, read it back through `RunEntry.codecAndFlags()` -- ⚠️ review measured that \"reachable\" costs nothing today: `SegmentWriter` writes `CODEC_NONE` unconditionally and `SegmentReader.read` never consults `codecAndFlags`, so `decodeRun(slice, byteStart, byteLen, recordCount)` with NO directory fetch at all -- `recordCount` is on the event -- passes every other clause at one request and fewer bytes, and writing a non-zero flag into a directory entry reds nothing. The flag is what makes the directory fetch necessary rather than decorative, and reachable from what was fetched, AND a consumer that uses the event's coordinates to drive it, ⚠️ **AND FEWER BYTES ACTUALLY FETCHED, WHICH IS THE ONLY HALF THAT PAYS** -- review named the implementation that satisfies every other word: `decodeRun(byte[] wholeSegment, long byteStart, long byteLen)` that opens the whole array and slices it. Bounded read, codec reachable, coordinates driving it, the SAME whole-object GET, and zero bytes saved -- after which criterion 3 would \"correct\" the javadoc to say a reader consumes them and the field would look earned. So the assertion is on BYTES FETCHED **AND ON REQUESTS MADE, BOTH, AT TWO SHARES WITH A STATED RELATION AT EACH** -- **THE CRITERION IS AN INVARIANT OVER ALL K, NOT A LIST OF SAMPLED SHARES, AND THAT IS THE LESSON OF NINE BREAKS.** Every earlier wording enumerated shares, and every round found an implementation passing the samples and failing between them -- the last two by arithmetic on this cell's own figures. So, as properties: **(i) wherever the bounded path is taken it issues EXACTLY TWO requests, at every K, independent of the run layout**; **(ii) the bytes it fetches are at most the header range plus the SPAN of the K extents -- `max(byteStart + byteLen) - min(byteStart)` -- plus a stated constant**; **(iii) which path runs at a given K is a pure function of K and the recorded crossover, with no other input**. ⚠️ **(ii) IS THE SPAN AND NOT THE SUM, and an earlier wording said sum while the same sentence mandated fetching the span**: at K = 169 the span is 8,289,919 bytes against a sum of 886,067, so a sum-based bound either reds the very reader this row mandates or has to be set at 93% of the object, where it is 2.1% tighter than the \"strictly fewer\" it exists to strengthen and the open-ended `bytes=byteStart-` survivor walks back in. ⚠️ **AND THE LOW CASE GOES AT THE SMALLEST BOUNDED SHARE, NOT AT K = 64**: the interleave gap is `objLen/K`, so it is LARGEST at smallest K and K = 64 is the MILDEST point in `[1, crossover)`, not the sharpest. Review measured `coalesce(ranges, 256 KiB)` merging at K = 64 (gap 131,072) and shattering at K = 31 (gap 270,600) into **32 requests against 1** -- runs-per-node, switched off just below the lowest asserted share, exactly as the 64 KiB coalescer did one round earlier. Invariant (i) kills both without knowing the gap limit. ⚠️ **THE SAMPLED CASES ARE MINIMUM EVIDENCE FOR THE INVARIANTS, NOT THE SPECIFICATION**: **K = 1**, K = 2, K = 64, K = crossover, K = crossover + 1 and ~400 -- ⚠️ **K = 1 IS THERE BECAUSE AN EARLIER EVIDENCE SET STARTED AT K = 2 AND REVIEW WALKED THROUGH THE GAP**: `K > 1 && K <= crossover` satisfies (i), (ii) and (iii) LITERALLY -- a pure function of K and the crossover, two requests wherever taken -- and greens all five samples while fetching 8 MiB for a single 5,243-byte run, the ~1,600x amplification this row opens on as its motivation. It is not contrived: a one-run node has nothing to share, so it never enters M5.45h's node-scoped owner and keeps the legacy whole-object GET. ⚠️ **AND K = 2 IS NOT \"the smallest bounded share\"** under a `K <= crossover` threshold, which an earlier version called it. ⚠️ **THE K = 2 CASE PLACES ITS RUNS AT THE SEGMENT'S EXTREMES**, because (i)'s \"independent of the run layout\" otherwise has no layout-varying case: `coalesce(ranges, 4 MiB)` passes every share and still issues three requests for a two-run node holding the first and last run. ⚠️ **AND (iii) NEEDS A SECOND FIXTURE AT THE SAME K, NOT ANOTHER K**: every sample varies only K against ONE fixture, so `sum(byteLen) <= crossover * AVG_RUN_BYTES` is green at all of them while the path is really a function of the EXTENTS -- at 10,486-byte runs that switch lands at K = 84 and the recorded crossover governs nothing. Same K, different run sizes, same path is what makes (iii) falsifiable -- ⚠️ **AND THE TWO Ks ARE NAMED, BECAUSE AN EARLIER VERSION OF THIS CLAUSE GAVE THE FIXTURE A SHAPE AND NO K AND TEN OF THE TWELVE COMPLIANT CHOICES ARE VACUOUS**: on this cell's own figures the `sum(byteLen)` proxy AGREES with the K-based path at K = 1, 2 and 64 under doubled runs and at K = 1, 2, 64 and 169 under halved runs. The only two that red are **K = crossover with DOUBLED runs** (1,772,134 > 886,067) and **K = crossover + 1 with HALVED runs** (445,570 <= 886,067) -- the pair round 11 named and the repair dropped. That is structural rather than a fact about one proxy: anything monotone in K disagrees with K only in a band adjacent to the boundary, so the falsifying cases are always at the boundary -- AND THE BOUNDARY PAIR IS TWO-SIDED**: at K = crossover, **exactly TWO requests and fewer bytes** than the whole-object GET; at K = crossover + 1, **exactly ONE request and exactly the whole-object bytes**. ⚠️ **AND A CASE AT K = 64 SITS BELOW THE SWITCH, BECAUSE MOVING THE LOW SIDE OF THE PAIR UP FROM K = 1 REMOVED THE ONLY ASSERTION SITE THERE -- THIS ROUND'S OWN EDIT BROKE THAT.** Review measured the survivor, and it is the natural implementation rather than a contrived one: `coalesce(ranges, GAP_LIMIT)` at a conventional 64 KiB gap, which is literally what doc 10 §7 prescribes. At K = 169 the interleave gap is ~49,650 bytes, everything merges, two requests, green; at K = 170 and ~400, whole-object, green -- while **at K = 64 the gap is ~131,072 bytes, nothing merges, and it issues 65 requests against 1**, runs-per-node, unasserted because every enumerated case sat at or above the crossover. ⚠️ **AND THAT REGION COLLIDES WITH M5.45h, WHICH LANDS FIRST**: its criterion 1 asserts ONE invocation at K = 64 against a fake counting INVOCATIONS, and every crossover this row records (169, 213, 1,556) is above 64, so the bounded path taken there is TWO invocations of the widened seam. ⚠️ **THE RESOLUTION IS NOT TO LOOSEN M5.45h's ASSERTION** -- that would be a weakening in another row's test, which non-negotiable 2 and testing.md rule 5 forbid and `check-test-integrity` cannot see across rows. M5.45h's \"one fetch\" is about the SHARING axis -- one fetch operation per (node, segment) rather than one per client -- and this row's \"at most two requests\" is about the SHAPE of that operation. The two are orthogonal and M5.45h's wording conflates them, so **THE DISTINCTION IS WRITTEN INTO M5.45h's OWN CRITERION 1 BY THE COMMIT THAT CREATES THIS ROW**, not owed by this row later -- review measured why an earlier draft's "this row owes M5.45h the distinction before M5.45h is taken (it is `todo`, so this is a criteria edit)" is self-defeating: this row DEPENDS on M5.45h, so its owner arrives only once M5.45h is DONE, at which point the escape that sentence offers is closed and the only exits left are the two it forbids. M5.45h now asserts its fetch count as **at most one `fetchDirectory` and at most one `fetchRange` invocation per (node, segment)** -- an equality rather than the literal 1 and rather than a constancy claim, since constancy is not decidable from the single K = 64 sample that cell carries. This row still owes the cases naming which path runs at each asserted share. ⚠️ **A ONE-SIDED PAIR BINDS NOTHING, AND AN EARLIER VERSION OF THIS CLAUSE WAS ONE**: \"at most two requests\" is satisfied by the whole-object path at ONE, so recording the crossover at 169 and branching at `nodeRunCount <= 1` greens K = 1 on the bounded path and K = 169, 170 and ~400 on whole-object reporting 1 <= 2 -- verbatim the defect criterion 1 says the pair exists to close. The repair costs no extra case, only a relation at each side. ⚠️ **REVIEW BROKE THE THREE EARLIER WORDINGS OF THIS CLAUSE AND THE FOURTH THE SAME WAY**: none of them stated the bounded path's request count AS A FUNCTION OF K, so \"header GET plus one ranged GET PER RUN below the crossover\" passed every one of them. On this row's own cited price -- R12, one GET against $0.02/GB -- an honest measurement of that implementation records a crossover near 331, so a node holding 330 runs issues **331 requests against 1**: the same runs-per-node scaling the clause exists to kill, switched off just below the one share anyone asserted. Whether K slices are UNIONED or issued separately is the whole question, and it is now stated rather than presupposed. ⚠️ **AND THE ROUND TRIP IS ASSERTED DIRECTLY, BECAUSE IT IS THE ONLY GROUND ADR-0044 KEEPS THE FIELDS ON AND BYTES AND REQUESTS ARE BOTH BLIND TO IT.** Review measured the mutation INSIDE the intended implementation: replace `event.byteStart()` with `reader.find(event.key()).byteStart()` and go header -> await -> parse -> slice. Two requests at every K, byte-identical, codec still refused, nothing reds -- and the fields have bought NOTHING, which is the whole question this row exists to answer. So: **THE SECOND REQUEST, WHICHEVER IT IS, IS ISSUED BEFORE THE FIRST RESPONSE IS DELIVERED**, asserted at the same seam the counts are taken at. ⚠️ **NOT \"the slice before the header response\", WHICH AN EARLIER VERSION SAID AND REVIEW GREENED TRIVIALLY**: fetch the SLICE first and the header second -- slice, await, header, await, parse, refuse the codec, decode -- and the header response is simply the last of four events, so the clause holds while the reader takes TWO sequential round trips and the coordinates buy nothing. The symmetric predicate is false for both sequential orders and true only for the concurrent reader ADR-0044 prices at one saved RTT. Without that clause this row cannot answer its own question, and the clause that would have caught it -- \"a consumer that uses the event's coordinates to drive it\" -- has no assertion form. ⚠️ **AND BYTES ARE BOUNDED ABOVE, NOT ONLY \"STRICTLY FEWER\"** -- by invariant (ii), which is the SPAN; ⚠️ an earlier version of this sentence restated the bound as the SUM and survived two thousand characters past (ii) correcting it, a 7,403,852-byte difference at K = 169 that would have redded the union slice this criterion mandates and left one ranged GET per run as the only admissible shape. The sum clause is deleted rather than annotated; what this sentence still carries on its own is the shape it was written for: an open-ended `bytes=byteStart-` needs no object length, is expressible, passes \"strictly fewer\" at one run of 1,600, and fetches about half the segment. At ADR-0042's ~400-of-1,600 node share the bounded path must NOT be taken at all: **exactly the whole-object GET, one request, no more bytes.** ⚠️ **AN EARLIER DRAFT SAID \"NO MORE REQUESTS\" AT BOTH SHARES, WHICH IS <= 1 AND EXCLUDES THE VERY DESIGN THIS ROW EXISTS TO AUTHORISE** -- header-then-slice is TWO, as this cell says twice. Review measured what survived instead: the removal arm, chosen on a wording artifact, or a prefix GET the ADR lists as refused. A criterion that reds the intended implementation is worse than a loose one, because it looks strict. ⚠️ Naming two shares without a relation is satisfied by `isPositive()`, which an earlier draft of this clause was -- counted at **M5.45g's byte source** -- ⚠️ **AND THE COUNT IS OF EVERY REQUEST THE PATH MAKES, NOT OF ONE METHOD ON IT**, because review measured the difference: M5.45g's seam takes a `Grant` and yields a segment's bytes with no range, so this row must WIDEN it, and a widened seam with `fetchRange` and `fetchDirectory` beside each other lets a fake count `fetchRange` and miss the directory fetch entirely. Production then runs that directory fetch once per DELIVERY -- per-node residency is M5.45h's and this row's `format` half has no dependency, so it may land first -- and the same 401-against-1 reappears with 1 counted. The fake counts the seam, the seam is asserted to be the ONLY path from `ConsumerClient` to bytes, and the assertion is on the TOTAL. ⚠️ It is the only seam the consumer path can contain (ADR-0023 bars `client` from `binjava.binstore`, and `GrantIssuer`'s javadoc records that consumer-side GETs are invisible to `CountingBinStore` -- so \"the store seam\", which an earlier draft of this criterion named, is not reachable from here and would let the bytes arm and the consumer arm be two different tests). ⚠️ **BECAUSE BYTES ALONE LEAVES THE REQUEST COUNT OPEN AT EVERY SHARE**, which review measured: a directory GET plus ONE RANGED GET PER RUN, always on, satisfies a bytes assertion taken at the favourable share -- one run of 1,600 -- and is **401 requests against 1** at ADR-0042's ~400-run node. That is runs-per-node, which non-negotiable 6 forbids BY NAME, and this row's own \"two requests versus one\" framing is true only at K = 1. M5.45h's K = 64 case does not backstop it: this row's `format` half has no dependency and may land first -- OR the fields come off the wire, which is a format change under non-negotiable 8 and updates the format, every reader, every writer, the fakes, the six golden files and ADRs 0042/0043/0044 in ONE commit; (3) whichever way it goes, **EVERY SITE IN ADR-0044's SWEEP TABLE IS CORRECTED OR RE-CONFIRMED, AND THE TABLE IS THE ENUMERATION** -- ⚠️ an earlier draft of this criterion listed \"FOUR sites\" and was ALREADY STALE when it was written: review counted seven, three of them added by the same commit. A criterion that copies a list cannot survive the next edit to that list, which is rung 7 where rung 4 is available; pointing at the table is the rung-4 form. ⚠️ **AND THE BANNERS ARE THE PART THAT MATTERS**: AGENTS.md says a revision banner WINS over the prose it corrects, so a banner still asserting the retired claim is worse than uncorrected prose -- which is exactly what round 1 of M5.45f blocked on; ⚠️ **(3b) THE EVENT'S EXTENT IS CHECKED AGAINST THE DIRECTORY'S, AND A DISAGREEMENT REFUSES RATHER THAN DECODES.** ADR-0044 states the rule -- the event's coordinates are a HINT that buys concurrency, the segment's own `RunEntry` is the TRUTH -- and says \"M5.66 carries the assertion\"; review measured that an earlier version of this cell carried no such criterion, which is the half-landed fix this task has now produced six times. ⚠️ **IT IS A CORRECTNESS RULE, NOT HYGIENE**: criterion 2 MANDATES the parallel design, so the slice is fetched on the event's copy while the directory carries its own. A publisher off-by-one or a replayed v3 event then decodes from the wrong offset and hands a NEIGHBOURING run's records to the shard, with the offset advancing, under a valid signature -- ⚠️ **AND IT FAILS SILENTLY RATHER THAN LOUDLY**, which is what makes the case necessary: `SegmentWriter.encodeRun` length-prefixes and CRC32Cs EACH run block, so a drifted `byteStart` landing on a DIFFERENT run's block passes that block's own CRC and decodes cleanly -- ADR-0032's silent data error one layer out, and invisible to every other criterion here because the bytes, the requests and the round trip are all correct. The case feeds an event whose `byteStart` disagrees with the `RunEntry` the same reader fetched and asserts a refusal; ⚠️ **(4) AND security.md RULE 3's RANGE HALF IS ANSWERED HERE, BECAUSE THIS ROW IS WHERE THREE PLACES SEND IT** -- ADR-0044's Decision (c), doc 04 §2c's banner and that same test javadoc all hand it to M5.66, and an earlier draft of this cell named neither the rule nor the standard. Review measured the consequence: this row takes its second arm, removes the fields, corrects the two sites criterion 3 used to enumerate, marks itself done -- and rule 3's range half is closed by nobody, with a `format` test javadoc still pointing at a dead handoff. That is the IDENTICAL mechanism review already measured losing this exact clause when ADR-0043 handed it to M5.45b. If the bounded read lands, a range becomes expressible and the grant is scoped to it where the backend can sign one; if the fields come off, the clause is closed by DECLINING it with the reason. ⚠️ **AND security.md ITSELF GAINS THE POINTER, because \"updated or cited\" describes the state that ALREADY EXISTS**: four places point AT this row and rule 3 points at nothing, so a reader of the standard cannot learn the clause is unmet or who holds it. The standard is edited, not merely cited from here | NFR-4 | todo -- **depends on M5.45g and M5.45h**, which builds the byte source criterion 2 counts at. ⚠️ An earlier version of this cell said "no dependency; it is a `format` question, not a `client` one", and review measured the contradiction: criterion 2 counts "at M5.45g's byte source", says this row must WIDEN it, and requires that seam be the ONLY path from `ConsumerClient` to bytes -- while `client` holds no byte source at all today (`ConsumerClient` opens `delivery.segment()`; the package is `ConsumerClient`, `ConsumerRecord`, `DefaultEnvelope`, `Delivery`, `SubscriptionTransport`). Taken first, the owner discharges criterion 2 against a local harness production does not use -- the exact shape this row warns about elsewhere. ⚠️ **AND ON M5.45h, WHOSE MECHANISM EVERY REQUEST COUNT ABOVE K = 1 PRESUPPOSES.** A node's K runs are K distinct `RunKey`s and `ConsumerClient` takes exactly ONE, so "one union slice" at K = 169 and "exactly the whole-object GET, one request" at ~400 both need ONE fetch SHARED ACROSS 169 or 400 clients -- M5.45h's criterion 1 by name, which itself depends on M5.62. ⚠️ An earlier version of this cell declined that dependency on the WRONG GROUND: whether M5.45h's ASSERTION covers this row, when the question is whether its MECHANISM is needed. It is. Without it the cheap discharge is calling the widened seam once with K ranges from a local driver, which reports 2 where production reports 170 and 1 where production reports 400. ⚠️ The `format` half -- the `SegmentReader` entry point -- is genuinely independent of both and may be written first; what cannot be is the measurement |
| M5.67 | AN ACK EVENT WITH THE WRONG SEQUENCE BLINDS `checkAckOrder` WITHOUT MOVING ANY FLOOR. ⚠️ M5.50 split the `ackEvents` floor so a trace of pure CONFIRMED events cannot report nothing forever -- that closes blinding by KIND. Review then measured the same family reached through CONTENT: replace the sequence argument at all three `AckOrderInvariants.AckEvent.acked(...)` sites in `CommitProtocolSimulation` (lines 407, 571, 598) with `0L` and the full 1,000-seed sweep is **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**. Events stay ack-kind and stay 11,110, so M5.50's new floor is green at 11110 >= 3000; the mixture floor is green; and `ackFloorAlwaysZero` is green too, because `lowestAckedSequenceIn` filters by EPOCH only and the slot-0 CONTINUE already puts a 0 in every chain. Every ack then names its own chain's floor, which is confirmed by construction and never above the watermark, so both `found.add` sites are unreachable and `failing` is empty. ⚠️ **WRONG-EPOCH IS NOT IN THIS FAMILY** and needs no row: an ack landing in an epoch with no confirmations reds loudly through the violation list. ⚠️ **AND A BIGGER COUNT CANNOT FIX IT** -- the count is already right. The shape that kills it is M5.28/M5.29's: a RELATION the blinded state cannot satisfy. ⚠️ **AND THE PRODUCTION ROLE FOUND THE SAME SHAPE BY A SECOND ROUTE**, which is why this row is one row and not two: `ackKindEvents` is the SAME POPULATION as `commits` -- equal on all 1,000 seeds, and structurally so, since every `acks.add(...acked(...))` sits adjacent to a `commits++` or `zombieWrites++` -- and `commits` already has a floor at the same threshold twenty lines up. So M5.50's floor adds exactly one bit, that the emission exists at all, and TOLERATES DROPPING 73% OF IT: guarding the site at line 598 with `if (acked.sequence() % 3 != 0)` takes the count to ~7,400, still above 3,000, leaves `commits` untouched, blinds `checkAckOrder` to a third of the acknowledgements, and the suite is green. ⚠️ The relation that kills BOTH routes is the same: **`ackKindEvents == commits + zombieWrites`**, which holds by construction at all three sites, survives a later switch to `runForwarding`, and is M5.28's `refusalsSeen < gracefulReleases` shape. ⚠️ ACCEPTANCE: (1) the `0L` mutation above reds the sweep; (2) the assertion is a relation rather than a floor -- ack sequences matched against what the commits `Result` already carries, or more than one DISTINCT ack sequence per epoch, and the row says which and why; (3) it does not duplicate M5.50's floor, which stays | NFR-1 | todo -- found while reviewing M5.50, and outside what that row claims |

Landed as a rule in AGENTS.md § Never. ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT scripted and not scriptable**: it governs what an agent SAYS, which leaves no artifact in the tree to gate. Recorded here so the rule has a task, not so a gate is implied | — | done |
| M0.47 | Dispatch reviewers by context and cap the review rounds. ⚠️ Three parts, all written and tested, none in the tree: `review-roles.sh` derives the required roles from the staged paths (docs-only → `reviewer` alone; anything executable → both), because `check-reviewed.sh:16` hardcodes `for ROLE in reviewer test-reviewer` and a test-reviewer handed a diff with no test can only file a vacuous verdict — and downstream, "pass over a diff with nothing to test" and "pass over a diff nobody read" are THE SAME BITS. `review_rounds.py` counts distinct reviewed hashes per task and fails past review.md rule 12's cap of two. `review_delta.py` emits the prior round's open findings so a verify round gets the delta instead of the whole diff. ⚠️ Derived, never declared: this LOOSENS a gate, so the author must not be able to claim "docs-only". ⚠️ Measured justification, and it is this repository's own record: 8 of 12 tasks exceeded the two-round cap, M0.13 at **eleven**, where rounds 9-11 fixed `minor` findings that `check-reviewed.sh:41` never blocked on. ⚠️ Beyond the held scripts, `review.sh context` must PRINT the round number and the severity contract — rule 11 exists and was violated eight times because it lives in a skill body read at task start, while the verdict arrives many turns later saying only "minor" | — | done |
| M0.48 | Delete the `Reviewed-by:` trailer convention (git.md rule 6). ⚠️ It was never a rule — an agent invented it and repeated it on nine commits as the constant `Reviewed-by: reviewer, test-reviewer`. No script reads it, no standard required it, and until M0.47 it could only restate what `check-reviewed.sh` enforced unconditionally. ⚠️ Self-asserted, so it can be FALSE: one commit was drafted carrying it while the production reviewer had returned `changes-requested` and recorded no verdict at all. ⚠️ A SKIPPED review still must be stated in the body, because there it is the only trace. ⚠️ The alternative — deriving the trailer from `.harness/review/` in `check-commit-msg.sh` — was considered and rejected by the user in favour of deletion | — | done |
| M0.49 | `tdd_scan.py` must scope the Gradle task to the owning module, and the rule must be a pure transform. ⚠️ `plan()` emitted the bare root task `test` for every source outside buildSrc, so `./gradlew test --tests <one class>` ran `test` in all eight modules at `org.gradle.parallel=true`. A module with no test source is NO-SOURCE and skipped — **not** failed, as a first draft of this row claimed and review disproved by running it — but a module that HAS test sources and no match fails the build, and whether the owning module wrote its JUnit XML first is a **scheduling race**. Measured: `tdd-red.sh` recorded 2 of 7 M1.0 ids, then 7 of 7 once scoped. ⚠️ `task_for()` is extracted as a PURE string transform because the rule was reachable only through a working-tree glob: the first tests named a class that was not in the tree, so `plan()` took its "cannot locate" path and both tests asserted against an ERROR message — identically before and after the fix, so the red record proved nothing and deleting the fix survived (testing.md rule 1, non-negotiable 7) | — | done |
| M0.50 | The round cap cannot prevent the round it caps. ⚠️ `check-reviewed.sh` checks verdict EXISTENCE first and `finish`es on failure, so the cap at round 3 is never reached until verdicts for round 3 already exist — it refuses the commit only after the review has been paid for in full. The refusal belongs in `review.sh context`, which must decline to build a packet past the cap and say "split it, or argue the finding". ⚠️ Found on M0.49, whose round-2 MAJOR could not be fixed without paying for a round 3 that the gate would then reject | — | todo |

Found on M0.49, where both remedies rule 12 offers — argue, or split — cost a round | — | todo |
