## M1 — walking skeleton

Decomposed in [milestones/M1/SPEC.md](milestones/M1/SPEC.md). ⚠️ The M0 rows
above are NOT all done: 25 remain todo. M1 proceeds because its blockers are
the harness gates that already exist, not the ones still queued.

| ID | Task | Serves | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1.0 | ⚠️ **SPI ONLY — the trust domain is NOT yet threaded through the write path**, because no write path exists before M1.6; that clause is carried by M1.6/M1.8. `CredentialSource` SPI + `StaticCredentialSource` + `Principal`; trust domain threaded through the write path ([ADR-0021](decisions/0021-credential-source-spi.md)) | FR-20 | done |
| M1.1 | `BinStore` SPI + `MemoryBinStore` + conformance harness. ⚠️ M1 subset: no `putIfMatch`, no multipart — M1 excludes leases/epochs/seal (ADR-0002 is M4) and multipart arrives with the real segment format in M2. Both are ADDITIONS; no M1 signature moves for them | FR-8 | done |
| M1.1b | ⚠️ **Blocks M1.2.** `Body.checkedStream()` verifies only at EOF or on close, so an OVER-LONG body passes silently: a content-length-driven backend reads exactly `length` bytes and never reaches EOF, and S3 then stores a truncated object that reads back as a valid short segment — the exact failure `readFully`'s javadoc names. `readFully` catches both directions; `checkedStream` catches one, so its javadoc ("REFUSES a body whose length disagrees") is overstated. Fix: at `seen == length`, attempt one more read. ⚠️ Three more untested shapes in the same method, all verified surviving: the single-arg `read()` override is never executed (both tests use `readAllBytes`, so an honest body read byte-wise counts 0 and is rejected as short); an honest reader that stops exactly at `length` without seeing EOF — the ordinary S3 path — is unconstrained; and `close()` closing the delegate when `verify()` throws is asserted by nothing, though the comment claims it. ⚠️ Latent only because `checkedStream` has no production caller yet | FR-8 | done |
| M1.1c | ⚠️ **Blocks M1.2.** `Body.checkedStream()` inherits `mark`/`reset`/`skip` without adjusting `seen`. An HTTP client that marks, streams, then resets to retry a 500 sees `seen == 2 × length` at close and fails a RETRYABLE upload permanently; `skip` undercounts and refuses an honest body | FR-8 | done |
| M1.1d | `MemoryBinStore.list(prefix, startAfter, 0)` throws `IndexOutOfBoundsException` from a method contracted on `IOException` — `page.get(-1)` when `maxKeys <= 0`. ⚠️ M1.3b's LIST ceiling reaches exactly this when its budget hits zero. Also: the key-limit message prints `key.length()` characters while the check measures UTF-8 bytes | FR-8 | todo |
| M1.1g | `Body.checkedStream()` leftovers from M1.1b/c review. ⚠️ Its headline finding is FIXED here, not deferred: the over-long test used `readAllBytes`, which drives to EOF, so the lazy design threw from inside the same call and the case could not tell eager from lazy — review restored the pre-fix design with the suite green. It now over-reads WITHOUT reaching EOF. Remaining, none blocking: (a) `skip()` counts the delegate's return value, which cannot distinguish a real skip from `FileInputStream.skip` reporting past end-of-file — so a short body can pass through a skipping consumer; (b) the close-time probe is an unbounded blocking read, mitigated by a stated contract on the supplied stream rather than a timeout, so a delegate that parks after `length` bytes deadlocks in `close()` instead of failing. ⚠️ Both need a real backend to express; neither is reachable from an in-tree fake | FR-8 | done |
| M1.1e | ⚠️ **Blocks M1.2.** Three conformance gaps a FILESYSTEM backend passes while being wrong, each verified surviving: (a) **shrinking overwrite** — both overwrite cases use a new body no shorter than the old, so a backend opening the final path without `O_TRUNC` passes and then serves `hi23456789`; (b) **destroy-then-fail** — the lying-body case asserts only that nothing was CREATED, so a backend that streams into the final path and checks length at EOF has already destroyed the previous good object; (c) **empty listing** — no case lists zero matches, and `Files.list` of a non-existent prefix directory throws `NoSuchFileException`, so recovery over a fresh bucket dies at startup. Also: the key-limit probe is all-ASCII, so counting chars instead of UTF-8 bytes survives, and `NAME_MAX` is bytes | FR-8 | done |
| M1.1f | Conformance leftovers, none blocking: a zero-length object never round-trips; `minPartSize` and `batchDelete` are returned by production and read by no assertion; and `CyclicBarrier.await()` in the concurrency case has no timeout, so a backend that stalls hangs the suite instead of failing it | FR-8 | todo |
| M1.2 | `LocalFsBinStore`, `putIfAbsent` via `O_CREAT`/`O_EXCL` | FR-8 | done |
| M1.3 | `CountingBinStore` + the cost-assertion test fixture | FR-8, R9 | done |
| M1.3b | `GoverningBinStore`: ratio-to-expected, LIST ceiling, priority classes; writes never refused | FR-21 | todo |
| M1.4 | Segment v0 writer: preamble, directory, runs, footer; record framing carries `_id`/`_op_type`/`_version` ([ADR-0020](decisions/0020-record-envelope-and-mapper.md)) | FR-2 | done |
| M1.5 | Segment v0 reader + round-trip + golden file | FR-2 | done |
| M1.6 | **In-process `Ingest` API** + config; no HTTP on the classpath | FR-1 | done |
| M1.6b | **`DefaultIngest`** — the production composition behind the `Ingest` seam: accumulator → segment PUT → commit → publish, blocking until durable. ⚠️ Raised because **nothing in `*/src/main` composed the parts**: M1.6 shipped the interface, M1.8–M1.11 shipped `Accumulator`, `SegmentPublisher`, `CommitLog` and `SubscriptionHub`, and every end-to-end test wired them BY HAND. So there was no production `Ingest` for the HTTP adapter to delegate to, and M1's completion condition — a document written **as `_bulk`** travelling the full path — was not provable. Found by two reviewers independently noting no `implements Ingest` exists outside a test fake | FR-1 | done |
| M1.6c | Durable ordinal registry behind `StreamResolver`: resolve an index NAME to its stream id, and survive a restart. ⚠️ M1 supplies the mapping from the caller, which is why `StreamResolver` exists as a seam at all — the producer writes `POST /logs/_bulk` while the plugin derives its stream from the OpenSearch index UUID, and something must join them. ⚠️ Raised because the `// SKELETON:` marker on that seam named no task, and the M1 SPEC requires a marker the milestone review can actually check | FR-1 | todo |
| M1.6d | A dropped inline push must degrade to LAG, not to record loss. ⚠️ ADR-0004 says a lagging consumer falls behind and recovers from the log — **nothing in M1 implements that**: `DefaultIngest` drops a push past its byte budget and `ConsumerClient` drops on a full queue, and neither end detects an offset gap or reads the commit log. So a drop is currently silent record loss for that subscriber. M1.6b counts drops (`droppedPushes()`) so the loss is at least visible; this row closes it | FR-5 | todo |
| M1.7 | HTTP adapter: Helidon 4.3.0, streaming `_bulk` parse, delegates to `Ingest`. `POST /{index}/_bulk?partition=N` → 202 once durable. ⚠️ The partition is an explicit query parameter with **no default** — nothing in M1 can derive it (aliases and `os_routing` are ADR-0015/M6) and a default of 0 would funnel every producer that forgot it into one partition behind a 202. ⚠️ Ships with a **fixed principal**, not per-request authentication (M1.7c) — SPEC § Scope puts authentication out of M1. ⚠️ Does NOT stream end-to-end: the handler materialises the body, so the body is capped at 32 MiB **and 200,000 records** with a 413 — a byte cap alone does not bound retained heap, since the smallest legal record is 24 bytes — and criterion 8 stays open (M1.7b) | FR-1 | done |
| M1.7b | Let the ingest seam accept a STREAM of records so the HTTP adapter stops materialising the body. `Ingest.append` now takes a `RecordSource` (`void forEachRecord(Consumer<SegmentRecord> sink) throws IOException`) instead of a `List` — a push-shaped seam, the same idiom `BulkParser.parse` already uses, so a caller with a `List` still satisfies it with `records::forEach` (all existing call sites updated that way). `BulkService` appends in bounded CHUNKS of `APPEND_CHUNK_RECORDS` (1,000) as it parses — never one `List<SegmentRecord>` for the whole request, and never one `Ingest.append` call for the whole request either. ⚠️ **The second half was found by review, round 1, not designed up front**: a first draft pushed `BulkParser.parse` straight into ONE `Ingest.append` call. `DefaultIngest.append` holds the pod's single accumulator lock for as long as its `RecordSource` takes to run, which is correct for an in-memory `List`'s `forEach` but was, in that draft, the whole REQUEST BODY's socket read — serializing every OTHER producer on the pod behind one slow client, exactly the "many producers share one segment, concurrently" property `DefaultIngest`'s own javadoc describes. Chunking bounds the lock to "add 1,000 already-parsed records", independent of network speed or body size. ⚠️ **Two more real bugs found and fixed while building this**: (1) `bufferedPerStream` was updated once after a chunk was fully consumed rather than per record, so a chunk that fails partway (safe by design — every record with an external `_version`, ADR-0020, makes a retry of an already-applied prefix a rejected stale version rather than a duplicate; a missing version is not covered) left the per-stream buffered count stale, corrupting the NEXT append's offset slice — `StreamingAppendTest#recordsAlreadyHandedToTheAccumulatorSurviveASourceThatFailsPartway` pins this, red-recorded by reverting the per-record fix; (2) the TRAILING partial chunk (fewer than 1,000 records at end of body) was appended OUTSIDE the try/catch that maps a store failure to 503, so it escaped as a bare RuntimeException and surfaced as a 500 — found by the pre-existing `BulkEndpointTest#theTwoOhTwoIsSentOnlyAfterAppendSucceeds` failing once chunking was added, not by a new test. `BulkServiceTest#bulkServiceNeverBuffersTheWholeBodyBeforeAppending` proves the first record reaches `Ingest.append` after only a small prefix of a 4+ MiB body is read (mirrors `BulkParserTest#bulkBodyIsNeverFullyBuffered`'s own pattern one layer up), red-recorded by reverting to an internal re-materializing mutant, and re-verified against the final chunked design (disabling chunking via a huge chunk size) still kills it. Authorization is now checked BEFORE the body is read: an unauthorized caller's body, however large, is never opened. `MAX_BODY_BYTES`/`MAX_RECORDS` are left at their pre-existing caps: raising them to criterion 8's 200 MB is T12/M1.18's job, which this change makes possible rather than attempting itself | NFR-1 | done |
| M1.7c | Per-request authentication on the `_bulk` endpoint: resolve a `Credential` to a `Principal` via `CredentialSource`. ⚠️ M1.7 ships a FIXED principal — SPEC § Scope puts authentication out of M1 deliberately, and this row exists so the deferral is scheduled rather than merely mentioned. Raised because M1.7 first cited **M1.8** for it, which is the accumulator task and already done: the sentence read as a plan and scheduled nothing | FR-1 | todo |
| M1.7d | A `BulkParseException` names the line it was on. ⚠️ On a 10,000-action body the shape alone does not locate the error, and the javadoc claimed a line number it never carried | FR-1 | todo |

Committed with `SKIP=check-tdd`, the same visible-skip discipline CI already uses for `check-reviewed` — recorded here rather than silently worked around | — | done |

Fixed with reference counting (`release`); the T4 test failed before the fix and passes after, confirmed by reverting the fix and re-running it. Two new T1 tests on `NodeSubscriptions` pin both directions. ⚠️ Does NOT re-prove the exact duplicate bound M1.17 already proves at T2 -- OpenSearch's own engine prefers `batch_start` over `pointer.init.reset` whenever it's present, so a real restart cannot distinguish "resumed from persisted pointer" from "reset to earliest" by document count alone; only a controlled T2 fake can pin that arithmetic | NFR-11 | done |

## M2 — segment format

Decomposed in [milestones/M2/SPEC.md](milestones/M2/SPEC.md), decided by
[ADR-0003](decisions/0003-adaptive-tagged-membership-filter-in-the-object-key.md)
and [ADR-0008](decisions/0008-cas-primitives-write-once-log-cas-lease.md). The
M1 rows above are NOT all done either (M1.6c/d, M1.7c-h, M1.10b, M1.11b,
M1.15e, M1.16c, M1.19/M1.19b, M1.20/b/c, M1.1d/f, M1.3b, M1.22, M1.24/25 remain
`todo`), but none of them block M2: M1's own completion condition (all 10
acceptance criteria) already holds, per
[milestones/M1/VERIFIED.md](milestones/M1/VERIFIED.md).

| ID | Task | Serves | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| M2.0 | `BinStore.putIfMatch(String key, Body body, Version expected)` on the SPI, implemented on `MemoryBinStore` and `LocalFsBinStore`, with conformance cases per ADR-0008: a version mismatch returns an empty `Optional`, never throws; a matching version succeeds and advances the version; an absent key with a non-null expected version fails distinctly from a version mismatch (throws, since there is no version to have moved from). `MemoryBinStore` CAS's via `ConcurrentSkipListMap.replace(key, oldEntry, newEntry)`, the same reference-equality mechanic `putIfAbsent` already relies on; `LocalFsBinStore` has no native CAS, so it serializes per key with an in-JVM monitor -- sound because this backend is a dev/test fixture, not a distributed target (ADR-0008's MinIO addendum makes the same point about this class of backend). Mutation verified for both: replacing the atomic step with a naive read-then-write let the concurrency case's `exactly one writer may win` assertion fail, then restored. `CountingBinStore` and the ingest module's `StoreFakes` decorators updated to delegate the new method (non-negotiable 6: a lost match is still counted, same reasoning as a lost `putIfAbsent`). ⚠️ `BinStoreConformance.java` split into it and a new `ConditionalWriteConformance.java` (code-structure.md rule 1: the file passed 500 lines) -- `BinStoreConformance` now extends it, so both existing backend test classes keep compiling with their one `extends` unchanged | FR-8 | done |
| M2.1 | `Capabilities.conditionalWrites` reflects support for BOTH `putIfAbsent` and `putIfMatch`, checked at startup and failing loudly if either is missing (ADR-0008: "`Capabilities.conditionalWrites` requires both"). No production `main()` exists yet to call a check from (M1 built a library of components, not a wired service), so the check is `Capabilities.requireConditionalWrites()` -- a pure, no-I/O method any future startup code calls once -- rather than invented wiring. Both real backends proven to pass it via the conformance suite; `CapabilitiesTest` proves it fails loudly (and names ADR-0008) when the flag is false | FR-8 | done |
| M2.2 | `MultipartWriter multipart(String key)` on the SPI and both in-tree backends, conformance cases: parts below `Capabilities.minPartSize` refused where the backend can express that (checked at `complete()`, since only the highest-numbered part is exempt and which part is last is not knowable earlier), the assembled object reads back byte-identical to what was written part-by-part, parts assemble in PART-NUMBER order regardless of upload order, `close()` without `complete()` behaves like `abort()` (never an implicit publish), an aborted/never-completed upload never touches a pre-existing object at that key. `CountingBinStore` and the ingest module's `StoreFakes` decorators updated to delegate/count the new writer (`multipart()` itself plus each `uploadPart`/`complete`/`abort` call, and an implicit `close()`-as-abort exactly once, never double-counted against an explicit `complete()`/`abort()` that already ran). `MultipartConformance.java` added as a third conformance file (alongside `BinStoreConformance`/`ConditionalWriteConformance`), chained by inheritance so both backend test classes keep their one `extends`. Mutation verified: part-size-check bypass, append-instead-of-replace-on-reupload, and each of the three counting paths (create, abort, implicit-close) all caught by a dedicated test, then reverted | FR-8 | done |
| M2.3 | The index-ordinal registry (`<prefix>/ctl/registry/indices.json`): read, first-registration-wins CAS assignment via `putIfMatch`, client-side caching so a known index needs no repeated full read (ADR-0008: "~0/s, only on index creation"). Split per code-structure.md rule 4/7: `format.IndexRegistry` is pure encode/decode (hand-written JSON, no library dependency -- the shape is flat string-to-non-negative-int, needing neither nesting nor escaping), `ingest.IndexOrdinalRegistry` is the thing that touches `BinStore`, caching indefinitely and revalidating with one `stat()` per miss (a `get()` only if the version actually moved). A losing writer in a first-registration race adopts the winner's ordinal for the SAME index rather than retrying into a second one; concurrent registration of DIFFERENT indices converges on distinct ordinals. Mutation verified: the adopt-the-winner check, the zero-request cached-lookup path, and the ordinal-assignment arithmetic, each caught by a dedicated test, then reverted | FR-8 | done |
| M2.4 | The adaptive filter codec: `A`/`N`/`Z`/`R`/`B` encode and decode (ADR-0003), one golden file per tag. This is the WIRE FORMAT only -- Kirsch-Mitzenmacher hashing and no-false-negative/FPR properties are M2.5's job. `Z`/`R` answer `mightContain` directly from their own exact/RLE bitmap (no hashing needed, since the bitmap IS the membership set); `Bloom` exposes raw `k`+bits+`m` but no hash-based query yet. `N` deliberately carries no membership predicate at the type level -- a caller must read the header (ADR-0003: `N` and an unrecognised tag both mean "must read", never "no match"); reader-side wiring of that policy is M2.7. `m` for `B` is derived from the payload's byte length (always padded to a byte boundary at encode time, not `BitSet.toByteArray()`'s own trimmed length, which would silently shrink `m` whenever the top byte is all zero). `k` is a single decimal digit (1-9): the grammar has no delimiter between it and the base64url payload. Mutation verified: every validation guard, every decode refusal path, and every tag's round-trip (including the two edge cases -- an all-zero-top-byte Bloom losing its true `m`, and an RLE-encoded empty bitmap), each caught by a dedicated test, then reverted | NFR-12 | done |
| M2.5 | The Bloom filter (`B`): Kirsch-Mitzenmacher construction from one 128-bit hash, a no-false-negative property test across randomised index populations, and a measured-FPR test at n=100/500/1,000 against the analytic prediction (research doc 30-design-space/02 §3). Adds `Bloom.of(ordinals, m, k)` and `Bloom.mightContain(ordinal)` to M2.4's wire format: `h_i = h1 + i·h2 (mod m)`, one hash per ordinal standing in for `k` independent ones. ⚠️ Deviates from research doc 02 §7's named xxh3-128/murmur3-128 to SHA-256 (already used elsewhere in this codebase, e.g. `LocalFsBinStore`'s key hashing) -- those are named for hot-path throughput, irrelevant at once-per-flush construction cost, and this choice is still revisable: M2.6, which wires the filter into `SegmentKey` and therefore into segments actually persisted, has not landed. Recorded as [ADR-0024](decisions/0024-bloom-hash-is-sha-256-not-xxh3-murmur3.md), with a revision banner on the research doc. FPR measured at the doc's own 10% target (4.79 bits/item, k=3) across all three n values, all within 0.4 percentage points of the analytic prediction in an isolated run; the 3-point tolerance asserted is not tuned to that run, so it stays generous against sampling noise while still catching a systematic (hashing/indexing) bug. Mutation verified: an extra, never-set probe position breaks the no-false-negative property without affecting FPR; halving the effective table width breaks all three FPR tests without affecting no-false-negative -- confirming the two properties are independently constrained, then reverted. Round-1 review found `Bloom.of`'s round-up-to-a-byte-boundary rounding untested on an unaligned `m` (every test pre-rounded it) and a negative `k` throwing the wrong exception type (allocating `new int[k]` before the canonical constructor's own validation ran); fixed with a dedicated test for the former and validating `k` at the top of `of()` for the latter. test-reviewer round 1 found the FPR tolerance (0.03) too loose to catch a dropped-probe bug (one fewer effective `k`, measuring 0.0107-0.0217 off analytic, comfortably under 0.03) and Bloom missing the negative-ordinal guard its sibling variants (`ExactBitmap`/`RunLength`) already have; fixed by tightening the tolerance to 0.008 (roughly 4 standard errors above the correct-code noise floor at 20,000 trials, comfortably below the dropped-probe deviation) and adding the guard with a dedicated test, both mutation verified | NFR-12 | done |
| M2.6 | Fix `SegmentKey.headerLenOf()`'s parsing hazard FIRST, in the SAME commit as wiring the filter into the writer path: `lastIndexOf("-h")` is not safe once the filter component can legally contain the substring `-h` (base64url's alphabet includes `-`), proven by an adversarial fixture whose filter payload contains that exact substring -- THEN compute candidates (cheapest-first: `A`, `Z`, `R`, `B`) from the segment's distinct-index set via the ordinal registry, emit the shortest that fits, degrade to `N`. ⚠️ Merged deliberately: round-1 spec review (M1.26) found that landing the writer change before the parsing fix, as two separate commits, ships a real header-length corruption for any produced key whose filter payload happens to contain `-h`, for as long as the gap between the commits lasts -- and the fix has no dependency on the writer change, so there was no reason to accept that window. Fixed `headerLenOf` by anchoring on the 16-hex-digit `sequence` field via regex (`-[0-9a-f]{16}-h(\d+)-`), not a bounds-adjusted `lastIndexOf`, since a naive fix still risks a spurious earlier match. `SegmentKey` gains a 6th field (`filterEncoded`) with the 5-arg constructor kept as a legacy overload defaulting to `N`, so every pre-existing call site compiles unchanged. New `format.FilterCandidates` (pure) picks the shortest fit; `IndexOrdinalRegistry.registeredCount()` answers the `A` candidate's "how many total" question from the local cache with ZERO extra requests -- deliberately not revalidated, since a stale-low count can only make `A` wrong in the SAFE direction (a false positive, which a Bloom filter already tolerates by design, never a false negative). `SegmentPublisher` reads the segment's own directory once (reused for both the filter and the existing record counts, not parsed twice), resolves each distinct index's ordinal, and embeds the computed filter. Real extra cost: zero once every index in a flush is already known to this pod; a genuinely NEW index costs one CAS registration (ADR-0008: "~0/s, only on index creation") -- several pre-existing `DefaultIngestTest`/`SegmentPublisherTest` request-count assertions updated to reflect this, each with the exact new count and why. Verified against a real OpenSearch node (`clusterTest`), not just unit tests. Mutation verified: the parsing-hazard fix, the constructor's filter-validation guard, the legacy-constructor default, the filter's embedding into `key()`, every branch of `FilterCandidates`' cheapest-first order (including the pathological all-fragmented-ordinals case that degrades to `N`), `registeredCount()`'s zero-request cache read, and `SegmentPublisher`'s actual wiring -- each caught by a dedicated test, then reverted. Round-1 review found the regex-anchoring fix incomplete: it assumed "the leftmost match is always the real one," which is false when `podShortId` (constrained at the time only by `isBlank()`) can itself contain a `-h<digits>-` run, e.g. `podShortId = "pod-0123456789abcdef-h5"` made `headerLenOf` silently return 5 instead of the true 999 -- reproduced and confirmed via a standalone harness before fixing. Closed with two guards, not one: `SegmentKey`'s canonical constructor now refuses a `podShortId` containing `-` or `/` (verified via grep that no existing call site anywhere in the tree uses either character in a `podShortId`, so this is non-breaking), and `headerLenOf` additionally anchors its regex search to only the substring after the key's last `/` via `Matcher.region()`, which also closes a second, not-yet-reproduced route through `prefix` found analytically rather than by the reviewer. `SegmentPublisher`'s constructor gained the identical `podShortId` restriction for the same reason `isBlank()` was already checked there: fail at construction, not silently deferred to the first flush. Both guards mutation verified: reverted, observed a genuine red via `tdd-red.sh` on the exact reviewer reproduction and the `/`-variant, then restored. test-reviewer round 1 found two majors: the tail-anchoring guard's own protection against a marker-shaped `prefix` was real (verified correct in production) but never actually exercised by a test -- fixed with a dedicated adversarial-prefix test, mutation verified against dropping the tail anchor; and `aSegmentMissingARegisteredIndexGetsAnExactOrBloomFilterNotA` asserted only the chosen filter's TYPE, which cannot catch a wiring bug in `SegmentPublisher.chooseFilter`'s own ordinal resolution (e.g. a loop-variable bug silently resolving the same ordinal for every distinct index) -- fixed by decoding the filter and asserting `mightContain` against the ordinals an independent `IndexOrdinalRegistry` instance reads back for the same store and prefix, mutation verified against exactly that loop-variable bug | NFR-12 | done |
| M2.7 | Reader-side unknown-tag handling: `MembershipFilter.decodeOrMustRead(String)` -- distinct from the writer-side `decode`, which still fails loudly on a tag it does not know -- treats a tag byte outside `{A,N,Z,R,B}` (simulating a future writer's format this reader has not seen) identically to `N`: `None`, "must read the header," never "no match." A malformed PAYLOAD for a tag this class DOES recognise (e.g. `decode("Ax")`, `decode("B")`) still throws -- only the tag byte is forward-compatible, never real corruption of an understood format. Proven end-to-end (acceptance criterion 9's own "asserting the reader still finds the data," not merely a unit assertion on a returned enum): a real segment built via `SegmentWriter`/`SegmentReader` (independent of the ingest module), a fabricated key whose filter uses tag `Q`, a reader-decision helper that would skip a segment only on positive proof of absence -- and the record is found, because `None` exposes no `mightContain` at all, forcing the skip/read decision to be made explicitly rather than defaulted. No production reader-path caller exists yet to retrofit (the recovery/GC consumer is out of scope for M2, per the SPEC's own Design section); this is the policy method that consumer will call. Mutation verified: reverting the tag-based special-casing (falling straight through to `decode`) breaks the unknown-tag and the end-to-end fixture tests; broadening it to swallow ANY decode failure (not just an unrecognised tag) into `None` breaks the malformed-payload test -- each caught by a dedicated test, then reverted. test-reviewer round 1 found the new tests only ever exercised tags `Q`/`N`/`A`/`B`, never `Z` or `R` -- a mutation dropping either from the recognised-tag guard would silently turn a REAL exact/RLE filter into "must read" on every access, defeating that filter's whole cost point, and survived every test in the file. Fixed by exercising all five known tags in the malformed-payload test and adding a dedicated round-trip test proving each of A/Z/R/B decodes to its own real variant, never `None`; both mutation verified against dropping `Z` from the guard | NFR-12 | done |
| M2.8 | NFR-12 pathological-input test: new `format.PathologicalKeySizeTest`, at the project's 10,000-registered-index target scale (research doc 02 §0), measures the FULL emitted key's byte length against the 1024-byte provider cap -- not just the filter payload against `FilterCandidates`' own internal 900-byte budget, which is all any existing test checked. Reuses `FilterCandidatesTest`'s own already-proven candidate-selection scenarios for all five outcomes (`A`: every registered index present; `Z`: a handful of low ordinals; `R`: a few dense-with-gaps clusters; `B`: ~500 sparse indices, this project's expected default; `N`: the pathological every-other-ordinal fragmentation across 10,000 that makes every other tag too large to fit, degrading rather than overflowing), each embedded in a `SegmentKey` built with a deliberately generous (not merely typical) prefix, pod id and header length -- exactly the inputs that could silently erode the ~124 bytes research doc 02 §0 assumes are left over for everything but the filter. Measured, not assumed: 131 bytes for `A`/`N` (identical, since `N`'s payload is the same one character as `A`'s -- the adversarial part of the pathological case is entirely in candidate SELECTION, not the emitted key's size), 134 for `Z`, 138 for `R`, 532 for `B` (the largest of the five, still under half the 1024 cap), each pinned by an exact assertion so a future widening of the fixed key structure shows up as a changed number rather than silently eroding headroom until it doesn't. Mutation verified: an extra literal character appended to `SegmentKey.key()`'s format string breaks all five pinned lengths, then reverted. test-reviewer round 1 found a BLOCKING, pre-existing production defect this measurement task exists to catch: none of the five fixtures approach `FilterCandidates`' 900-byte internal budget, so the suite never proved the region where a filter judged to "fit" 900 bytes still combines with this fixture's own ~130-byte fixed overhead to exceed the 1024-byte cap -- reproduced against the real, unmutated code at a plain ~1,120-sparse-index segment (nowhere near the 10,000-index pathological scale) where a 897-byte Bloom filter correctly "fit" 900 but `SegmentKey.key()` threw `IllegalStateException`, since this fixture's real fixed overhead leaves only 894 bytes, not 900. Fixed at the design level, not by re-tuning a constant: `FilterCandidates.chooseShortestFitting` now takes the filter budget as a required parameter instead of an internal 900-byte constant; new `SegmentKey.filterBudgetBytes(...)` computes the REAL number from the same fields (prefix/timestamp/podShortId/sequence/headerLen) that will actually go into the key, by rendering the identical template `key()` uses with an empty filter slot -- a single source of truth, not a second hand-derived formula that could itself drift. `SegmentPublisher.chooseFilter` now computes this real budget per flush (moving the one `sequence.getAndIncrement()` call earlier so the SAME sequence number is used for both the budget computation and the final key, never two independent draws). Proven with a new `everyPopulationFromOneToTenThousandIndicesNeverOverflowsTheKeyBudget` test walking every population from 1 to 10,000 sparse ordinals and asserting the key never exceeds 1024 bytes (measured maximum: exactly 1024, at n=1,116 -- the budget is tight, not padded with unexamined slack), plus a direct reproduction of the exact n=1,120 case that used to throw, now correctly degrading to `N`. All `FilterCandidatesTest` call sites updated to pass an explicit budget (900, a test fixture's own illustrative choice, no longer a hidden production default). Verified against a real OpenSearch node (`clusterTest`) after the `SegmentPublisher` change. Mutation verified: each of `FilterCandidates`' branches (the `A` short-circuit, the empty-set short-circuit, `fits()` forced true/false) caught by a dedicated test; the pre-fix 900-byte-ignoring behavior caught by the two new budget-correctness tests -- each reverted after observing red | NFR-12 | done |
| M2.9 | Closes a decomposition gap found while writing M2's `VERIFIED.md`: acceptance criterion 10 ("every emitted key round-trips through `MemoryBinStore` and `LocalFsBinStore` unchanged") and its own T0/T1 test-plan row ("a backend-specific character escaping bug, base64url's `-`/`_` are unusual in some key-naming conventions") had no task or test anywhere in M2.0-M2.8 -- `BinStoreConformance`'s own round-trip tests use generic keys like `"p/a"`, never a real filter payload's base64url characters, and `plugin`'s cluster tests (`SearchableIT`/`ShardFanOutIT`) build their `SegmentKey` via the legacy 5-arg constructor, which always carries the literal `N`, never a real payload. New `ingest.SegmentKeyBackendRoundTripTest`: a key whose filter is a REAL Bloom filter (reusing `PathologicalKeySizeTest`'s own 500-sparse-index fixture, confirmed by running it to genuinely contain both `-` and `_`, not merely one or neither) is put then stat/get through both backends, byte-identical and findable at the exact key. Mutation verified: each backend's `put` writing under a `-`-to-`+`-mangled key while `get`/`stat` still look under the real one (simulating exactly the escaping bug named in the SPEC) breaks its own test, then reverted | NFR-12 | done |
| M2.10 | `milestone-review` harvest: `MemoryBinStore.putIfMatch`'s version-match check (M2.0) already correctly uses `Version.equals()` (a record, value equality by construction), not `==` -- but no test constructs a FRESH `Version` instance carrying the same token as the current one, so a regression to reference equality would pass every existing test silently (`LocalFsBinStore` is only accidentally immune, since `readMeta` always reconstructs a new instance from disk). Not a live defect; a cheap regression test worth adding | FR-8 | todo |
| M2.11 | `milestone-review` harvest: `IndexOrdinalRegistry.ordinalFor()` (M2.3) holds its lock across the WHOLE read-modify-CAS-retry cycle including the network calls, serializing a burst of many distinct new-index registrations on one shared instance to one in flight at a time. Accepted at M2.3 as a latency tradeoff, not a request-rate defect (ADR-0008 frames bursty registration as rare) -- revisit if a later milestone's throughput makes concurrent new-index bursts common | FR-8 | todo |
| M2.12 | `milestone-review` harvest: `MembershipFilter.Bloom.of()` (M2.5) doesn't validate `m` before use (a non-positive `m` throws a raw `ArithmeticException` instead of `Bloom`'s own canonical-constructor message) and doesn't reject a negative ordinal in its input collection, unlike `mightContain()`'s own guard -- a write/read asymmetry accepted at M2.5 as low-risk since `m`/ordinals were not yet caller-supplied. `FilterCandidates` (landed M2.6) is now `Bloom.of`'s one real caller and always passes a positive `m` and non-negative ordinals, so still unreached in practice, but the risk condition this was accepted against has changed | NFR-12 | todo |
| M2.13 | `milestone-review` harvest: `FilterCandidates` and `BloomConstructionTest` (M2.6) each independently declare the same Bloom sizing constants (4.79 bits/item, `k`=3) with no shared source of truth -- a future FPR-target retune could update one and silently miss the other, so `FilterCandidates` keeps sizing to the old target while the property test keeps passing against the new one | NFR-12 | todo |
| M2.14 | `milestone-review` harvest: `SegmentKey`'s canonical constructor (M2.6) only catches `IOException` when validating `filterEncoded`; `MembershipFilter.RunLength.decode` can throw an unchecked `IndexOutOfBoundsException` on a pathological uvarint run (`pos+run` overflowing `int`, then `BitSet.set(from, negative-to)`). Unreachable via any writer call site today (a writer only ever validates an encoding it just built), but will become reachable once a reader constructs `SegmentKey` from a previously-persisted, untrusted key string -- most likely the recovery/GC-path consumer, not yet built | NFR-12 | todo |
| M2.15 | `milestone-review` harvest: no `clusterTest` exercises a segment carrying a REAL, non-`N` membership filter tag through the plugin's own read path against a running OpenSearch node. `SearchableIT`/`ShardFanOutIT` build their `SegmentKey` via the legacy 5-arg constructor (always `N`); M2's own Design section notes a subscribed consumer never looks at the filter at all. Real-node coverage of a non-`N` tag is backend-round-trip only (M2.9), not plugin-read-path -- revisit once a recovery/GC-path consumer that actually reads the filter exists | NFR-12 | todo |
| M2.16 | `milestone-review` harvest: round-1 review of this very harvest (M2.10) found the original wording of this row wrong -- `Files.createTempDirectory` in every `LocalFsBinStore`-backed test (`LocalFsBinStoreTest`, `SearchableIT`, `ShardFanOutIT`, `RestartResumeIT`, `OffsetMonotonicityIT`, `DeleteAndVersionIT`, `MemoryFlatUnderTenXBodySizeTest`, M2.9's own `SegmentKeyBackendRoundTripTest`) does NOT violate testing.md rule 17: `binjava.java-conventions.gradle.kts` already pins `java.io.tmpdir` to `build/tmp` for every product module's test task (M0.33's own row already says so), verified by actually running `LocalFsBinStoreTest` and confirming its directories land under `build/tmp`, not the OS temp dir. The real, much smaller residual issue: `deleteOnExit()` silently no-ops on a NON-EMPTY directory (confirmed: 26+ leftover directories from one test run), so these dirs accumulate under `build/tmp` across runs rather than the OS temp dir filling up -- a cleanup nit, not a rule-17 violation. Worth a shared JUnit extension or `@TempDir` in place of the hand-rolled `Files.createTempDirectory`/`deleteOnExit()` pattern, not a sweep against a rule that already holds | — | todo |
| M2.17 | M3 spec written and reviewed, matching M1.26's own precedent ("M2 spec written and reviewed" as an M1-milestone task). `milestones/M3/SPEC.md`: the adaptive flush interval (ADR-0016 §2's `fillRatio` mechanism, carried forward by ADR-0017 with the writer-count-scaling half withdrawn), NFR-6 re-proven under it, and one reserved `i8 lane` byte in the on-disk directory entry (no logic wired -- M10's job). Scope narrowed against a real fork found while drafting: `roadmap.md`'s own "Deferred into a later milestone" table separately promises "the `i8 lane` field lands in the wire format at M3", which on investigation ties (per ADR-0014/0015) to a materially larger routing-resolution feature (murmur3 partition hashing, the plugin pushing index metadata over its subscription channel) than the roadmap's own M3 completion condition (interval + memory) names -- confirmed with the person running this session which half to build; the byte reservation lands, ADR-0014's own lane-scheduling logic stays deferred to M10 and ADR-0015's routing resolution stays where the roadmap's own milestone table already puts it, M6 (round-1 spec review caught this row's first draft conflating the two into "M10") | — | done |

Four other minors surfaced during this harvest were NOT turned into rows,
explicitly dropped rather than silently: M2.1's `CapabilitiesTest` message
assertion not verifying the ADR-0008 citation specifically was corrected in
that commit's own body, not a live code gap; M2.2's "no `.harness/review`
verdict for an earlier round" is a description of this session's own
recording practice, not a defect; M2.6's own round-2 minor -- its test
helpers' unanchored `lastIndexOf('-')` extraction, the same pattern
production code was hardened against -- was already accepted there as
"benign only because current fixtures don't produce a payload ending in
`-`", not re-verified by mutation testing (round-1 review of this harvest,
M2.10, found an earlier draft of this paragraph misattributed it to M2.7/
M2.8 and conflated its reasoning with theirs); and M2.8's "sequence read
once" fix plus `filterBudgetBytes`'s lack of a standalone unit test were
each independently confirmed low-risk-and-adequately-covered by both
reviewers' own mutation testing at THAT time.

## M3 — ingest path

Decomposed in [milestones/M3/SPEC.md](milestones/M3/SPEC.md), decided by
[ADR-0017](decisions/0017-every-pod-writes.md) carrying forward
[ADR-0016](decisions/0016-designated-writer-per-az.md) §2. Scope is the
adaptive flush interval, NFR-6 re-proven under it, and one reserved `i8
lane` byte in the on-disk directory entry (no scheduling logic wired --
that stays M10's job; routing resolution stays M6's, per roadmap.md's own
table). M2's own rows above are all `done`; M1's still-`todo` rows (see the
note above M2) remain unblocking for the same reason they did not block M2.

| ID | Task | Serves | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| M3.0 | Reserve the `i8 lane` byte in the on-disk directory entry, per ADR-0025. `SegmentFormat.VERSION` bumps 0 -> 1 (not a silent width change: `SegmentReader` accepts BOTH versions, computing the entry width from the segment's own preamble via new `directoryEntryBytesFor(version)`, so a `VERSION_0` segment an earlier build already wrote still parses -- `GoldenSegmentTest`'s existing `golden/segment-v0.bin` is untouched, not regenerated). `DIRECTORY_ENTRY_BYTES` widens 48 -> 49; `SegmentWriter` always encodes the new byte as `0` (no caller has a real lane value -- `RunKey` gains no lane component); `SegmentReader` decodes it into a new `RunEntry.lane()` field, synthesising `0` for a `VERSION_0` entry that has no such byte at all. A NEW golden fixture, `golden/segment-v1.bin` (`GoldenSegmentV1Test`), proves the new shape round-trips, matching the `wire-format-change` skill's own checklist item "golden-file tests for the old AND the new shape" -- not the old one rewritten to look like the new one. Every existing hardcoded byte-offset assertion in `SegmentWriterTest`/`SegmentPublisherTest` that assumed the old 48-byte width updated to read the width from `SegmentFormat.DIRECTORY_ENTRY_BYTES` instead of a literal, so a THIRD format change cannot silently drift these tests again. Verified against a real OpenSearch node (`clusterTest`). Mutation verified: `directoryEntryBytesFor` mapping `VERSION` to the wrong (v0) width breaks all three golden-v1-parsing tests; its `default` case silently accepting an unrecognised version instead of refusing breaks both the unknown-version-refusal test and the direct unit test on the method itself -- each reverted after observing red. Round 1 review found: two doc typos ("47 bytes narrower" instead of 1; `SegmentWriter`/`SegmentReader`'s class summaries still said "v0 segment" after this task made the writer always emit v1), the format's own defining research doc (`docs/research/30-design-space/01-object-layout-and-format.md`, cited by ADR-0025) left stale with no mention of the new shape, and ADR-0025's own alternatives section claiming lane is "a per-run property, not per-record" -- directly contradicting ADR-0014's explicit "lane is per record, it must be" -- all fixed. test-reviewer round 1 found two MAJORs: no test distinguished the lane byte's own offset from the always-zero `codecAndFlags` field next to it (a reader aliased to the wrong offset passed every test, since both fields were 0 either way), and no test exercised a `VERSION_0` segment's synthesised-`0` lane path at all (only v1 segments were ever checked). Fixed with two new tests: one patches a real segment's `codecAndFlags` to a non-zero sentinel (recomputing the header CRC) and confirms `lane()` still reads 0 from ITS OWN offset, not the sentinel; the other opens the existing v0 golden fixture and asserts `lane()` synthesises to 0 for every entry -- both mutation verified against the exact bugs named. Round 2 review found the v0-synthesis test didn't actually kill the mutation it was written for: `golden/segment-v0.bin`'s own bytes happen to be 0x00 at every entry's "would-be lane" offset (coincidence of a small fixture, not a synthesised value), so dropping the reader's version guard entirely still passed it. Fixed with a hand-built synthetic v0 segment (`SegmentWriter` can no longer emit v0 at all) whose second run's UUID has a deliberately non-zero leading byte landing exactly at the first entry's own offset, mutation verified against the exact dropped-guard bug. test-reviewer round 1 found two more: the codecAndFlags sentinel (`0x2A000000`) made only the FIRST of its four bytes non-zero, so an off-by-one alias (reading codecAndFlags' LAST byte, adjacent to the true lane offset, instead of the first) survived -- fixed with all four bytes non-zero (`0x2A2A2A2A`), mutation verified against exactly that off-by-one; and `directoryEntryBytesFor`'s two accepted-version cases were only exercised indirectly (via a headerLen-disagreement exception several tests away), never asserted directly on the method itself -- fixed with a dedicated mapping test, mutation verified against the two cases swapped | FR-18 (byte reservation only) | done |
| M3.1 | `IngestConfig` gains `intervalCeiling`, `fillRatioLowThreshold`/`fillRatioHighThreshold` (defaults 0.4/0.9), and `intervalLengthenDelay`/`intervalShortenDelay` (`T_lengthen`/`T_shorten`, defaults 2 min / zero), with a `flushInterval` -> `intervalFloor` rename reflecting its new role (M1's fixed 250ms is now the FLOOR of a range, not the only value) and full validation (ceiling >= floor, thresholds in range and correctly ordered, delays non-negative). Legacy 3-/4-arg constructor overloads default the new fields to M3's chosen operating point, so ~20 existing call sites keep compiling unchanged -- the two that used a 1-hour "NEVER" duration to disable the time trigger needed a new `IngestTestSupport.pinnedIntervalConfig` helper (pins ceiling to whatever floor is passed, since the new 5s default ceiling would otherwise reject a 1-hour floor). Pure config-shape change: `Accumulator`/`DefaultIngest` still only ever read `intervalFloor()`, no adaptive behavior wired yet (M3.2/M3.3's job). Mutation verified: reverted each of the 7 new validation branches (ceiling>=floor in both directions plus the equal boundary, both threshold range checks, the low<high ordering check, both delay non-negativity checks plus the zero boundary), observed a genuine red via `tdd-red.sh`, then restored. Round-1 test-review found the `fillRatioHighThreshold > 0.0` half of its own guard was masked by a separate check's message happening to contain the same substring -- fixed with an exact-message assertion instead of a substring match, re-verified against the exact masking scenario | NFR-1 | done |
| M3.2 | `Accumulator.lastFillRatio()`: `fillRatio = actualSegmentBytes ÷ targetSegmentSize` from the most recent `drain()`, `0.0` before any segment has ever been drained. Measured against the REAL serialised segment length (`segment.length`), not `bufferedBytes()`'s pre-flush framing estimate -- the preamble/directory/footer are real bytes the estimate never counts, so fillRatio can genuinely exceed 1.0 right at the size-trigger boundary, proven by a dedicated test rather than assumed. No interval adjustment yet -- split out for reviewability, per M3 SPEC's own task sketch; `Accumulator`'s flush-trigger behavior is unchanged. Mutation verified: a fixed non-zero initial value, computing the ratio from the estimate instead of the real bytes, and a "stuck at the first drain" bug each caught by a dedicated test, then reverted. test-reviewer round 1 found no test proved `lastFillRatio()` survives an empty drain (returns `Optional.empty()`) AFTER a real measurement was already recorded -- every existing test's first drain followed an empty accumulator, never a second drain call after a genuine one. Fixed with a dedicated test, mutation verified against resetting the value on the empty-drain path | NFR-1 | done |
| M3.3 | `Accumulator.adaptInterval(nowMillis)`, called from `drain()` right after `lastFillRatio` is measured: `lastFillRatio` sustained at or below `fillRatioLowThreshold` (0.4) for the full `intervalLengthenDelay` (2 min) lengthens `currentInterval` directly to `intervalCeiling`; sustained at or above `fillRatioHighThreshold` (0.9) for `intervalShortenDelay` (0, i.e. the very next flush) shortens it directly to `intervalFloor` -- a binary jump per the M3 SPEC's Design section, never an intermediate ramp. Per-pod, no coordination (ADR-0017 point 2): every accumulator starts at the floor and earns lengthening only from its OWN measured fillRatio. Two wall-clock-free streak timestamps (`lowStreakStartMillis`/`highStreakStartMillis`, via the injected `Clock`, matching `ConsumerClient`'s M1.16c precedent) track how long each band has been continuously true; a `fillRatio` strictly between the two thresholds resets BOTH streaks, so neither a stale low-streak nor a stale high-streak can fire once the other band -- or the middle -- has been observed since. New `currentInterval()` accessor; `isFlushDue()` now compares elapsed time against this instance's own adapted interval, not `intervalFloor()` directly. Mutation verified: wrong starting interval, lengthening without waiting out the sustained delay, never lengthening at all, never shortening at all, the middle band failing to reset a streak, and specifically the direction-swap bug the SPEC's Design section itself warns ADR-0016 §2b's own (withdrawn, writer-count) table could induce if misread -- each reverted after observing a genuine red via `tdd-red.sh`. Two self-caught test-design bugs before review: an overly convoluted 3-instance version of the shorten test collapsed to one instance; and the oscillation test's original "high" fixture (`"x".repeat(900_000)` against a 1 MiB target) measured only ≈0.858 fillRatio by direct arithmetic -- never actually reaching the 0.9 threshold it was meant to exercise -- fixed to `"x".repeat(1_000_000)` (≈0.9538) with explicit per-iteration band assertions. Round-1 review: both reviewers passed with no blocking or major findings. test-reviewer recorded one MINOR, not fixed per review.md rule 11: no test drives `lastFillRatio` to exactly 0.4 or 0.9 (every fixture sits well clear of the boundary on both sides), so a boundary-off-by-one mutation (`>=`/`<=` swapped to `>`/`<` in `adaptInterval`) would survive undetected -- left for a future task rather than risked as a same-round fix | NFR-1 | done |
| M3.4 | New `IntervalPutRateTest` (T1, no Docker): a modeled 1 MiB/s stream through `Accumulator` + `SegmentPublisher` + `CountingBinStore` (`MemoryBinStore`, no `CommitLog` -- `SegmentPublisher`'s own "one flush is one PUT" is exactly the quantity ADR-0016's PUT/s table counts, and a commit-log append is a separate request that table never folds in), driven entirely by an advanced `TestClock`, never real sleep. Appends 131072 bytes (1 MiB / 8 ticks-per-second, dividing exactly) every 125ms tick, checking `isFlushDue()`/publishing after each. Asserts three things once the interval reaches its ceiling: it settles within 1s of the configured 2-minute `intervalLengthenDelay` (not merely "eventually"); it STAYS at the ceiling afterward (a ~0.625 fillRatio at 1 MiB/s over 5s is a middle-band reading, which must hold, not shorten); and the measured PUT rate over a subsequent 60s window (12 flushes at the 5s ceiling) is within ±20% of `1 ÷ intervalCeiling` = 0.2 PUT/s -- not the fixed-250ms rate's 4 PUT/s (acceptance criterion 4). A bounded give-up (4 simulated minutes, not a generous 10 -- `MemoryBinStore` never frees a PUT, and at the floor's 250ms cadence a "never lengthens" bug retaining ~256KiB segments for 10 minutes risked an actual `OutOfMemoryError` under `:ingest:test`'s 512m heap before the assertion ever fired, found by running the mutation below) fails the test with a clear message instead of hanging or OOMing. Mutation verified: swapped the two branches' target intervals in `Accumulator.adaptInterval` (low fillRatio sets the floor, high sets the ceiling) -- the interval never leaves the floor, and the test fails cleanly inside the 4-minute bound; reverted, confirmed green. No production code changed -- the adaptive logic already shipped in M3.3; this task is the modeled-traffic proof the SPEC's own T1 row calls for, since every `AccumulatorTest` test drives one flush at a time and cannot by itself prove the interval holds under a sustained stream | NFR-1 | done |
| M3.6 | ADR-0026 + M3 SPEC amendment: a lone producer's throughput scales INVERSELY with the flush interval, because `BulkService` appends in blocking 1000-record chunks and so advances exactly one chunk per flush. Measured on the real stack, not extrapolated: 2 MiB took 51.5s with the interval warmed to its 5s ceiling (10 flushes, 5.15s each, ~210 KB each) = ~40 KB/s, against M1.18's measured ~806 KB/s at the 250ms floor -- a 20.3x ratio matching the 20x interval ratio exactly. Two consequences: the interval does NOT self-correct for a lone producer (each ~210 KB flush is `fillRatio` ~0.026, deep in the low band, which is precisely what KEEPS it at the ceiling -- confirmed by 5.15s/flush holding for the whole measured run), and the single-producer shape M3.5 was specified around cannot reach the state acceptance criterion 5 names at all (a blocking producer never has more than one ~210 KB chunk outstanding, at the ceiling or the floor), so it would have passed while exercising nothing. Found by M3.5's first two attempts timing out at 8 and 20 minutes, both short of the 200 MB target; a 200 MB single-producer body at the ceiling would take ~86 minutes. AC5 and the T12 row amended to specify 24 CONCURRENT producers, landing `fillRatio` in the MIDDLE band (~0.65) -- the one regime that HOLDS the interval at its ceiling while the timer flushes a ~5.2 MiB accumulator, 23x what a lone producer ever holds. Round-1 review found two MAJORs, both fixed: (a) the first draft specified "enough producers to exceed the 8 MiB target", which is self-defeating -- that is `fillRatio` >= 1.0, the HIGH band, and with `intervalShortenDelay` = 0 the interval drops back to the floor on that very drain, so the state under test would survive exactly one flush; (b) the first draft justified accepting the lone-producer slowdown as affecting "only a pod with no meaningful traffic", which is false -- `fillRatio` <= 0.4 at the 250ms floor is any pod under ~13.4 MB/s, and the SPEC's own criterion-4 worked load (1 MiB/s per pod) settles at the ceiling BY DESIGN, so the ~44 KB/s cap is a per-connection property of normally-loaded pods; restated honestly and carried in roadmap.md's own "Deferred into a later milestone" table rather than waved away. Two minors also fixed while editing the same prose: the per-chunk figure is ~223 KB (1000 x ~228 B), not the ~210 KB first written (that was the measured run's 10-flush average, whose last append was a ~190-record tail), making steady-state ~44 KB/s and ~79 min rather than ~40 KB/s and ~86 min; and the `Research:` line cited an anchor that does not exist in the cost-model doc -- R1b is defined in `standards/cost.md`. Docs-only; no production or test code changed | NFR-1, NFR-6 | done |
| M3.5 | New `MemoryFlatAtIntervalCeilingTest`, NFR-6 re-proven under the adaptive interval per the AC5 wording ADR-0026/M3.6 amended: the interval warmed to its 5s ceiling and 24 CONCURRENT producers streaming 200 MB between them, under a real `-Xmx256m` via a new `memoryBoundCeilingTest` Gradle task (separate from M1.18's `memoryBoundTest` so the two tests' genuinely different real costs get their own budgets). 24 producers x ~223 KB is ~5.2 MiB against the 8 MiB target -- the MIDDLE band, the one regime that HOLDS the ceiling: the high band shortens straight back to the floor (`intervalShortenDelay` is zero) and the low band is where a lone producer already sits. ⚠️ The band is deliberately NOT asserted tightly (M3.6 round-2 review): the arithmetic counts BODY bytes while `fillRatio` is measured on the SERIALISED segment, so the true ratio is nearer 0.62 -- what the test pins is the OBSERVABLE consequence instead. Three assertions: a warm-up probe (one tiny body's 202 must now wait >2s, proving by observation rather than by trusting `adaptInterval` that the interval really lengthened), flush cadence measured PER 10-SECOND WINDOW during the load (<2.0 files/s), and then M1.18's own two memory bounds (peak sampled heap under 220 MiB; never above `max(128 MiB, 10x bytes written so far)`). ⚠️ The warm-up cannot be skipped: an `Accumulator` STARTS at the floor and the middle band leaves the interval UNTOUCHED, so concurrent producers alone would sit at ~0.65 forever and the run would be M1.18's 250ms regime under a different name -- a `WarpableClock` jumps the same clock `DefaultIngest` reads past `intervalLengthenDelay` between two tiny low-`fillRatio` flushes. Measured: 200.8s, within 5% of ADR-0026's own ~192s prediction. ⚠️ Round-1 test-review found a MAJOR and DEMONSTRATED it with a run rather than an argument: the cadence check was originally an AVERAGE over the whole load, which is blind to a mid-run shortening because the flush COUNT here is interval-INVARIANT (24 blocking producers contribute one chunk per flush whatever the interval, so ~152 files are written either way and only elapsed time differs) -- a mutation shortening after 13 drains put ~77% of the body through the 250ms regime and passed, green, in 66s. Fixed by sampling the store's file count per 1s sample and asserting every 10-second window, which fails that exact mutation at 4.33 files/s. ⚠️ The threshold is MEASURED, not derived: the naive 250ms/5s ratio predicts 16 files/s at the floor, but the floor here is producer-bound rather than timer-bound and really measures 4.33, against which the first-draft 4.0 had only 8% margin -- the clean path measures 182 windows, max 0.792 files/s, so 2.0 sits 2.5x above the real ceiling max and 2.2x below the real floor regime. ⚠️ Round-2 review (both reviewers independently, each having MEASURED it) found that this very fix introduced a new silent-green hole: moving `Files.walk` into the sampler races `LocalFsBinStore`'s own `put*.tmp` -> final rename, so it can throw `UncheckedIOException(NoSuchFileException)` roughly 1 run in 200-400, and a sampler dying there is a DAEMON -- `join()` returns at once and the cadence, peak and ratio checks all judge whatever prefix was collected and PASS. Hardened despite review.md rule 12's 2-round cap, stated rather than deferred: the walk is retried 3x, any escaping throwable is recorded into a `samplerFailures` list asserted empty, and a further assertion requires the LAST sample to land within 5s of the load's end, so a part-way death is observable rather than silent. Rule 11 would have allowed landing it, but round 1's version of this minor was about a sampler that COULD NOT throw -- it was this round's own change that made it able to. Round-1 review also fixed: the new Gradle task inherited none of the java-conventions `java.io.tmpdir` setting (it is a standalone `tasks.register<Test>`, not a JvmTestSuite target), leaking ~175 MB to the system temp directory per run against testing.md rule 17; and two warm-up `Thread.sleep(300)` calls whose stated justification was false (`append` blocks until durable, so the 202 already proves the drain ran) were removed. ⚠️ Committed with `SKIP=check-tdd`, stated plainly rather than worked around: `tdd_scan.py` maps this class to `:http:test`, which EXCLUDES it (it only runs under `memoryBoundCeilingTest`), so the script runs nothing and can mint no red record -- the same structural gap M1.18 has. Falsifiability was verified BY HAND under the correct task instead, and unlike M1.18's blind spot it is clean and mechanical: disabling `adaptInterval`'s lengthen branch makes the probe return in 256ms instead of >2000ms and the test fails in 3 seconds with a real JUnit `<failure>` element and a self-describing message, not a crash. Closing the gap properly is M0.52 | NFR-6 | done |
| M3.7 | M3's `VERIFIED.md`: one evidence line per acceptance criterion, each naming the test or command that demonstrated it, plus an explicit list of what did NOT run. Every figure re-measured for the record with the build cache and up-to-date checks both defeated: `./gradlew test --rerun-tasks --no-build-cache` across ALL modules (**415 tests, 0 failures** -- binstore-backends 86, binstore-spi 29, client 15, format 122, http 36, ingest 93, plugin 34), `:plugin:clusterTest` against a real OpenSearch node (**9 tests, 0 failures**, six ITs), and `:http:memoryBoundCeilingTest` (**tests=1 failures=0, 199.245s**). ⚠️ Three honesty defects in this record's own drafts, each caught and fixed rather than shipped. (1) The first two suite runs reported `BUILD SUCCESSFUL` in 1-2 seconds having executed NOTHING -- `--rerun` alone left tasks `FROM-CACHE`/`UP-TO-DATE` -- caught by checking task outcomes and result-XML timestamps rather than the exit code. (2) Round-1 review found a MAJOR: criterion 5 quoted `200.0s ... on the committed bytes` lifted from the M3.5 commit body, a run made BEFORE that commit and never repeated, with no results on disk, while the same file explicitly declared its sibling `memoryBoundTest` NOT RE-RUN -- the silence read as deliberate contrast. Re-run for real; the line now cites 199.245s with results on disk. (3) Review also found the suite covered only 4 of 8 modules while `client`'s `ConsumerClient` reads `RunEntry`/`SegmentReader` and is therefore one of the readers criterion 6 requires to pass at the new 49-byte width -- now all modules. Two further minors fixed: criterion 1 had restated its SPEC text as only the lengthening half (the gate enforces enumeration, not faithfulness, so a narrowed heading passes while covering half a criterion), and the did-not-run list said `the two strongest quality gates` when the absent set is five -- most consequentially the COST METER, since M3's completion condition is a request-rate reduction and no gate measures it. `check-milestone-verified.sh` green (7 criteria, all with evidence) | — | done |
